- **Doc-touch:** **No canonical docs are edited in this slice** — amendments are APPROVED here and APPLIED in Phase 1. Gate dispositions are recorded in the PR body, which is transient. What this slice does enumerate is the *target* list each gate will later touch, so that Phases 1, 3, and 4 inherit it: `diagnostic-system.md` (GATE-C/D/H routing), `collection-types.md` plus the spec (GATE-D), `evaluator.md §7.1` (the GATE-E Create capture), `precept-language-spec.md` §0.6/§0.7/Principle 11 (GATE-F/H), `fault-system.md` (GATE-C/H), and `philosophy.md` (owner-gated, the GATE-F overflow honesty).
- **Gate:** This slice **is** the gate-resolution slice. Within it, the tiers differ: GATE-F is Tier-3, because it amends locked spec text — owner approval is required, and the prior locked decision is quoted verbatim in the conversation. GATE-K is Tier-2, because it is new language surface — a conversation opener comes before any `/design`. GATE-L is a Tier-2 deferral. The remaining gates — B, C, D, E, G, H, J, N, and O — are CANON_DESIGN settle-or-confirm gates decided against existing design, not new surface.

### Slice 0.3 — Runtime-canon design reconciliations (capture so the deferred runtime build inherits a complete spec)

This slice captures the runtime contracts that the compiler's behavior forces. **Every capture targets a named `docs/runtime/*.md` section** — this is the boundary's mandatory discipline that nothing may be deferred without a capture pointer. The obligations that land in `evaluator.md §7.6` are authored as **ONE coherent constraint-plan/sweep design pass**, not as uncoordinated per-item edits; this is the runtime-canon analog of Phase 6's move that collapses the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers (the emit-or-retire dispositions, the conformance suite's coverage tags, and the Stage-2 audit) into the single merged diagnostic register. **None of this slice is runtime CODING** — it is runtime DESIGN, which is IN SCOPE for this plan. The executable bodies stay `NotImplementedException`; verified against source: in `Evaluator.cs`, Fire `:80`, Update `:99`, InspectFire `:121`, InspectUpdate `:132`, and Restore `:155` all `throw new NotImplementedException()`.

- **Work:**
  - **GATE-I — fault-delivery contract.** The runtime canon currently contradicts itself about how a fault is delivered — as a thrown exception or as a structured outcome value. This is a **verified contradiction inside runtime canon**, and the gate settles it as a design note reconciling all three sources to **ONE shape**:
    - The **throw** shape: `result-types.md:51` (verbatim: *"Faults … throw `FaultException` — they are not in the outcome hierarchy"*), reinforced at `:63`, where fault/exception handling is placed outside the outcome hierarchy's ownership (verbatim: *"Fault/exception handling — `FaultException` is defined in `fault-system.md`"*), and at `:72` (verbatim: *"excluded from the outcome hierarchy and throw `FaultException` instead"*).
    - The **structured-variant** shape: `result-types.md:104` declares `public sealed record Faulted(Fault Fault) : EventOutcome;`, the outcome table at `:117` lists `Faulted` as a Backstop variant, and `:127` states (verbatim) *"`Faulted` surfaces this as a structured outcome variant rather than a raw exception at the runtime boundary"*.
    - The evaluator's lean: `evaluator.md §7.6` references the structured variant, and `evaluator.md` **Decision 4, "Structured Outcomes, Never Exceptions"** (`evaluator.md:1754`) leans toward `Faulted`.
    - The open marker: `fault-system.md` **Q1** (`fault-system.md:288`, plus the "pending Q1" markers at `:8/:95/:301/:322/:337`) lists the question as **open** — verbatim Q1 framing: *"whether `Fault` is a control-flow exception or a structured value"*.
    - The task: **settle to ONE shape.** Decision 4 leans `Faulted`; either reconcile `result-types.md:51/63/72` to match, or document the runtime-boundary throw as a deliberate exception to the structured-outcome rule. The throw-versus-return **wiring** is deferred runtime code; the **contract** is what gets captured now. **This gate is owner-facing** — it sits adjacent to the guarantee surface — **so surface the contradiction to the owner; do NOT unilaterally pick a shape.**
  - **Restore residue.** Confirm that Restore **substantially dissolves** as a distinct validation concern, per spec §0.7. The dissolution reading is verified at `precept-language-spec.md:272`: hydration is *"trusted as valid at the time it was persisted … does not re-validate … the next operation through the contract re-governs it … the runtime fault traps backstop any out-of-contract value"*. Record the **verified spec contradiction** against `precept-language-spec.md:1946`, where §3A.2 says restoration *"produces a distinct outcome space: … constraint failure (persisted data violates current definition's rules/ensures)"* — that is restore-time re-validation, which contradicts the trusted-hydration model at :272. Settle the reading: next-operation re-governance holds, with the runtime fault traps as the out-of-contract backstop; the §3A.2 re-validation framing is the residue to reconcile away. Route the remaining definition-evolution and migration residue to **GATE-N**.
    - **One landable COMPILER_CODE edit falls out of this, and it must NOT be punted to some future "Restore-API finalization":** remove the `RestoreConstraintsFailed` discriminated-union subtype. It is **verified live at `RestoreOutcome.cs:18`**: `public sealed record RestoreConstraintsFailed(IReadOnlyList<ConstraintViolation> Violations) : RestoreOutcome;`. This removal is a **pure API-surface type deletion, classified COMPILER_CODE, and scheduled in Phase 7's API-solidity work** — it is **not** runtime coding, because it removes an outcome type that the dissolved-Restore model can no longer produce. This slice **records the obligation**; Phase 7 lands the edit.
  - **OD-1 — shared expression-evaluation core.** Record the constraint that the runtime evaluator and the compile-time `ConstantFold` must share **ONE** expression-evaluation core. This constraint is cheap to establish now and expensive to retrofit later. The stakes: if the two evaluation paths diverge, a value could constant-fold one way at compile time and evaluate another way at runtime, which breaks determinism — the philosophy commitment that same definition plus same data always yields the same outcome.
  - **Standing declines, each with a full four-leg rationale (per Frank §6 — the adversarial plan review's per-decision-rationale-completeness section, which found the captured standing declines stated their conclusion without the rejected-alternatives and tradeoff legs and required attaching them).** Record **convex-polyhedra domains** and **predicate abstraction** as **permanently off the interactive path**. The grounds: convex polyhedra's data-dependent latency breaks determinism requirement R2, and the theorem-prover step that predicate abstraction needs breaks requirement R1. R1 and R2 are the two hard requirements the solver-free static-analysis survey (`research/architecture/compiler/solver-free-static-analysis-techniques-survey.md`) holds every candidate proof technique to: R1 is no opaque solver and a legible witness; R2 is per-keystroke worst-case predictability — the pipeline recompiles on every keystroke, so a technique's worst-case cost must be bounded and data-independent. And the solver-free lock holds per spec §0.6 #3. Each decline is recorded with all four legs the per-decision rationale rule requires — Rationale, Alternatives rejected, Precedent, and Tradeoff accepted.
  - **D9 — injectable per-operation clock.** D9 is the decision number under which the clock requirement travels — a numbered decision label distinct from this plan's own D1 through D4. Record the requirement that `now()` reads an injectable per-operation clock, so that temporal evaluation is **deterministic and testable**. Determinism is a core philosophy commitment — same definition plus same data equals same outcome — and a direct wall-clock read breaks it. Application is queued for Phase 4.
  - **Point C — outcome-type ownership for external-input value violations.** Point C is one of the open questions earlier plans dropped or half-carried, now re-placed by this plan. The work: map which outcome type owns each kind of external-input value violation — `InvalidArgs` versus `ConstraintsFailed` versus `Faulted`. Reconcile the evaluator's argument gate with business-domain value/qualifier validation. And give the compile-time/runtime division of labor a **single canonical home** — today that division must be assembled from more than seven separate locations. **This MUST be specified before the runtime build starts** — a violation class with no assigned owner is a silent gap handed to the deferred initiative.
- **Exit criteria:** The fault-delivery contract is either settled to ONE shape in a runtime-canon note, or the contradiction is surfaced to the owner with the recommended shape and left pending the guarantee-surface call. The Restore dissolution and the verified §0.7:272-versus-§3A.2:1946 contradiction are reconciled, and the definition-evolution residue is routed to GATE-N. The `RestoreConstraintsFailed` removal is **recorded as a Phase-7 COMPILER_CODE obligation** with its `RestoreOutcome.cs:18` pointer. OD-1, the standing declines (each carrying all four rationale legs), D9, and Point C are recorded as runtime-build constraints, each with a named capture pointer. **Every capture names its `docs/runtime/*.md` section** — no deferral without a pointer. **No runtime code lands; the `Evaluator.cs` stubs remain stubs.** All of this is verifiable by inspecting the runtime-canon notes — none of them asserts that the runtime executes anything; each one *specifies* what the runtime must execute.
- **Doc-touch (runtime-canon capture, IN SCOPE for this slice):**
  - **GATE-I:** reconcile `result-types.md:51` (plus `:63/:72`) ↔ `result-types.md:104/:117/:127` ↔ `evaluator.md §7.6/§9` (plus Decision 4 at `:1754`) ↔ `fault-system.md` Q1 (`:288`) to one fault-delivery shape. Also record in `result-types.md` the shape of the recoverable governance refusal outcome — the structured refusal for reclassified band violations, which is distinct from `Faulted` — for the deferred band-governance lane.
  - **Restore:** `evaluator.md §7.1` (the FromJson/Restore dissolution plus the structural-consistency trap backstop) and `runtime-api.md § Restoration`. Note that the spec-side reconciliation of §3A.2:1946 versus §0.7:272 is owed in Phase 1 Slice 1.5 as CANON_DESIGN. The `RestoreConstraintsFailed` **code** removal is a Phase-7 `RestoreOutcome.cs` edit — recorded here, not landed here.
  - **OD-1:** `evaluator.md` (the shared-core constraint) and `compiler-and-runtime-design.md` (the ConstantFold↔evaluator shared-core seam).
  - **Standing declines:** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` (convex polyhedra and predicate abstraction off the interactive path, with the four-leg rationale).
  - **D9:** `evaluator.md` and `runtime-api.md` (the injectable per-operation clock, queued for Phase 4 application).
  - **Point C:** `evaluator.md` (the argument gate), `business-domain-types.md` (value/qualifier validation), and one single canonical home for the division of labor.
- **Gate:** **GATE-I is owner-facing**, because it sits adjacent to the guarantee surface — surface the contradiction; do not unilaterally pick the fault-delivery shape. The remaining captures — the Restore reading, OD-1, the standing declines, D9, and Point C — are CANON_DESIGN reconciliations against existing runtime canon, not new language surface. They proceed once recorded, with GATE-I and any other guarantee-surface reading surfaced for owner confirmation.

### Slice 0.4 — Grounding-research reconciliation (reconcile-before-feeding-agents gate)

- **Work:** Apply the § A checklist from the design-revision companion (`compiler-readiness-plan-2026-06-16-design-revision-notes.md` § A) to the grounding research document, `research/architecture/compiler/fault-floor-definition-2026-06-11.md`, so that it stops carrying the **superseded** parked/atomic framing before it grounds any Phase-1-or-later slice. The principle (recorded in memory as "reconcile stale docs before feeding agents"): a working or research document written before a later locked decision carries stale open questions that the new canon has since closed; the fix is to reconcile the source document itself, not to patch each agent prompt that cites it — agents inherit whatever they are handed. The concrete edit list, each item a § A row:
  - **Status field.** The doc's status field records the three applied owner decisions — D1 (overflow parked), D2 (band split), D3 (atomicity relaxed) — and records that the 2026-06-16 plan derives from the corrected framing. This applies Frank finding A1, the status-field finding — the advisory finding from the adversarial plan review that a document's front-matter status must state what the document actually is rather than overclaim settledness.
  - **Floor count 11 → 9.** D1 parks TWO families, not one: decimal representability overflow (family 9) AND temporal representability overflow (family 10) both move OUT of the prove-or-reject floor and into a new **"Known faults — accepted, parked"** sub-section. Removing two of eleven leaves nine. The corrected count is propagated everywhere the doc says "11 floor families," including the Conclusions block.
  - **Floor-invariant carve-out — the single most load-bearing reframe.** The doc's statement "prove-or-reject, never defer" is amended to read *"prove-or-reject EXCEPT decimal/temporal representability overflow — a parked known fault we never claim prevented,"* with the carve-out marked as an **owner-approved honesty carve-out** (it passes through the philosophy gate) and kept **distinct** from the band relocation — overflow is genuinely relaxed, while band prevention merely relocates.
  - **NumericOverflow inversion reframe.** Drop the doc's framing of NumericOverflow as "THE one missing obligation family / build at every sink." Keep its diagnosis of *why* the band lane was vacuous, since that analysis remains correct. Add a **PARKED** disposition replacing the `[high]` gap row. Note that `FaultCode.NumericOverflow` survives as a documented defense-in-depth known-fault trap — a runtime check kept as a backstop — with **no live `[StaticallyPreventable]` guarantee** behind it. Verified live against source: `FaultCode.cs:44-45` carries `[StaticallyPreventable(DiagnosticCode.NumericOverflow)]` on `NumericOverflow = 12` — that attribute partner is now an honest **gap**, not a guarantee. `NumericOverflow` also appears in `StaticallyPreventableMap.BijectiveDiagnosticCodes`; the registry trim in Phase 1 Slice 1.4 gives this an honest disposition.
  - **GATE-A relocation.** GATE-A moves from the "owner decision the plan gates on" category to a "Parked — revisit post-runtime" register. Record that "decimal without the obligation" is its de-facto resolution.
  - **Temporal overflow.** Folds into the same PARKED register. The doc's prescription "verify-then-build / ADD `FaultCode.TemporalOverflow`" becomes "document as parked known fault (same representability genus)." The **only** piece kept as COMPILER_CODE is the checked-constant-fold sliver — the compiler's own self-protection when it folds constants. Reconcile the P4-TEMP-11 row — temporal overflow's row in the spec-coverage-gap input register (Coverage Ledger §D), among the temporal rows the Phase-4 work inherits — to PARKED.

The only retained ordering is **registry-honest-before-position-checker** — the honest registry must land before the creation-exhaustiveness checker first reads it. Note additionally that the wiring leg of the old coordinated move was itself deferred runtime code, so the coordinated move was doubly void.

  - **Write-aware tier-2 split, made structural.** The doc's write-aware tier-2 discharge treatment is split along the compiler/runtime boundary: the same-transaction (intra-chain) half is compiler work, landing in Phase 3 Slice 3.1; the committed-state half is a deferred runtime leg. **No sentence in the research doc may claim committed-state discharge as a compile-time guarantee.**

Flag the philosophy gap this creates. Position-totality (the Phase-2 property that the obligation-creation walk visits every catalogued child-expression position) still closes every other lane.
  - **Conclusions four-leg rationale.** The Tradeoff leg of the doc's Conclusions changes to *"band scale-backs remove invented-bound friction; representability overflow accepted as a parked, documented known fault (honesty-over-completeness)"*. Attach the missing rejected-alternatives and tradeoff legs to the standing-declines record, per Frank §6 — the review's per-decision-rationale-completeness section, glossed at the standing-declines item in Slice 0.3.

Reconcile any fault-code member-count stated in the doc against the live `FaultCode.cs` — **verified at 15 members this pass**.
  - **Double-register dedup label (the FF-prefixed re-pass rows).** The research doc carries two sets of row identities: its work items live in the floor-gaps / scale-backs / registry-corrections registers, whose rows carry FG-, SB-, and RC- prefixes, while its own re-pass over its Findings, Floor, Scale-backs, Registry, and Conclusions sections carries FF-prefixed rows. Label that FF-prefixed re-pass explicitly as a **doc-revision-of-the-research-doc**, not a second set of work items, so that no reader mistakes a row like `FF-position-totality-checker` (the re-pass row about the creation-exhaustiveness checker) for a duplicate of `FG-01` (row 1 of the floor-gaps register — the obligation-position incompleteness finding). Also ensure the friction-reduction machinery has a **primary work home** in the Phase 2/3 verification slices, so that it exists as scheduled work and not only as a doc-revision row. That machinery comprises five proof-engine mechanisms — the mechanisms whose job is to keep the prove-or-reject discipline from producing spurious rejections or spurious diagnostics: flow narrowing; sign-set algebra; event-ensure folds; error-taint suppression under PE-G13; and forwarding-fact vacuous suppression with `ProofStrategy.Vacuous`. PE-G13 is the proof engine's upstream-error-handling rule (`docs/compiler/proof-engine.md`): an obligation whose expression already carries a type error stays unresolved but emits no proof diagnostic, because the type checker already reported the root cause.
- **Exit criteria:** The research doc no longer asserts any of the superseded claims: the 11-family floor, the "build representability at every sink" prescription, the atomic-coordinated-move constraint, the "zero consumers" wording, or the unqualified "no diagnostics ⇒ no faults" invariant. It carries the corrected content: the 9-family floor, the parked-overflow carve-out, the Frank B2 and B3 wording, the structural write-aware split, and the dedup label marking the FF-prefixed re-pass rows as doc revision. The friction-reduction machinery is pointed at its Phase-2/3 verification home. **This is the only Phase-0 exit criterion that gates the NEXT phase** — no Phase-1-or-later slice may start until this reconciliation lands, because those slices inherit whatever the research doc says. Verifiable by inspecting the reconciled research doc against the design-revision companion's § A row list; no code changes, no runtime outcome.
- **Doc-touch:** `research/architecture/compiler/fault-floor-definition-2026-06-11.md` receives the full § A edit list; the design-revision companion's § A serves as the checklist and is itself transient. Also update the issue map in `research/language/README.md` (or the relevant subfolder README) so that the reconciled research connects forward to the 2026-06-16 plan — research must not stand alone as policy, per the promote-or-cite rule.
- **Gate:** None. This is research-doc reconciliation against owner decisions that are already locked — D1, D2, and D3 are settled, so the application is mechanical, Tier-1 under the consultation tiers. It does, however, constitute a **hard internal ordering**: this reconciliation comes before any Phase-1-or-later slice. That ordering is mirrored in the Sequencing red-team section.

### Phase 0 exit (all of)

- Every gate in the canonical Phase-0 roster — **GATE-B/C/D/E/F/G/H/J/K/L/N/O from Slice 0.2, plus GATE-I from Slice 0.3** — has a **recorded disposition**. GATE-A is recorded as resolved (park), since D1 already settled it. GATE-M — the question of PRE0141 bound-expression ownership, i.e. whether emission of PRE0141 (`UnprovedAssignmentQualifierCompatibility`) for bound expressions has a single owning pipeline stage or keeps its current split ownership — is deliberately **not** a Phase-0 gate. It resolves at Phase-5 start as a side effect of 2c-ii — the second half of the locked relational-rules-and-bounds design, which makes field-referencing bound modifiers participate in proof — because 2c-ii's desugaring of bounds to rules can dissolve the split ownership. An explicit fallback carries it to Phase 6 if 2c-ii does not resolve it: the split ownership is then carried explicitly to Phase 6 as the one recorded carve-out in Slice 6.4's single-stage diagnostic-ownership analyzer.
- The **GATE-F amendment set is enumerated complete, per Frank B1** — every §0.7, §0.6, and Principle-11 site is named with its verbatim text, including the L112 "defensive redundancy" ruling and the D1 overflow-honesty addition — and the set is either owner-approved or explicitly recorded as pending. `philosophy.md` has been surfaced to the owner and **not edited**.
- The **runtime-canon reconciliations** — the GATE-I fault-delivery contract, the Restore dissolution together with the §3A.2/§0.7 contradiction, the OD-1 shared expression-evaluation core, the standing declines each with four-leg rationale, the D9 injectable clock, and the Point C outcome-type ownership map — are captured in their named `docs/runtime/*.md` sections, so the deferred runtime build inherits a complete spec. The `RestoreConstraintsFailed` removal is recorded as a Phase-7 COMPILER_CODE obligation.
- The **grounding research doc is reconciled** per Slice 0.4 — the only Phase-0 exit criterion that gates Phase 1 and beyond.
- **No COMPILER_CODE landed; no RUNTIME_CODE landed; the `Evaluator.cs` stubs remain stubs.** Every exit criterion is satisfied by an inspectable artifact — a disposition row, an owner direction, a runtime-canon note, or the reconciled research doc — and **none asserts a runtime-execution outcome**. Phase 0 lands no compiler admissions at all. The later phases will admit fault-safe operations and defer their **enforcement** to §11, the Runtime-Coding Handoff, each with a capture pointer — the admit-now / defer-enforcement discipline. No admission is ever gated on the runtime being built, and no compiler rejection is ever held in place merely because the runtime is absent.
- The plan is promoted from `Draft plan` to `Locked` only when this phase clears.

## 4. Phase 1 — Fault-registry honesty + band right-sizing (compiler half) (NEXT)

**Goal:** make the fault-code registry **honest**, and right-size the band lane, with every change landing **entirely compiler-side**. Three things land in this phase.

- **(a) Band reclassification.** The band-containment lane — the three diagnostics that check a value against a declared bound: PRE0078 (declared-band interval containment), PRE0135 (length containment), and PRE0136 (count containment) — is reclassified for non-proof-carrying fields. This reclassification is the application of owner decision D2 (band split), under which non-proof-carrying band checks are downgraded while the carries-proof set stays in the fault floor. Today these three checks are faults under prove-or-reject; after this phase they are a proven-violation **warning** instead. The practical effect is that the compiler stops false-rejecting valid precepts whose bounds it merely cannot prove.
- **(b) Registry repartition.** The **whole** fault-code registry is trimmed and repartitioned in one pass. This pass produces the **honest registry that the Phase-2 creation-exhaustiveness checker reads**. Position-totality is the Phase-2 property that the obligation-creation walk visits every catalogued child-expression position; the creation-exhaustiveness checker is the Phase-2 build-time check that asserts that property. That ordering — honest registry first, checker second — is the single hard cross-phase ordering the plan has, per D3 (atomicity relaxed).
- **(c) Canon amendments.** The owner-approved spec and canon amendments land, making the canonical docs consistent with the shipped reclassification and disclosing the parked overflow families honestly.

Two things deliberately do **not** land in this phase, and are instead **captured in runtime canon and deferred** to §11 (Runtime-Coding Handoff): the runtime **enforcement** of the reclassified bounds, and the committed-state discharge soundness work that the bound reclassification implies. Nothing this phase asserts requires the stubbed runtime to execute.

**Dependencies:**

- **Upstream (Phase 0 gates).** Phase 0 must have cleared five owner gates before this phase can stage its routing table: GATE-D (how PRE0101, the duplicate-key diagnostic, is routed), GATE-E (the posture for mincount at entity birth), GATE-F (the complete spec/philosophy amendment set from Frank finding B1, owner-approved), GATE-G (the disposition of min-bound discharge vacuity — the problem that a minimum-bound discharge can today be recorded as vacuously proved; the owner picks between an in-engine fix that makes `DeclarationValue` satisfactions resolvable in the proof engine and a narrowing amendment to the spec's §0.7 discharge-tier sentence), and GATE-H (confirmation of the three-category taxonomy). The routing table built in Slice 1.1 cannot be staged until those answers exist.
- **Within-phase (D3 — exactly ONE hard ordering).** Slice 1.4, the registry trim and repartition, must complete **before** the Phase-2 creation-exhaustiveness checker reads the fault codes. That is the only hard ordering. The prior plan's "Slice-wiring-before-band-retirement" coupling — the requirement that the runtime-enforcement wiring for the reclassified bounds land before the compile-time band rejection was retired, so that the bounds would never pass through a window in which nothing enforced them — is **dropped** under D3 (atomicity relaxed). The reasoning: with the runtime stubbed, the reclassified bounds are enforced nowhere regardless of ordering, so the transient window between slices is harmless. Slices 1.3 (band reclassification), 1.4 (registry), and 1.5 (spec amendment) therefore land **incrementally** — no atomic merge.
- **Owner decisions owed at slice start.** Slice 1.2, the DesugarsToRule synthesis, carries an owner decision at slice start — whether to land the synthesis ahead of the runtime sweep or to co-defer the two. That decision must be surfaced to the owner, not assumed. Separately, Slice 1.5's reconciliation of `philosophy.md` is **owner-gated and never auto-edited.**

**Effort class:** L (7–11 days). This is lighter than the original "Floor Right-Size" estimate — "Floor Right-Size" being the prior plan's name for this phase — for two reasons: the coordinated-move machinery is gone under D3, and the work to retarget the registry toward a representability obligation drops out under D1 (overflow parked) — which removes the single largest sub-item.

---

### Slice 1.1 — Routing table (staging; no code/spec edit)

- **Work:** Translate the Phase-0 owner answers into a single concrete **routing table**. The table has one row per affected diagnostic code. Each row records the code's post-move identity, and its `FaultCode`, if it has one. The first three possible identities — `fault`, `definition-incoherence`, and `flag-warning` — are the three categories of the settled three-category taxonomy: the fault floor (prove-or-reject), definition incoherence, and the flag layer. The fourth identity, `governance-exit`, is not a fourth diagnostic category: it marks a code that leaves the diagnostic lanes entirely because the behavior it checked becomes a rule, which governance owns and whose enforcement is deferred to the runtime — the PRE0101 put-semantics outcome below is the instance. The rows to stage:
  - **PRE0101** (the duplicate-key diagnostic) routes to either `DuplicateKey` or a governance exit, keyed off the GATE-D answer. If GATE-D rules that inserting a key requires its absence, `DuplicateKey` stays in the fault lane. If GATE-D rules for put/overwrite semantics, uniqueness becomes a **rule**, which means governance owns it and enforcement is deferred. One part of this row is unconditional: today PRE0101 falsely collapses into `CollectionEmptyOnMutation`, and that collapse is fixed **regardless** of which way GATE-D goes — so the fix gets its own row marked "lands unconditionally."
  - **Qualifier taxonomy label** — the row for GATE-C. GATE-C is the Phase-0 gate over the qualifier assignment lane. The behavior of that lane is settled either way: keep both of the lane's existing rejections, and build the qualifier-source obligation — the build lands in Phase 3 Slice 3.2. GATE-C settles only the *label* under which the lane is classified. The two candidate labels: keep the existing `QualifierMismatch` fault-code identity, or introduce a new, separately named category for type-identity violations. The gate never blocks, because the build proceeds identically under either answer — which is why GATE-C is not among the five blocking gates in this phase's upstream dependencies. The row records whichever label the owner chose. This is a label cell, not a gating cell.
  - **Mincount-at-birth posture** (GATE-E) — the answer is one of: a birth exemption, a deferred first-mutation check, or an every-Create-fails flag. Record which compile-side artifact Phase 3 owes as a result (a proof-ledger fix, a definition-incoherence flag, or both — "ledger" here meaning the proof ledger that records discharges, not this plan's Coverage Ledger). Also record that the `count>0` discharge arm of Strategy 2 **survives** this move.
  - **Authorized amendments** — the exact sentences in spec §0.7, spec §0.6, and Principle 11 — a principle in `precept-language-spec.md`, not in `philosophy.md` — that GATE-F approved for editing. Recording them here means Slice 1.5 applies only the approved set, nothing more.
  - **omit × global-rule join** — record the Phase-0 adjudication of how an `omit` interacts with a global rule. If the owner ruled it floor-shaped, the row says "queue Phase 2 position walk." If flag-shaped — meaning the join is a matter of PRE0082 hook-guard coverage, where PRE0082 is `UnsatisfiableGuard`, the diagnostic for a guard that can never be true, and the hook-guard question is its coverage of the guards on state hooks — the row says "record the PRE0082 hook-guard reclassification to the post-runtime flag layer." Either way the row states which — no silent move.
- **Exit criteria:** The routing table exists, as compiler-internal staging — it lives in the PR body, not in any canonical doc. It carries an explicit row for every code that Slices 1.3–1.5 touch, and every row has both a post-move identity and a marker saying either "conditional on GATE-X" or "lands unconditionally." No code and no spec is edited in this slice; the slice is verifiable simply by the table's presence and by the downstream slices consuming it.
- **Doc-touch:** The routing table is recorded in the PR body only — a transient staging artifact, not canon. No canonical doc is edited.

### Slice 1.2 — DesugarsToRule compile-side synthesis (COMPILER_CODE; runtime sweep DEFERRED)

- **Work:** Synthesize the `DesugarsToRule` catalog metadata on modifiers and bounds into **span-tagged `TypedRule` / `TypedEnsure` constraint objects in the typed model**. This is the catalog-driven compile-time lowering that produces the constraint shapes. It is testable by asserting that the synthesized constraint shape exists in `Compiler.Compile` output — **no runtime execution is involved.** Be clear about the split: this slice is only the compiler half of the desugar; the runtime half — the governance/ingress **sweep that actually fires** the synthesized rules — is deferred.
  - **Wording discipline (Frank finding B2):** be precise about what consumes `DesugarsToRule` today. It has **zero constraint-synthesis or enforcement consumers**, but **exactly 2 cosmetic/display consumers exist**: the MCP catalog formatter (`tools/Precept.Mcp/CatalogFormatters.cs:253+`, which renders the `Desugars to rule: …` lines — verified live) and the GrammarGen TextMate color lane (`GrammarGen/Program.cs:173`). No pipeline stage synthesizes it into a `TypedRule` or `TypedEnsure`. Never write "ZERO consumers."
  - **⚠ Unconsumed-artifact guard (Gate — a decision owed to the owner at slice start):** synthesizing constraints that nothing reads creates **dead model state**. This slice is genuinely compiler-pure **only if** a real compile-time consumer exercises the synthesized rule. The candidate consumers are: the Phase-2 creation-exhaustiveness checker, a model-shape test, or the Phase-5 prove-or-reject discharge for 2c-ii. There is a real possibility that 2c-ii discharges via relational narrowing rather than by reading the synthesized `TypedRule` — confirm which in Phase 5. **If 2c-ii does not read the synthesized rule, co-defer this synthesis together with the runtime sweep** rather than landing an unconsumed half. Surface the land-now-versus-co-defer decision to the owner at slice start, and apply the same caveat to the Phase-5 2c-ii synthesis.
- **Exit criteria (compile-time only):** Either (1) the modifier/bound `DesugarsToRule` metadata produces the synthesized `TypedRule`/`TypedEnsure` shape in `Compiler.Compile` output, with the model-shape test green, **and a named in-scope compile-time consumer reads it** — verified via the model-shape test plus `dotnet test`; **or** (2) the slice is co-deferred with the runtime sweep and that decision is recorded with its reason. No exit criterion asserts that the synthesized rule fires at runtime.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` records the `DesugarsToRule → typed-model synthesis` seam — the compiler half. **Runtime-canon capture (deferred enforcement, RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER):** `docs/runtime/evaluator.md §7.6` records how the synthesized constraints are loaded into the constraint plan and fired at each commit and ingress lane. That sweep is the deferred half, and the §7.6 section is the capture pointer that keeps the deferral honest (§11).

### Slice 1.3 — Band reclassification for NON-PROOF-CARRYING fields (compiler-pure, D2)

- **Work:** For **non-proof-carrying fields only**, reclassify the band-containment checks from fault/prove-or-reject down to a **proven-violation warning** — a compile-time flag. The point is that the compiler stops rejecting intervals it merely cannot prove. The concrete moves:
  - PRE0078 declared-band `IntervalContainment`, PRE0135 `LengthContainment`, and PRE0136 `CountContainment` each become the proven-violation warning — a downgrade from prove-or-reject.
  - **Split and rename PRE0078** so that its "exceeded the representable range" message no longer attaches to a band check. With representability overflow **parked** under D1, that message now attaches to nothing. This is a compiler-pure message split; it is unrelated to the parked overflow fault families, and it is independent of the representability obligation that D1 declines to build.
  - **Fix the PRE0135 `?` placeholder** — the unresolved slot in that diagnostic's message.
  - **Preserve the carries-proof set.** The real dividing line is carries-proof versus not, not band-versus-not-band. Two things survive explicitly and keep prove-or-reject: mincount's `count>0` access-safety discharge arm in Strategy 2, and the preservation of sign-modifier and rule-fact reasoning.
  - Dissolve the band half of **BUG-017** and all of **BUG-018** as fault-floor items. BUG-017 is the bug that the proof engine silently skips the result-versus-declared-bound check when the result interval is unbounded; its band half is the declared-band containment portion, which this reclassification dissolves. BUG-018 is the bug that declared `maxcount`/`mincount` were never enforced — the count-containment obligation was never created, so the proof was dead code. **Re-file BUG-021** (min-only containment) to Phase 3, where everything that survives the overflow park is re-verified.
  - **OutOfRange asymmetry (Frank finding A2 — verified):** `OutOfRange`'s `DiagnosticCode` was **never** a member of `StaticallyPreventableMap.BijectiveDiagnosticCodes`. That set holds only `DivisionByZero, SqrtOfNegative, UnguardedCollectionAccess, UnguardedCollectionMutation, NumericOverflow, LengthBoundViolation, CountBoundViolation` (verified at `StaticallyPreventableMap.cs:28–37`). Therefore "remove OutOfRange from the bijective core" is a **no-op**; the bijective removal is real only for `LengthBoundViolation` and `CountBoundViolation`. This is noted in-line so that the executor — and Slice 1.4 — does not hunt for a nonexistent OutOfRange bijective row.
  - **Frank finding A6 (commit and PR framing):** this change **relocates** prevention — from compile-time rejection to future runtime governance, for non-proof-carrying values. It does **not** weaken prevention. Keep that framing **distinct** from D1's overflow, which is genuinely relaxed — a disclosed parked gap — rather than relocated.
  - Per **D3** (atomicity relaxed), this slice lands **without** waiting on the deferred runtime enforcement.
- **Exit criteria (compile-time only — verifiable via `precept_compile` / `dotnet test`):** an out-of-band write on a **non-proof-carrying** field **compiles clean** — it is no longer a fault rejection. A **provable** band violation emits the proven-violation **warning**. The **carries-proof set still rejects** unprovable cases: the mincount `count>0` discharge is intact, and sign and rule-fact preservation are intact. No diagnostic message claims "representable range" for a band check. **No exit criterion asserts "governance refuses the write"** — that refusal is the deferred runtime enforcement, captured in §11.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` — rewrite the no-deferral contracts for the reclassified families — the doc's per-family statements that a family is proved at compile time or rejected, with no deferral to a runtime check — and update the Strategy rows. `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` — the PRE0078 / PRE0135 / PRE0136 reclassification: severity, message, and the PRE0078 split. **Runtime-canon capture (deferred, RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER):** `docs/runtime/evaluator.md §7.6` — the reclassified bounds are evaluated as a **recoverable governance refusal**, NOT as `EventOutcome.Faulted`. `docs/runtime/fault-system.md` — OutOfRange / LengthBound / CountBound are no longer fault codes for these fields, so the impossible-path table — the fault-system doc's table of fault paths that compile-time prevention makes impossible at runtime — omits them. `docs/runtime/result-types.md` — the recoverable-refusal outcome shape, kept distinct from `Faulted`.

### Slice 1.4 — Registry trim + repartition (WHOLE, one pass — the HARD-ordering producer)

This slice produces the one hard ordering constraint that binds scheduling under D3. The registry must be honest — which requires both the band reclassification (Slice 1.3) and this repartition — **before** the Phase-2 creation-exhaustiveness checker asserts against it. Execute all the corrections below as **one pass**, so the Phase-2 checker never reads a half-repartitioned registry.

- **Work:**
  - **REMOVE** `LengthBoundViolation` and `CountBoundViolation` from `[StaticallyPreventable]` **and** from `BijectiveDiagnosticCodes` (`StaticallyPreventableMap.cs:28–37`), and strip the `[StaticallyPreventable]` partner from `OutOfRange`. Per Frank finding A2: the bijective-core removal is real only for Length/Count; for OutOfRange it is a no-op, since OutOfRange was never in the bijective set. Author-visible behavior for PRE0079-on-default is unchanged — PRE0079 is `OutOfRange`, the diagnostic emitted when a declared default value violates a numeric bound, and on that default-value case it keeps Error severity under its definition-incoherence identity.
  - **NumericOverflow (D1):** do **NOT** retarget it to a representability obligation. Keep `FaultCode.NumericOverflow` (`FaultCode.cs:45`, value `12`) as a documented **known-fault defense-in-depth trap**. Its `[StaticallyPreventable]` partner is an honest **gap**, not a guarantee. (The PRE0078 split/rename rides Slice 1.3, not this slice.) **Frank finding A3, executor precision:** the statement "no live obligation behind `NumericOverflow`" is the **target state, and it is not true at HEAD**. Today the computed-field result-versus-declared-bound check at `ProofEngine.Analysis.cs:538` (`CollectComputedFieldBoundObligations`) stamps a live `IntervalContainmentProofRequirement` that routes to `NumericOverflow`. That obligation is exactly the band `IntervalContainment` that **Slice 1.3 reclassifies** — so the honest gap becomes true only **after** Slice 1.3 lands, not before.
  - **PRECEPT0002 analyzer touch (in-slice work, buildability-verified):** the completeness analyzer for `[StaticallyPreventable]` (`src/Precept.Analyzers/Precept0002FaultCodeMustHaveStaticallyPreventable.cs`, Error severity, wired into the compiler build via `src/Precept/Precept.csproj:28-30`) fires on any `FaultCode` member lacking the attribute. That means stripping `OutOfRange`'s attribute while keeping the member fails `dotnet build` as this slice is literally written. The analyzer is therefore in this slice's touch set, ahead of the scheduled Phase-6 analyzer edits. The design call is already made — an attribute-less member can be an honest gap — so the in-slice work is only the **mechanism for admitting an honest gap**: either the analyzer learns to accept a deliberately-unprevented member (via an explicit marker it recognizes), or the member is deleted. Record whichever disposition is taken.
  - **ADD** three routes. First, `FaultCode.IndexOutOfBounds`, routing PRE0100 — this fixes the false "collection was empty" message on what is actually an index failure. Second, `FaultCode.KeyNotFound`, routing PRE0099. Third, the **bijective row for `UnexpectedNull`**, routing PRE0116 — PRE0116 is `UnprovedPresenceRequirement`, the unproved-presence diagnostic. Today `UnexpectedNull` (`FaultCode.cs:24`, value `5`) carries `[StaticallyPreventable(DiagnosticCode.UnprovedPresenceRequirement)]`, but its diagnostic is **not** in `BijectiveDiagnosticCodes` — so a presence trap falls through the backstop and reports a **divisor** message. Adding the bijective row fixes that misroute.
  - **PRE0101 → DuplicateKey** only if GATE-D rules for **require-absence** semantics. Under put semantics, PRE0101 exits to governance instead — the compile-side exit is recorded here, and enforcement is deferred. The false `CollectionEmptyOnMutation` collapse is fixed **regardless** of the GATE-D outcome.
  - **RETIRE** the generic `_ => FaultCode.DivisionByZero` backstop — the fall-through arm in `CreateFaultSiteLink`'s `diagnosticCode switch`, at `ProofEngine.Diagnostics.cs` ~`:488–491`. **Executor note:** there are **two additional** `DivisionByZero` fall-through sites to retire beyond the backstop: the DiagnosticCode-level defaults at `ProofEngine.Diagnostics.cs:386` and `:392`, in addition to the FaultCode backstop at `:490`. The replacement is **per-family honest routing**, keyed to requirement identity rather than to expression shape: the qualifier family routes to `QualifierMismatch`; the PRE0157 dimensional-product family routes to the `TypeMismatch` genus or a new code. That requirement-identity rewrite covers the function containing both extra sites, and the exit criteria check both in addition to the backstop. This item also includes the **pow-exponent misroute fix**: today the routing shape-keys `≥0 ⇒ SqrtOfNegative`, so a pow-exponent obligation reports as a square-root problem; re-key the routing to requirement identity so a pow-exponent obligation reports honestly.
  - **Do NOT add `FaultCode.TemporalOverflow`.** Under D1, temporal overflow parks together with decimal overflow — they are the same representability genus.
  - **KEEP** the four type-checker traps — `TypeMismatch`, `UndeclaredField`, `InvalidMemberAccess`, and `FunctionArityMismatch` — as defense-in-depth, with no proof obligation owed. Add a one-line disposition for evaluation-stack depth.
  - **ADD `ProofStrategy.Vacuous`** — a ledger-honesty rider. Replace the `ProofStrategy.Literal // vacuously proved` mislabel at `ProofEngine.cs:1181` (and the parallel dead-end-state suppression at `:1194`) with the new strategy. The soundness of recording these as vacuous is verified against `GraphAnalyzer.BuildEdges`: that function ignores `row.Guard`, so it over-approximates reachability — meaning "unreachable" is a structural fact, and recording it as `Vacuous` is honest, where claiming a real `Literal` proof was not. The `ProofStrategy` enum lives at `ProofLedger.cs:100`.
  - **Frank finding A3 guardrail:** deliver the honest routing by **extending the attribute-derived bijective map and the `[StaticallyPreventable]` attributes**. The residual hand-written switch in `CreateFaultSiteLink` must **shrink, not grow** — do not trade the generic-backstop smell for a switch-on-`*Kind`-identity smell, which the CLAUDE.md catalog rule forbids.
- **Exit criteria (verifiable via `dotnet test` + `precept_compile`):** No rule code is registered as a fault — Length/Count/OutOfRange are stripped from `[StaticallyPreventable]`, and Length/Count are stripped from the bijective core. No obligation family routes through a literal `_ => DivisionByZero` fall-through at **any** of the three sites — the DiagnosticCode-level defaults at `ProofEngine.Diagnostics.cs:386` and `:392` and the FaultCode backstop at `:490` — with per-family honest routing in place instead. The PRECEPT0002 honest-gap mechanism is dispositioned (the analyzer accepts the deliberately-unprevented member, or the member is deleted) and `dotnet build` is green. PRE0099, PRE0100, and PRE0101 no longer produce false-emptiness messages. A presence, currency, or pow fault reports an **honest** message rather than a divisor message. `ProofStrategy.Vacuous` is recorded instead of `Literal // vacuously proved`. The `CreateFaultSiteLink` switch **shrank**. The bijective map matches `FaultCode.cs`. **This honest registry is the prerequisite the Phase-2 checker reads** — the plan's one HARD ordering.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` — the code routing for PRE0099/0100/0101/0116/0157 plus the retired backstop. **Runtime-canon capture (CANON_DESIGN, in scope):** `docs/runtime/fault-system.md` — fix the 13-versus-15 member-count drift (finding RC-09); replace the stale `NullInNonNullableContext` partner with `UnprovedPresenceRequirement`, which is the live presence diagnostic (`FaultCode.cs:23` carries the `[StaticallyPreventable(DiagnosticCode.UnprovedPresenceRequirement)]` partner on `UnexpectedNull`); and, in the same pass, **document `NumericOverflow` as an accepted, NOT-statically-prevented known fault**. `docs/runtime/result-types.md` — the FaultCode inventory. Reconcile any member-count figure against the live `FaultCode.cs` (Frank finding B3, provenance — the enum currently runs `DivisionByZero=1 … CountBoundViolation=15`).

**Gate (HARD, D3):** Slice 1.4 must be committed and green before any work on the Phase-2 checker begins; the Phase-2 checker asserts against the post-repartition codes.

### Slice 1.5 — Spec/canon amendments (CANON_DESIGN, owner-gated) + canon-drift reconcile + overflow honesty doc

- **Work:**
  - Apply **ONLY** the amendment set that GATE-F and Frank finding B1 **enumerated and the owner approved**. The full enumeration was settled in Phase 0; this slice applies it and nothing beyond it. The approved set:

Document NumericOverflow as a known-not-prevented fault in the same pass. This coordinates with Slice 1.4's touch on the same file — author the two as one consistent edit.

---

**Phase 1 exit:** Four conditions, one per slice. The band lane is right-sized **compiler-side** (Slice 1.3): out-of-band writes on non-proof-carrying fields compile clean, provable violations warn, and the carries-proof set still rejects. The fault registry is **honest** (Slice 1.4): no rule codes are registered as faults, per-family routing is in place, the bijective map matches `FaultCode.cs`, and `ProofStrategy.Vacuous` is in place. The spec and canon are **reconciled and owner-approved** (Slice 1.5): overflow is honestly disclosed, `fault-system.md` matches the registry, and the `philosophy.md` gap is surfaced but the file is not edited. The modifier/bound synthesis either landed or was co-deferred with a recorded reason (Slice 1.2). Sequencing was **incremental — no atomic merge**, per D3; the **one HARD ordering** — Slice 1.4's honest registry **before** the Phase-2 checker — is satisfied. Every runtime-enforcement half (the band governance, the DesugarsToRule sweep) is **captured in `docs/runtime/*.md` and deferred** (§11), under admit-now / defer-enforcement: the compiler admits the fault-safe operation (the band lane compiles clean), the runtime enforcement defers with a capture pointer, and no exit criterion above asserts a runtime-execution outcome.

## 5. Phase 2 — Obligation-position totality + creation-exhaustiveness checker

**Nature:** COMPILER_CODE — compiler implementation — with one CANON_DESIGN slice (record-only documentation work) that captures the runtime precondition this phase secures.

**Goal:** Make completeness of the fault floor a **structural** guarantee rather than a hand-maintained one. Here is the problem this phase fixes. Today, the fault floor is built by a hand-written obligation-creation walk over the expression tree. That walk recurses into a *hand-picked subset* of child-expression positions per arm. It has **no default arm and no exhaustiveness check**. The consequence: if a subexpression that can fault sits in a position the walk was never enrolled to visit — a `when` guard, an interpolation hole inside a `because` message, an argument to a member call — the walk collects **no obligation for it, and the proof ledger reports a false `Proved`**. Two live bugs verify this is real, not hypothetical: BUG-031 (arithmetic inside a guard collects no divisor obligation) and BUG-032 (`coll.at(A / B)` collects no divisor obligation for the argument). This phase does four things. First, it catalogs every child-expression position as a single source of truth (Slice 2.1). Second, it closes the 10 live fail-open positions so the walk is total — it visits every catalogued position (Slice 2.2). Third, it builds a build-time checker that fails the compiler's own build if any obligation family ever stops being created at any catalogued position again (Slice 2.3). Fourth, it repairs the corpus of sample and fixture files that the now-correct walk breaks (Slice 2.4). Finally, the runtime precondition this totality secures — the InspectFire / evaluator-§10 dependency, explained in Slice 2.5 — is captured in the runtime canon docs.

**HARD dependency:** Phase 1 Slice 1.4 — the registry trim and repartition that produces the honest fault-code registry — must land before this phase's checker is built. Concretely, the checker and the closed walk must assert against the registry *as it stands after* that repartition: the IndexOutOfBounds and KeyNotFound fault codes routed to their own diagnostics, UnexpectedNull given a one-to-one pairing with its preventing diagnostic, the `_ => DivisionByZero` fallback arm retired, and `ProofStrategy.Vacuous` in place. `ProofStrategy.Vacuous` is the proof-strategy enum member that Slice 1.4 adds as a ledger-honesty measure: an obligation that is discharged only because its position is structurally unreachable is recorded honestly as vacuous, instead of being mislabeled as a literal proof the way the current code records it. This is the single cross-phase hard ordering that binds scheduling under owner decision D3 (atomicity relaxed — changes land incrementally, with exactly this one must-precede constraint kept). Every other must-precede relationship the plan names is already satisfied by the phase order as written. Why the ordering is hard rather than a preference: a checker built to assert against the *pre*-repartition registry would bake the dishonest fault-code routing into the structural guarantee itself, permanently.

**Other dependencies:** Two Phase 0 items feed this phase. First, Phase 0 Slice 0.1(b) adjudicates the interaction between `omit` and global rules; that adjudication decides whether the omit-join is a position this phase's walk must cover — see Slice 2.3 for how each outcome is handled. Second, the BUG-031 owner fork: the owner must choose between walking guard expressions for obligations versus restricting the guard grammar so guards cannot fault. The conservative default is *walk*. The fork is surfaced as a Tier-2/3 owner consultation — a conversation before design, escalating if it conflicts with locked spec — before Slice 2.2 builds.

**Effort:** L (8–12 days). The catalog projection, the walk closure, and the checker are mechanical work against locked spec. The variable-size tail is the corpus-repair wave in Slice 2.4, whose size depends on how many existing files were leaning on the false `Proved`s.

**Verifiability:** Everything Phase 2 produces is provable at compile time via `precept_compile` / `dotnet build` / `dotnet test` with the runtime still stubbed. BUG-031 and BUG-032 were both *behaviorally confirmed* by running `precept_compile` on repro files — not by grepping the source — and their fixes are confirmed the same way. No exit criterion in this phase asserts a runtime-execution outcome.

---

### Slice 2.1 — `ChildExpressionPositions` catalog projection (catalog-before-code)

- **Work:** Add a single catalog projection, named `ChildExpressionPositions`, that enumerates — for every discriminated-union subtype that carries expressions — the set of child positions a pipeline walk must recurse into. The projection covers the **5 expression-bearing DU families**:
  - `TypedExpression` — 41 records in `SemanticIndex.cs`, of which roughly 15 have child positions typed as `TypedExpression`. The positions to catalog: `TypedBinaryOp{Left,Right}`; `TypedFunctionCall{Arguments[]}`; `TypedMemberAccess{Object, Arguments[]}` — and note that the **`Arguments[]` position is exactly the BUG-032 gap**: the member is `TypedMemberAccess.Arguments` at `SemanticIndex.cs:113`, and the existing walk at `ProofEngine.cs:319-323` recurses into `Object` only, never into the arguments; `TypedUnaryOp{Operand}`; `TypedConditional{Condition,ThenBranch,ElseBranch}`; `TypedQuantifier{Collection,Predicate}`; and the interpolation, collection-literal, and list-element arms.
  - `TypedAction` — 3 shapes; catalog the value and target child positions per action.
  - `TypedInterpolationSegment` — 2 subtypes. `TypedTextSegment` has no child positions. `TypedHoleSegment{Expression}` at `SemanticIndex.cs:158` is the **`because`-interpolation hole** — an expression embedded in a `because` message — and it is one of the live fail-open positions.
  - `TypedTransitionRow` — 2 subtypes; `TypedTransitionRowSuccess` carries a `Guard`, at `SemanticIndex.cs:571`/`582`.
  - `TypedEventRow` — 2 subtypes; carries a `Guard`, at `SemanticIndex.cs:592`/`600`.
  - Include explicitly the two positions the current code never iterates at all: the **AccessModes guard collection** — the state-hook / access-mode `.Guard` at `SemanticIndex.cs:526`/`612` — and **`TypedMemberAccess.Arguments`**.
  - **Declared-exclusion vocabulary:** the projection must also be able to declare that a given child position is *deliberately not walked*. Some members typed as child expressions are intentionally skipped — for example `TypedPostfixOp.Operand`, where recursing would create a spurious presence obligation. Give the projection an explicit "deliberately excluded, with reason" disposition for each such member. This keeps the reflection test honest: every subtype is present, and every child-expression-typed member is either walked or catalogued as a declared exclusion — never silently absent from the accounting.
  - **Navigation note:** the `SemanticIndex.cs` line citations in this slice have rotated at HEAD. Navigate by record and member **name**, which are stable, not by line number. The reflection-based exit criterion never depended on the line numbers.
- **Catalog discipline (Frank finding A3 — derive, don't proliferate):** this projection becomes the single source of truth that every pipeline stage's child-walk derives from. It replaces the current pattern in which each switch arm hand-picks which children to recurse into. One clarification against the catalog rules in CLAUDE.md: this projection is metadata about the DU's *shape*, not enum-identity dispatch. Switching on the DU subtype in order to read that subtype's declared positions is the correct pattern — the subtype IS the metadata shape. It is not a forbidden `*Kind`-identity switch.
- **Exit criteria:** the `ChildExpressionPositions` projection exists and enumerates the child-expression positions for all 5 DU families, with every subtype present. A unit test asserts, per subtype, that the declared positions match the record's actual members typed as child `TypedExpression` — counting declared exclusions, so that walked positions plus deliberate exclusions together cover every such member. The practical effect: adding a new child-expression member to a record without cataloguing it fails the test. `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` are green. There is no `precept_compile` behavior change yet, because the projection has no consumer until Slices 2.2 and 2.3.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/language/catalog-system.md` (add `ChildExpressionPositions` to the canonical catalog inventory); `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` § Obligation Generation Contract (record that the walk now derives its recursion set from the projection, not from a hand-written switch).

---

### Slice 2.2 — Total obligation-creation walk (close the 10 fail-open positions)

- **Gate (before build):** BUG-031's owner fork must be resolved before this slice builds. The fork is tracked as open question O-8 in the bug tracker's BUG-031 entry — O-8 is the open-question number the soundness-and-coverage design work assigned when it first surfaced the question, and the bug entry carries it forward. The fork itself: *walk guards* for obligations, versus *restrict the guard grammar in the spec* so that guards cannot contain faulting operations. This is a Tier-2/3 owner consultation — it turns on a grammar-and-intent question (can guards fault?) that the proof engine cannot settle unilaterally. The conservative, prevention-preserving default is **walk + reject unprovable**: a guard operation whose safety cannot be proven is a rejection, not a skip, which keeps the code on the safe path. Confirm the fork's disposition with the owner at slice start. If the owner chooses "restrict," then the restriction must be stated in the language spec and enforced by a type-checker invariant — it must not be left implicit in the proof engine's omission — and that arm re-routes to the Phase 5 binder/type-checker work, while this slice still closes all the non-guard positions.
- **Work:** Drive `CollectObligations` (`ProofEngine.cs:208-269`) and `WalkExpression` (`ProofEngine.cs:275`) off the Slice-2.1 projection, so the walk is **total** over child positions. This closes all 10 live fail-open positions:
  1–5. **The guard slots — six distinct guard-bearing record shapes, not five.** Enroll the `.Guard` / condition expression of each of the following as obligation-walk entry points: rule guards, transition-row guards, event-row guards, ensure guards, access-mode guards (including the **AccessModes collection** that the current code never iterates), and **state-hook guards**. Two counts appear here — five numbered positions, six record shapes — and both are correct; here is how they reconcile. The earlier count of "5 guard slots" bundled the state-hook guards and the access-mode guards into one slot. That bundled numbering is what the phase total of 10 fail-open positions is built on, so the guard family keeps positions 1 through 5 in that total and the total stays at 10. But the bundled pair are in fact two distinct record shapes, and the state hooks' `Guard` is never walked today — `CollectObligations` walks `hook.Actions` but never `hook.Guard`. The implementer should therefore enumerate six record shapes while closing these five numbered positions. For context on the current state: `CollectObligations` today walks rule and ensure *conditions* plus computed and default expressions, but it passes **no `.Guard`** to `WalkExpression`. Guards are consulted only on the *discharge* side, for narrowing (`ProofEngine.Intervals.cs:523-529`) — never on the emission side. Closing these slots closes **BUG-031**.
  6–7. **2 because-interpolation slots** — recurse into `TypedHoleSegment.Expression` for the interpolated expressions inside rule and ensure `because` clauses.
  8–9. **2 default-expression interiors** — recurse into the *interior* of a field's `DefaultExpression` and an event argument's `DefaultExpression`. The declared-bound and qualifier obligations for defaults are already collected, via `CollectDefaultObligations` / `CollectArgDefaultObligations` at `:264`/`:267`; what is missing is that faults *nested inside* the default — for example a divisor within the default expression — must also be walked.
  10. **`TypedMemberAccess.Arguments`** — recurse into `ma.Arguments[]`; the walk at `:322` currently stops at `ma.Object`. Closing this closes **BUG-032**.
- **Bundle (HARD, same slice) — intra-guard short-circuit conjunct narrowing:** closing the guard slots creates an immediate regression risk for the canonical optional-field idiom `when X is set and X > 5`. Once the guard is walked, the newly-visited `X > 5` raises a presence obligation — an obligation that the preceding `X is set` conjunct ought to discharge. Without help, that idiom would mass-false-reject across the corpus. So this slice must also ship the **minimum discharge power**: intra-guard short-circuit conjunct narrowing, meaning a left conjunct like `X is set` narrows the facts available to the right conjunct. This narrowing lands in the **same slice** as the walk closure. That bundling is deliberate: D3's atomicity relaxation removes coordinated cross-cutting merges, but it explicitly keeps internal-correctness bundles like this one, because the closure without the narrowing is a regression, not a fix. The general conditional-totality proof-engine work remains Phase 5 — only the minimum needed to avoid mass rejection lands here.
  - **Guard-position obligation context (HARD, same slice):** an obligation raised *inside* a guard expression needs a position-aware `ObligationContext` — specifically, a new guard-slot context that knows left-to-right conjunct order — rather than a reused body context. The failure mode if a body context were reused: the guard-in-path discharge strategy would fetch the very guard the obligation came from and discharge it without regard to conjunct order. That would make the **reversed** idiom `when X > 5 and X is set` wrongly self-discharge, even though under short-circuit evaluation its left conjunct executes unprotected. The extension point is the flat `ObligationContext` discriminated union at `src/Precept/Pipeline/ProofLedger.cs:85-92`, which today has no guard-position context; add the guard-slot context there and make the bundled narrowing aware of left-to-right order. The BUG-031 exit criteria depend on getting this exactly right: the repro files must reject, while `when X is set and X > 5` must still compile clean.
- **Characterize the compat wave:** the total walk will produce a wave of new — and **correct** — compile errors across existing corpus files, because faults that were silently marked `Proved` now reject. Observe and inventory this wave by running `precept_compile` over the corpus. One framing caution: "the first inspection would crash under the eventual runtime" is the *motivation* for closing these holes, **not** an exit criterion — no runtime is invoked anywhere in this phase. The repair of the wave lands in Slice 2.4.
- **Exit criteria (compiler-completable, via `precept_compile`):**
  - The BUG-031 repros compile to a **rejection carrying a divisor obligation**. Concretely: `from Open on Go when X / Y > 0` and `rule Z > 0 when X / Y > 0`, where Y is assigned from an event argument and not proven non-zero, now emit the divisor diagnostic with an `Unresolved` obligation — matching the behavior the bare form `rule X / Y > 0` already has. Verified via `precept_compile`.
  - The BUG-032 repro emits **two** divisor-family obligations: `rule Items.count > 0 and Items.at(A / B) >= 0` produces the divisor obligation at the `.at(...)` argument position *in addition to* the index-bounds obligation (diagnostic PRE0100, the out-of-bounds-index check). Verified via `precept_compile`.
  - The canonical optional idiom `when X is set and X > 5` still **compiles clean** — the bundled narrowing discharges the presence obligation, so there is no mass false-reject. Verified via `precept_compile`.
  - `dotnet test` green, with new proof-engine unit tests pinning each of the 10 closed positions plus the narrowing arm.
  - *(No exit criterion asserts a runtime guard-evaluation or a runtime divide-by-zero — those are deferred runtime execution.)*
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` § Obligation Generation Contract — record that the walk is total over `ChildExpressionPositions`; that guard slots, because-holes, default interiors, and member-call arguments are all enrolled; and that intra-guard short-circuit narrowing exists as the minimum discharge power. `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` — the guard and member-argument positions now emit the divisor, presence, and index diagnostics they previously skipped. If the O-8 walk-versus-restrict fork from this slice's gate resolved as "restrict," `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` records the guard-shape restriction and that change re-routes to Phase 5. Flip **BUG-031 / BUG-032** to Fixed in the bug tracker, with commit references, at slice close.

### Slice 2.3 — 3-layer creation-exhaustiveness checker (the structural guarantee)

- **Work:** Build the build-time checker that makes a fail-open position **structurally impossible** from here on, rather than merely fixed once. What the checker structurally proves, per obligation family and per catalogued DU subtype X:
  `{declared child `TypedExpression` fields of subtype X} − {positions the walk recurses into} = ∅`
  In words: for every catalogued subtype, the set of child-expression fields it declares, minus the set of positions the walk actually recurses into, must be empty — so the build **fails** if any obligation family stops being created at any catalogued position. This checker belongs to the same analyzer family as the already-shipped catalog-exhaustiveness analyzers — `Precept0019PipelineCoverageExhaustiveness`, which checks coverage over `[HandlesCatalogExhaustively(typeof(T))]`, and `Precept0029ModifierAxisDisjoint`. Reuse the `[HandlesCatalogExhaustively]` / `DiagnosticCoverageScanner` machinery; do not fork a parallel mechanism. **Reuse caveat (verified against the source for buildability):** the named machinery is enum-keyed — `HandlesCatalogExhaustivelyAttribute` takes a catalog *enum* type, and `DiagnosticCoverageScanner` hardcodes the `DiagnosticCode` enum — whereas this checker's subject is keyed by DU subtype (child positions per sealed record subtype of `TypedExpression`). The executor therefore builds a DU-subtype-aware **sibling analyzer in the same family**: it extends the attribute-plus-analyzer pattern to DU base types (Roslyn can enumerate the sealed derived records in a compilation) and reuses the existing enumeration and walking machinery. That is a modest new sibling, not a literal drop-in reuse — but the do-not-fork intent stands unchanged. The "3 layers" of the checker: (1) every expression-bearing DU subtype is catalogued in `ChildExpressionPositions`; (2) every `WalkExpression` arm recurses into exactly its catalogued child positions — no hand-picked subset, no missing default arm; (3) every obligation family that a position can raise is enrolled at that position.
- **Close the 6 latent no-default-arm holes:** the systemic root cause — quoted from the BUG-032 analysis: "the hand-written `WalkExpression` switch recurses into a hand-picked subset of child positions per arm … No default arm / no exhaustiveness over child `TypedExpression` members" — leaves **6 additional positions** that compile today only because nothing has tripped them yet. Enumerate the whole family (per the standing project instruction: enumerate the whole family — every cell gets an explicit disposition), close each hole, and confirm the checker passes green over them. One known member of the family is **not** closed but is explicitly dispositioned: `TypedPostfixOp.Operand` is *deliberately* skipped, because recursing into it would generate a spurious presence obligation. It therefore receives a catalogued deliberate-skip disposition through the Slice-2.1 projection's declared-exclusion vocabulary, rather than a closure. Treat the count of 6 as an estimate; the enumerate-the-whole-family instruction absorbs any drift in that count.
- **Fold — ONE mechanism, not two (Frank finding A3, reuse-not-parallel):** an earlier, separate plan — the collection inner-type value-modifiers plan — left open a forward watch: a single enumerated coverage check over the whole family of paths by which an element can enter a collection, so that a future element-entry path cannot silently bypass the inner-type bound check. The adversarial reviewer pass on that collection plan's own Phase 1 named this coverage gap its strongest objection. The collection design carried the same requirement as its Falsifier 4: "every element via add/enqueue/push/insert/put/append/default-literal is covered against the inner-type bound". That watch folds into this **single** exhaustiveness checker as additional catalogued positions and families. Do **not** stand up a second tracker for it.
- **Conditional fold — omit×global-rule join:** fold the join between `omit` and global rules into the checker **only if** Phase 0 Slice 0.1(b) adjudicated it as **floor-shaped** — that is, a presence-lane obligation-creation join, which makes it a position the walk must cover. If it was instead adjudicated as flag-shaped — PRE0082 hook-guard coverage, belonging to the post-runtime flag layer of warning diagnostics — then it is out of scope here. In that case, record the reclassification explicitly; do not silently cover it and do not silently drop it.
- **Exit criteria (compiler-completable, via `dotnet build`):**
  - The checker runs at build time and **passes green** with all 10 walk positions from Slice 2.2 plus the 6 latent no-default-arm holes closed.
  - A deliberate regression test — a temporarily uncatalogued child position, or a `WalkExpression` arm with a recursion removed — makes `dotnet build` **fail** with the exhaustiveness diagnostic. The structural guarantee is thereby demonstrated, not just asserted.
  - The collection element-entry-path coverage is represented as catalogued positions and families inside the one checker, and no second tracker exists — verified by test.
  - `dotnet test` green.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` (a section on the creation-exhaustiveness property — the structural guarantee that obligation creation is position-total); `docs/language/catalog-system.md` (record the checker as the consumer that makes `ChildExpressionPositions` load-bearing); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (the build-time exhaustiveness diagnostic, in the `Precept00xx` analyzer family). If the omit-join folds in, note the floor-shaped disposition in `proof-engine.md`; if it does not, record the flag-shaped reclassification (PRE0082) wherever Phase 0 placed it.
