### Slice 2.4 — Corpus compat-wave repair (COMPILER_CODE; delegate to precept-author)

- **Work:** Repair the corpus broken by the position-totality wave from Slice 2.2 and the no-default-arm closures from Slice 2.3 — that is, the files whose previously-silently-`Proved` faults now correctly reject. This repair was an unbucketed item in the prior plan; here it is an explicit slice. Delegate all `.precept` authoring to the **precept-author** sub-agent: DSL work is never hand-authored in the main loop, and `.precept` files are interpreted by the runtime and validated via `precept_compile`, never via `dotnet build`. Because the project is pre-release with no users, the transient window in which the corpus is red is harmless (per D3, atomicity relaxed); fix fixtures as the waves surface rather than blocking the walk closure on a coordinated corpus rewrite.
- **Scope note:** this slice repairs the Phase-2 wave specifically. The band-reclassification wave (Phase 1) and the soundness false-accept waves (Phases 3 and 4) each have their own repair passes — do not pull those forward into this slice. Keep each repair attributable to its wave, so that any given fixture change traces back to the specific soundness fix that motivated it.
- **Exit criteria (via `precept_compile`):** every `samples/` and test-fixture `.precept` file either compiles clean again under the total walk, or its new diagnostic is confirmed **correct** — meaning the fixture had been relying on a false `Proved` — and the fixture is updated to the prevention-preserving form. The corpus is green under `precept_compile` with the Slice-2.2/2.3 machinery active. `dotnet test` is green, including any tests that compile corpus files.
- **Doc-touch:** none in the canonical docs — this is fixture repair. If a sample file was demonstrating a now-rejected pattern as if it were valid, update the sample's intent comment to the corrected idiom (reading a representative `samples/` file first, per the DSL-authoring rule). Record the wave inventory and the repairs in the PR body — that record is transient, not canon.

---

### Slice 2.5 — Runtime-precondition record (CANON_DESIGN, record-only)

- **Boundary classification:** this slice is **CANON_DESIGN** — documentation work in the canonical docs, no code. It captures, in the runtime canon, *why* the compiler-side checker (the compiler implementation of Slices 2.1–2.3) is a hard precondition of the deferred runtime. The checker itself is verifiable today. The InspectFire / Prospect-evaluation **coding** the checker protects is RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER — runtime coding deferred with a capture pointer — and is not part of this phase; today `Evaluator.InspectFire` throws `NotImplementedException`.
- **Work:** Record in the runtime canon that floor position-totality plus the creation-exhaustiveness checker together constitute a **HARD runtime precondition**, on two grounds:
  1. **InspectFire has no fault lane.** The per-keystroke `InspectFire` path evaluates every guard slot and produces a `Prospect` — the inspection verdict type with the three members Certain / Possible / Impossible — which has **no `Faulted` member**. A fail-open obligation position, meaning an un-walked guard that can fault, would therefore crash the live inspector at evaluation time, because there is no verdict for it to return. The Kleene/Prospect evaluation must not throw on an unresolvable subexpression. The only way to guarantee that is for the compiler to have *already* proven every guard-position fault either absent or rejected — which is exactly what the totality walk plus the checker deliver.
  2. **The evaluator contract survives language growth only via the checker.** Section §10 of the evaluator canon states the contract that "no errors ⇒ evaluator never faults" — a clean compile implies the evaluator can never fault. The creation-exhaustiveness checker is what makes that contract hold structurally as the language grows: without it, a newly-added child-expression position would silently re-open a fault lane.
- **Honesty caveat (D1 — overflow parked):** `evaluator.md §10`'s claim that "no diagnostics means no evaluator faults" is now **false for the parked representability family** — under owner decision D1, decimal and temporal representability overflow is a documented known limitation that is never claimed prevented. Amend §10 to **except** the parked representability fault: the checker closes every **other** fault lane, so the contract holds for everything *except* the parked family. This is the honesty-about-approximation carve-out — surfaced explicitly, not auto-resolved into a "prevented" claim.
- **Prove-or-reject (permanent):** this slice records a precondition only; it admits nothing. Where a guard fault cannot be proven absent, the totality walk of Slice 2.2 **rejects** — and that rejection is permanent prove-or-reject, not a hold pending the runtime. A fully-built runtime could never rescue an un-walked faulting guard, because the InspectFire path has no fault lane to receive it. This is genuine fault-unprovability, not a runtime-absence hold.
- **Exit criteria (CANON_DESIGN — no code, no runtime execution):** the runtime canon states the position-totality-plus-exhaustiveness-checker precondition with both of its grounds; §10's contract is honestly narrowed to except the parked representability family; and the two facts that make the precondition hard — InspectFire has no fault lane, and `Prospect` has no `Faulted` member — are recorded as the reason. The capture is cross-referenced from the proof-engine doc, so the compiler-side guarantee and the runtime-side dependency point at each other. The whole capture is reviewable as a doc diff, so it is post-hoc verifiable.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/runtime/evaluator.md §10` (the no-errors-⇒-no-faults contract narrowed to except parked representability; position-totality named as its compile-time precondition); `docs/runtime/evaluator.md §7.2` (InspectFire has no fault lane — totality is required so the per-keystroke path cannot crash); `docs/runtime/result-types.md` (`Prospect` Certain/Possible/Impossible has no `Faulted` member; Kleene evaluation must not throw on an unresolvable subexpression). Cross-reference from `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` (the creation-exhaustiveness property is the runtime's §10 precondition).

---

**Phase 2 exit:** `ChildExpressionPositions` is catalogued (2.1). All 10 fail-open positions are closed, with the intra-guard narrowing bundled in so the optional idiom `when X is set and X > 5` does not mass-false-reject (2.2). The 3-layer creation-exhaustiveness checker gates `dotnet build` green, and a deliberate regression makes it red (2.3). The corpus is repaired and green under `precept_compile` (2.4). The InspectFire/§10 runtime precondition and the D1 honesty carve-out are captured in the runtime canon (2.5). BUG-031 and BUG-032 are Fixed, verified via `precept_compile`. **No runtime dependency, no runtime-execution exit criterion.** The single HARD ordering — Phase 1 Slice 1.4's honest registry lands before this phase's checker first reads it — is satisfied.

## 6. Phase 3 — False-Proved repairs + write-aware tier-2 discharge (intra-chain only)

**Nature:** COMPILER_CODE + CANON_DESIGN

**Gating on:** Two earlier phases must land before this one, for ordinary dependency reasons. First, Phase 1 must have produced the honest registry, because the false-Proved repairs in this phase route their diagnostics through fault codes that must already be truthful. Second, Phase 2 must have made obligation positions total, because repairing a discharge path is only sound once the obligation that path discharges is actually *created* at every position — fixing how an obligation is proved is meaningless if the obligation was never collected in the first place. Neither of these is the plan's one hard ordering; that hard ordering is registry-honest-before-the-Phase-2-checker, per D3 (atomicity relaxed). These two are dependency-natural sequencing, not atomic-merge couplings.

**Effort:** L (8–12 days). D1 (overflow parked) removes the single largest item that a previous version of this phase carried — the representability-overflow obligation family. As a result this phase is materially lighter than the pre-decision estimate, and it carries no hidden risk of a representability build sneaking back in. This lightening was confirmed by finding A7 of Frank — the adversarial review of the prior version of this plan, whose accepted findings owner decision D4 folds in throughout; in that review's numbering, A-numbered findings are advisory (non-blocking).

**Goal:** Close every compile-time false-`Proved` hole. A false-Proved hole is any site where the proof ledger reports `Proved` (or `Proved`-by-`Literal`) over a fault that has not actually been ruled out. In the same pass, add the missing obligation families that are *not* parked, so that the fault floor becomes sound at compile time. The governing posture throughout is the prevention-engine contract: **where the compiler cannot prove a fault absent, it rejects** — an unprovable case is rejected, never skipped, and never falsely discharged.

This phase contains only the intra-chain (same-transaction) half of write-aware tier-2 discharge — that is, making a standing declared fact stop discharging an obligation once a write the compiler can see within the same event's action chain has invalidated it. The committed-state (cross-event/cross-transaction) half — discharging proofs against values persisted by earlier, separate operations — is sound only once the runtime enforces the discharging constraints at every prior commit. That half is therefore classified RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER, with its design captured in `evaluator.md`. No exit criterion in this phase asserts a runtime-execution outcome.

**Boundary discipline carried into every slice:**

- **Compiler-completable exit criteria.** Each slice exits at a claim the compiler alone can demonstrate: "the compiler rejects the unprovable case," "the compiler emits the honest diagnostic," or "the ledger no longer reports a false `Proved`." Each such claim is verifiable via `precept_compile` plus `dotnet test`. No slice exits at "governance refuses the committed-state write" — that would be a runtime-execution claim, which this plan cannot verify.
- **Genuine prove-or-reject versus admit-now / defer-enforcement.** The two postures apply to different situations, and every slice must apply the right one. Where a fault is genuinely unprovable-safe — the compiler cannot prove the math or operation safe, as with an intra-chain stale discharge — the compiler **rejects**, and that rejection is permanent prevention behavior no future runtime revisits. Where a repair is fault-safe once the runtime performs it, the compiler admits the compile-side half now and defers the enforcement to the named §11 (Runtime-Coding Handoff) capture pointer. Two Phase-3 items fit that description. One is committed-state discharge enforcement. The other is BUG-030, the UCUM cross-unit cancellation bug, which splits into two halves: Slice 3.3 builds its compile-side half, and its runtime value-application half is the deferred piece. That is admit-now / defer-enforcement, the same treatment the bands received under D2 (band split). The compiler does **not** keep rejecting merely because the runtime is unbuilt: pre-release nothing executes, so no live fault exists, per D3.
- **PARKED, do not re-add (D1):** three items are deliberately not built in this phase, no matter how naturally they would fit. They are the representability-overflow obligation family (covering decimal **and** temporal overflow), the maxplaces compile-time precision proof, and `FaultCode.TemporalOverflow`. Each is a documented known limitation, reconciled in Phase 1 Slice 1.5.

---

### Slice 3.1 — Write-aware tier-2 discharge (SAME-TRANSACTION / intra-chain ONLY)

**Work.** Add the write-awareness rule to tier-2 discharge. The rule: a standing constraint — a modifier fact or a declared rule fact — may discharge a fault obligation **only if** no same-transaction prefix write to the constrained field is unaccounted for. In other words, if an earlier action in the same event chain wrote to the field the constraint speaks about, the constraint cannot silently keep discharging as though the write never happened.

This is the repair mandated by GATE-B, the Phase-0 owner decision on which shape write-aware discharge takes. GATE-B has two candidate shapes: (i) preservation obligations attached to every write into a proof-carrying constraint, or (ii) staleness-invalidation of the declaration or rule fact, followed by forward re-establishment of the fact from the written expression's interval. The slice builds whichever shape the GATE-B owner outcome selects. The plan's grounding favors shape (ii), for two reasons: the proof engine's existing collection handling — the count-fact staleness machinery itemized in the reuse list below — already implements exactly that staleness discipline, so (ii) reuses a proven mechanism; and shape (i) would re-aim the PRE0078 band mechanism that Phase 1 reclassifies under D2, which makes (i) the weaker fit after that decision.

Concretely, the slice reuses the **existing sequential-staleness machinery** rather than forking a parallel mechanism. This is the verify-reuse discipline — new work must reuse the existing catalog and code, with the reuse verified against the actual source at file-and-line level rather than assumed — and Frank's advisory finding A3 confirmed the reuse claim for this slice:

- The existing machinery consists of `ProofObligation.ReassignedBefore` / `CountEstablishedBefore` / `CountInvalidatedBefore` (`ProofLedger.cs:51/78`). These flags are stamped by the `WalkActions` prefix-effect pass, and they are already consulted by the guard-consuming strategies (`ProofEngine.Strategies.cs:423/678/771/1105`, `ProofEngine.QualifierNarrowing.cs:142`). The classification of writes — a full replacement (`set`/`clear`) maps to `ReassignedBefore`, while grow/shrink operations map to `CountEstablishedBefore`/`CountInvalidatedBefore` — is catalog-driven via `ActionMeta.Effect` (`ActionEffectClass`). The slice extends that **same** classification path; it never switches on `ActionKind` identity, which would violate the catalog rules.
- **Corrected reader list (buildability-verified at HEAD).** The gate targets named in an earlier version of this plan — the modifier-fact and rule-fact arms of `TryFlowNarrowingProof`, and Strategy 4's rule-sourced relations — turn out to be **already gated** at HEAD. Specifically: `TryFlowNarrowingProof` *is* Strategy 4, and its `ReassignedBefore` gate (`ProofEngine.Strategies.cs:1105-1106`) executes before the branch-source selection that includes rule-sourced relations. That gate landed in commit `7ebbb68c` and is recorded at `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md:1425`. The tier-2 standing-fact readers that are genuinely un-gated — each of which must consult `ReassignedBefore` for the constrained field before admitting the standing fact as a live discharge carrier — are three sites:
  1. **`TryDeclarationAttributeProof`** (Strategy 2 — the proof engine's Declaration Attribute Proof strategy, which discharges obligations from a declaration's modifier and attribute metadata) — its modifier walk returns true on `SatisfactionCovers` with no staleness consult at all.
  2. **`ApplyTrustedRuleFacts`** (`ProofEngine.Composition.cs`) — the un-gated discharge path this slice's own exit fixture flows through; it was empirically confirmed falsely-`Proved` via `precept_compile` at HEAD.
  3. **`BuildNarrowedIntervals`** (`ProofEngine.Intervals.cs`) — un-gated rule-sourced relational facts and guard branches; its single caller passes the obligation, so the gate can be applied there.

  One plumbing note: `ResolveNumericSignSet` does not receive the obligation, so `ReassignedBefore` must either be threaded past it, or the facts must be pre-filtered per obligation before they reach it. The pre-filter option is viable because the per-obligation loop already has the obligation in hand and `ScopedNumericFact` carries a `Subject`. The mechanism is unchanged throughout — a stale fact must not discharge after a same-transaction write. Only the reader list is corrected relative to the earlier plan text; the exit criteria below are unchanged.

**Scope boundary.** This slice's *genuine compiler work* is intra-chain (same-transaction) write-awareness only. The compiler can SEE same-transaction prefix writes, so a stale intra-chain discharge is real fault-unprovability, and the compiler **rejects** it — this is permanent prove-or-reject that no future runtime rescues (the exit criteria below verify it). The committed-state (cross-event/cross-transaction) discharge — proofs discharged against values persisted by earlier, separate operations — is a different shape entirely: it becomes sound **once** the runtime enforces the discharging constraints on every prior commit. The runtime code in question is `Evaluator.cs` Fire/Update, which is currently `NotImplementedException`. The committed-state case's soundness is therefore gated on a runtime *capability*, not on a property the compiler can never prove. So the compiler **admits** the committed-state discharge now, relying on that captured prior-commit enforcement obligation, and the **enforcement defers** to §11 (Runtime-Coding Handoff), with the design captured at `evaluator.md §7.6 + §10`. This is admit-now / defer-enforcement, identical to the band treatment under D2. The compiler does **not** reject committed-state discharges merely because the runtime is unbuilt: pre-release nothing executes, so admitting them creates no live fault (D3), and the prior-commit invariant is the runtime's obligation, not grounds for compile-time over-rejection. No exit criterion asserts a runtime-execution outcome.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable).**

- A same-transaction prefix write that invalidates a standing modifier or rule fact causes the dependent fault obligation to stop discharging from that fact — the compiler **rejects** the now-unprovable case. Verified via `precept_compile` on a fixture chain; for example, a chain of the form `rule X != 0 … set X = <unproven> … 100 / X` rejects. `dotnet test` is green for the new discharge-staleness unit tests in the proof-engine suite.
- A chain with **no** intervening write still discharges from the standing fact. This guards against regression: the canonical idiom must not start falsely rejecting.
- No exit criterion references committed-state discharge or runtime governance.

**Gate.** The GATE-B shape decision (CANON_DESIGN) is settled in Phase 0, surfaced via the Pre-Design Owner Consultation gate because it is new obligation-discharge surface. If GATE-B is unresolved when the slice starts, the conservative default is shape (ii) — the reuse path.

**Doc-touch.** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` records the write-aware discharge rule and the chosen GATE-B shape, in the *Sequential proof flow* and *Strategy 4 rule-sourced relations* sections — extending the existing sections, not adding a parallel mechanism. **Runtime-canon capture (RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER):** `docs/runtime/evaluator.md §7.6 + §10` records the committed-state invariant the compiler's intra-chain repair leans on — "any constraint a compile-time proof discharges against committed state is enforced by the runtime sweep on every prior commit" — and `proof-engine.md` records that the compile-time committed-state discharge carries a **runtime-enforcement precondition**: the discharge is admitted compiler-side now, and the prior-commit enforcement is the deferred half.

---

### Slice 3.2 — Qualifier-source mutation obligation

**Work.** Add a new compile-time obligation on **every mutation of a field referenced as a qualifier source**: the compiler must prove that the values whose qualifiers depend on that field are re-established after the mutation, or it must **reject**. This closes the silently-wrong-money false-`Proved` that is live today in `samples/fee-schedule.precept` — in that sample, a qualifier source is mutated, the dependent values are never re-derived, and yet the proof ledger reports `Proved`.

The obligation **reuses the existing obligation-creation and discharge machinery**, and the same staleness-invalidation family as the already-shipped BUG-014/BUG-015 reassignment passes — concretely, `ReassignedBefore` over the qualifier-source field, consulted by `ProofEngine.QualifierNarrowing.cs:141-142`. The new work is a thin wiring layer over the established sequential-staleness discipline, not a forked obligation engine.

The diagnostic is **label-agnostic**. GATE-C — the Phase-0 owner decision on this diagnostic's taxonomy — settles only the *FaultCode identity*: whether the emitted code is `QualifierMismatch` or a named third category for type-identity violations. The obligation's creation, discharge, and reject behavior is identical under either label, so the build proceeds regardless of how GATE-C resolves; only the emitted code's identity follows the label.

*Single finding — this slice collapses what four separate input lists each counted once into one obligation. The four prior entries, named here by the row keys those lists used, are: P3, the prior plan's Phase-3 item list; FG-05, the floor-gap register (the fault-floor-definition register that the Coverage Ledger reconciles as its §A rows); FF, the fault-floor research doc's findings re-pass — a re-reading of the same findings performed as part of revising that doc, not a second set of work items; and P4-BIZ-26, the Phase-4 business-domain audit's finding list. The Phase-4 business-domain audit cross-references this slice; the obligation is **not** rebuilt there.*

**Pre-design caveat.** This introduces **new obligation surface**, so it routes through `/design` with owner consultation (the Pre-Design Owner Consultation gate) before building. The owner-gated piece is the taxonomy label (GATE-C). The behavior itself — build the obligation, reject the unprovable mutation — is already settled.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable).**

- The qualifier-source mutation in `samples/fee-schedule.precept` no longer compiles to a false `Proved`. A mutation of the qualifier source whose dependents are not re-established **rejects** — it emits the obligation's diagnostic — verified via `precept_compile`.
- A mutation that *does* re-establish the dependents discharges the obligation and compiles clean. This guards against the false-reject direction.
- `dotnet test` is green for the new qualifier-source-mutation obligation unit tests.

**Gate.** GATE-C (label only, CANON_DESIGN), plus `/design` for the new obligation surface.

**Doc-touch.** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` (the qualifier-source mutation obligation — its creation position and its staleness-discharge); `docs/language/business-domain-types.md` (a qualifier-source mutation soundness note); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (the emitted code under whichever label GATE-C selects).

---

### Slice 3.3 — Remaining false-`Proved` repairs (one row each — no double-count)

Each item below is a single deduplicated finding. Each is built once here; anywhere else it appears, it is cross-referenced, not rebuilt.

- **BUG-030 — UCUM cross-unit cancellation (COMPILE half only).** In the proof engine's interval and cancellation arithmetic, apply the catalog's UCUM conversion factor — for example, kg→g is ×1000 — so that a `DimensionalProduct` or `QualifierCompatibility` discharge stops admitting magnitudes that fall outside the *correctly-scaled* proven interval. Today the discharge treats differently-scaled units as if they were the same scale, which lets out-of-interval magnitudes through. The Strategy architecture is unchanged — only the cancellation factor enters the interval math. **Exit:** an interval whose bounds require the conversion factor now rejects a value that is outside the scaled interval; verified via `precept_compile`. *(The runtime VALUE-application — actually computing `4.00 USD/ft × 36 in → 12.00` at execution time — is RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER; see the capture pointer in this slice's doc-touch.)*

- **Presence-wrapping contradiction.** Fix the discharge path so that a presence obligation wrapped in a contradictory guard stays **`Unresolved` (reject)** instead of being falsely marked `Proved`. **Exit:** the wrapped-presence fixture rejects via `precept_compile`, and the proof-ledger unit test shows the obligation's disposition as `Unresolved`, not `Proved`.

- **Mincount-at-birth false-`Proved` seed.** Fix or retire the seed fact so the ledger does not report `Proved` at Create for a `mincount`-bounded field that is empty at birth — an empty collection cannot satisfy a minimum count, so the `Proved` entry is false. Mincount **retains** its role in discharging `count > 0` obligations — only the at-birth seed is corrected. Two proof-engine labels attach to that discharge role, and they name different mechanisms. Strategy 2 is the declaration-attribute proof strategy, which discharges an obligation from the subject's declared modifiers — and a declared `mincount` is such a modifier. `CollectionGrowth` is a separate proof-strategy tag, documented in `proof-engine.md` *Sequential proof flow*, for the companion discharge in which a prior grow action in the same chain establishes `count > 0`. Additionally, GATE-E — the Phase-0 owner decision on the mincount-at-birth posture — carries among its candidate outcomes a defined every-Create-fails refusal paired with a compile-time flag. If GATE-E chose that refusal-plus-flag posture, emit the **every-Create-fails definition-incoherence flag** for the case where every possible Create fails. **Exit:** the at-birth case no longer shows a false `Proved` in the ledger, and the every-Create-fails flag emits (if GATE-E chose the refusal-plus-flag posture); verified via `precept_compile`. *(The runtime Create-time **refusal** is RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER — captured at `evaluator.md §7.1 Create`. Only the compile-side ledger honesty and the definition-incoherence flag land here.)*

- **FunctionArg computed-arg totality sweep.** Working from `FunctionMeta`, verify that **every** computability-constrained parameter carries a live `ParamSubject` `NumericProofRequirement` obligation at **every** call position — for example, the pow exponent must be ≥0 and the sqrt operand must be ≥0 (`Functions.cs:206`, `PSqrtNumber`). This sweep intersects BUG-032's member-call-argument walk from Phase 2 Slice 2.2: the totality sweep verifies that the obligation is *created* at each catalogued argument position the walk now recurses into. The sweep also pins a catalog convention: `ParameterMeta` constraints stay **computability-only**. The reason: a discretionary band placed on a parameter would smuggle a rule into the fault floor, the prove-or-reject fault category. **Exit:** a call that supplies an unprovable computability-constrained argument at any position **rejects**; a missing-obligation hole at any call position fails the proof-engine totality test; verified via `precept_compile` plus `dotnet test`. *(Single row — collapses what three input lists each counted: the prior plan's Phase-3 item list (its P3 entry), the floor-gap register (row FG-10), and the fault-floor research doc's findings re-pass (its FF entry) — the same three lists identified in Slice 3.2's dedup note.)*

- **Choice-default membership.** A statically-decidable closed-domain violation at a field or event-argument **default** position is routed to the **TYPE CHECKER**, not to a proof obligation. The routing matters: making it a proof obligation would break the invariant pinned by the two test fixtures keyed C59 and C86 — the invariant that "Create from defaults always succeeds," which lives at `BuildDefaultEnvironment`, `ProofEngine.Analysis.cs:110`. **Exit:** a default value outside its choice domain emits a **type-checker** diagnostic, not a proof-engine fault, verified via `precept_compile`; the C59/C86 "Create from defaults succeeds" fixtures stay green. *(Single row — collapses what four input lists each counted: the prior plan's Phase-3 item list (its P3 entry), the Phase-4 primitive-surface audit's finding list (P4-PRIM-11), the floor-gap register (row FG-12), and the fault-floor research doc's findings re-pass (its FF entry). The Phase-4 choice work cross-references this; it does not rebuild it.)*

- **BUG-021 — min-only containment (re-filed from Phase 1).** Re-verify what survives the band scale-back under D2. With overflow parked under D1, any min-only residue whose only justification was "bounds feeding representability proofs" **dissolves** — the representability proofs it would have fed no longer exist. What remains is the genuinely proof-carrying residue: the `mincount` `count > 0` Strategy-2 arm, which belongs to the same family as BUG-017/018/019 — the "obligation never *created*" family (`bugs.md:203/210`) — and that residue stays prove-or-reject. **Exit:** the dissolved residue compiles clean, meaning no false-reject of a band that D2 reclassified; the genuinely proof-carrying min-only case still rejects when unprovable; verified via `precept_compile`.

- **BUG-028 / BUG-029 — read `bugs.md` before classifying.** Do not classify either bug from memory or from this plan's summary; the entries are verified live at `bugs.md:77/92`, and the builder must read them first.
  - **BUG-028** is default-satisfiability fold completeness — the construction handler is coarse and compound expressions short-circuit, so the fold under-emits. Under-emit is the **safe** direction here — a missed emission never introduces a false `Proved`. The bug is compiler-side, and it must be **re-scoped when the computed-fold lands**, because the Phase-5 computed-field default fold is its prerequisite. **Exit:** the under-emit fixtures are characterized; no false `Proved` is introduced (under-emit remains the safe direction); the re-scope note is recorded.
  - **BUG-029** is a message-quality bug: satisfiability diagnostics render the raw `TypedExpression` AST in user-facing messages. The fix is in `DescribeExpression` message rendering. It is compiler-side with no runtime dependency. **Exit:** the rendered message reads as source text, not raw AST, verified via `precept_compile`.
  - Both are compiler-side in the absence of a runtime dependency. **If either surfaces a runtime-enforcement dependency during the work, split out a capture obligation** — name the `docs/runtime/*.md` pointer — rather than silently bundling the runtime half into the compiler fix.

**Gate.** None beyond Phase 0's GATE-E (the mincount-at-birth posture decision) and the Phase-1 honest registry that these repairs route their diagnostics through.

**Doc-touch.** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` (the BUG-030 cancellation factor in interval math; the presence-wrapping discharge fix; the mincount-at-birth seed correction; the FunctionArg per-`FunctionMeta` totality convention, including the computability-only `ParameterMeta` rule); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (the choice-default routing to the type checker; the BUG-029 message rendering); `docs/language/business-domain-types.md` (the BUG-030 cross-unit record against that doc's Decision D8). **Runtime-canon capture (RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER):** the cross-unit reduction-rule section of `docs/runtime/evaluator.md` holds the BUG-030 **value-application** — the conversion-factor formula `k = ScaleToBase(u_q)/ScaleToBase(u_p)`, in which `u_q` is the quantity's unit and `u_p` is the price's unit; the conversion is target-directed, meaning it converts toward the reduction's target unit, the price's denominator unit; rounding is at maxplaces. This capture is the `evaluator.md` section — not yet written — that specifies how the runtime computes a value when a price's unit differs from the quantity's unit; e.g. 4.00 USD per foot times 36 inches yields 12.00. It is the highest-priority Phase-4 doc extraction, tracked under the cross-reference key MO-DSO-05 — the identifier Phase 4 uses for this same cross-unit reduction rule. Separately, `docs/runtime/evaluator.md §7.1 Create` holds the mincount Create-time **refusal** semantics (per GATE-E).

### Slice 3.4 — Min-bound discharge vacuity (GATE-G outcome)

**Work.** The work branches on how GATE-G — the Phase-0 owner decision on whether min-bound discharges are made resolvable in the engine or narrowed in the spec — resolves:

- **If (i) in-engine:** make `DeclarationValue` satisfactions **resolvable** in the proof engine, so that a min-bound discharge stops being vacuously `Proved` — today the satisfaction cannot be evaluated, so the discharge succeeds by vacuity rather than by proof. This is COMPILER_CODE. It composes with the relational single-pass narrowing that has already shipped (Strategy 4; `proof-engine.md` *Single-pass, depth-1 composition*).
- **If (ii) spec-narrowing:** nothing builds in this slice — the discharge-tier amendment to `precept-language-spec.md` §0.7 was **already folded into Phase 1 Slice 1.5** (CANON_DESIGN). The slice's only job under this branch is to record the no-build disposition explicitly, so the register row is visibly placed rather than silently empty.

With overflow parked under D1, the write-awareness consequence of making bounds proof-carrying is **lighter** than it once appeared: no representability obligation exists to consume the now-resolvable bounds, so option (i) does not reinstate a representability build. This is the reconciliation of GATE-G's framing — the strongest motivation for option (i) evaporates once overflow is parked, which makes the spec-narrowing path (ii) the cleaner default.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable).**

- **Under (i):** a min-bound discharge that was vacuously `Proved` now resolves honestly — it discharges when the `DeclarationValue` satisfaction actually holds, and it **rejects** when it does not; verified via `precept_compile` plus `dotnet test`.
- **Under (ii):** the no-build disposition is recorded, and the Phase-1 spec amendment is confirmed present — so no Phase-3 work is left orphaned.

**Gate.** GATE-G — a CANON_DESIGN decision with a conditional COMPILER_CODE tail. It is a single register entry, resolved in Phase 0.

**Doc-touch.** Under **(i):** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` (the `DeclarationValue` resolvability in the discharge path). Under **(ii):** none here — the doc work was folded into Phase 1 Slice 1.5's amendment of `precept-language-spec.md` §0.7.

---

### Phase 3 coverage reconciliation (every input register row placed)

This section places every Phase-3 input register row into a slice, a deferral with a capture pointer, or a park with a reason — so a reader can verify nothing fell through.

- **Floor gaps:** row (2), same-transaction write-unaware tier-2 discharge → Slice 3.1 (the committed-state half is RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER, captured at `evaluator.md §7.6/§10`); row (5), qualifier-source mutation → Slice 3.2; row (6), BUG-030 UCUM cancellation → Slice 3.3 compile half (the value-application is deferred); row (7), presence-wrapping contradiction → Slice 3.3; row (8), mincount-at-birth ledger fix → Slice 3.3 (the Create-time refusal is deferred); row (10), FunctionArg computed-arg totality → Slice 3.3; row (12), choice-default membership → Slice 3.3 (owned by the type checker); row (14), min-bound vacuity → Slice 3.4 (or already handled in Phase 1 Slice 1.5, if GATE-G resolves as (ii)).
- **Open bugs:** BUG-021 → Slice 3.3; BUG-028 and BUG-029 → Slice 3.3 (with the read-`bugs.md`-first discipline); the BUG-031/BUG-032 obligation-creation positions belong to Phase 2 — this phase repairs the *discharge* paths that run over those positions, not the creation walk itself.
- **Open decisions:** GATE-B (write-aware discharge shape) → Slice 3.1; GATE-C (diagnostic label only) → Slice 3.2; GATE-E (mincount-at-birth posture) → Slice 3.3; GATE-G (min-bound vacuity resolution) → Slice 3.4.
- **PARKED, not built here (D1):** the representability-overflow obligation family (decimal plus temporal); the maxplaces compile-time precision proof; `FaultCode.TemporalOverflow`. Each is documented as a known limitation in Phase 1 Slice 1.5, and the probe evidence — the observed temporal NodaTime behavior — feeds that honesty documentation.
- **RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER with capture pointers:** committed-state tier-2 discharge enforcement (`evaluator.md §7.6/§10`); the BUG-030 cross-unit value-application (the `evaluator.md` cross-unit reduction-rule section, cross-reference key MO-DSO-05 — the capture obligation, not yet written, specifying how the runtime computes a value when a price's unit differs from the quantity's unit, e.g. 4.00 USD per foot times 36 inches yields 12.00); mincount Create-time refusal (`evaluator.md §7.1 Create`).

*Nothing dropped silently: every Phase-3 input row is placed in a slice, deferred with a capture pointer, or parked with a reason above.*

**Phase 3 exit:** All listed false-`Proved` holes are closed — the compiler **rejects** each unprovable case at compile time, verified via `precept_compile` plus `dotnet test`. Intra-chain write-awareness has shipped, reusing the sequential-staleness machinery rather than forking a parallel one; this is the genuine prove-or-reject half, and a stale intra-chain discharge **rejects**. The committed-state discharge is **admitted** compiler-side, with its prior-commit **enforcement deferred** and captured at `evaluator.md §7.6 + §10` — admit-now / defer-enforcement, the same treatment the bands received, with no over-rejection held against the unbuilt runtime. The qualifier-source mutation false-`Proved` is closed. The representability-overflow, temporal-overflow, and maxplaces obligations are **not built** — parked and documented, per D1. Every slice rejects only what it cannot prove fault-safe; every admitted-but-unenforced obligation defers to §11 with a capture pointer; and no admission creates a live fault pre-release, per D3.

## 7. Phase 4 — Datatype-surface spec completeness (primitive / temporal / business)

**Goal:** take the datatype/literal surface — lexer literals, primitive and choice types, temporal types, business-domain types — to **100% of the canonical spec**, building on the right-sized, position-total, sound floor that Phases 1–3 deliver. This phase is the **67-gap datatype block** of the 236-gap spec-coverage audit. The 67 gaps break down as: Lexer 6, Primitives/choice 22, Business 28, Temporal 11.

The work is overwhelmingly independent fixes — type-checker, parser, literal-resolution, diagnostic-message, and proof-discharge fixes — against a spec that is already **locked**. A small number of findings are not simple fixes because the canon contradicts itself, and those carry an owner-reconciliation gate. The gates in play across this phase are: GATE-J, the Phase-0 owner gate over the canon's internal contradictions; GATE-K, the Phase-0 owner gate over the log-unit cross-unit policy, which applies at Slice 4.10; Decision 17, the locked temporal-canon decision that eliminated ISO literal forms; and the exchangerate doc-drift the owner has already ruled on. Four contradiction items exist. First, the sqrt §3.2-vs-§3.7 contradiction — covered by GATE-J, whose ruling this phase applies at Slice 4.6. Second, the choice field-vs-field canon contradiction, the other item on GATE-J's roster: the canon holds two conflicting readings; the owner picks the canonical reading, and the comparison that today compiles clean — a soundness-grade false-clean — becomes a rejection once the reading is set. Its corpus breakage rides the compat wave, and the phase exit verifies the rejection. Third, the ISO period/duration contradiction, which touches Decision 17 and is reconciled at Slice 4.6. Fourth, the exchangerate doc-drift the owner has already ruled on. Several findings also have **derived-value or conversion tails** — the part of the feature where a runtime would actually compute a value — and the stubbed runtime cannot execute those. Those tails are captured in `docs/runtime/evaluator.md` and deferred as admit-now / defer-enforcement: the compiler admits the fault-safe operation, and its enforcement defers with a capture pointer. The **type-derivation halves are compiler-pure and ship in this phase**.

**Boundary classification:** **COMPILER_CODE + CANON_DESIGN.** Every exit criterion below is verifiable via `precept_compile` / `dotnet build` with the runtime stubbed. Where a finding's *value computation* requires the evaluator, that tail is **RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER** with a named `evaluator.md` capture pointer. In each such compiler/runtime pair, the compiler's job is to **derive the type and admit the fault-safe operation**, deferring the value/conversion enforcement to §11 (Runtime-Coding Handoff). Genuinely unprovable-safe operations, by contrast, are rejected — that is permanent prove-or-reject behavior per §0 discipline 3. **PARKED items are not built here** — decimal/temporal representability overflow, the maxplaces compile-time precision proof, and the number-model redesign are documented as known limitations only. (The number model is the choice of how Precept represents numeric values; its redesign is parked under D1, the overflow-parking decision, and the maxplaces precision proof belongs to the same number-model class of parked work.)

**Dependencies:**

- **Phase 3 proof machinery (sequence Phase 4 after it — a strong ordering preference, though not a hard ordering; the plan's one hard ordering remains the honest registry landing before the creation-exhaustiveness checker):** Phase 3 delivers the qualifier-source mutation obligation, the compile half of BUG-030 (the UCUM cancellation-factor bug, where a price in one unit multiplied by a quantity in a commensurable different unit needs a conversion factor), choice-default membership routed to the type checker, the FunctionArg computed-arg totality sweep, and write-aware tier-2 discharge. Phase 4's business-domain discharge fixes — the operator-path fix for PRE0137, the counting-unit mismatch diagnostic; money×exchangerate commutativity; and price×duration cancellation — build **on** that Phase-3 discharge surface and must not re-fork it (the reuse-not-parallel rule). Several Phase-3 rows (choice-default, the BUG-030 compile half, the pow-exponent misroute) are **cross-referenced by Phase 4, not rebuilt** here.
- **Phase 1 Slice 1.4 (coordination, not a hard ordering):** the **pow-exponent ≥0 misroute** fix — where a negative-exponent violation is wrongly emitted under the square-root diagnostic — lands **once**, inside the registry repartition that Phase 1 Slice 1.4 performs. Phase 4's choice/primitive message work must **not** re-touch that routing. Coordinate the two phases so the fix is not duplicated.
- **Phase 0 GATE-J / GATE-K (owner reconciliation):** GATE-J covers the sqrt §3.2/§3.7 reading; the roster also includes the choice field-vs-field canon contradiction and the log-unit cross-unit policy (GATE-K), plus the ISO period/duration question (Decision 17, a Tier-3 owner conversation because it touches a locked decision). These are **CANON_DESIGN gates, and they block only the contested-reading slices** (4.6 and 4.10); the bulk of Phase 4 proceeds without them.
- **Phase 2 corpus repair absorbs Phase-4 breakage:** the soundness false-accept fixes in this phase — cross-counting-unit, choice field-vs-field, exponent-value — turn previously-clean corpus files into correct rejections. That breakage is part of the compat wave, and its fixture repair is delegated to the precept-author sub-agent under Phase 2 Slice 2.4.
- **Owner forks resolved at Phase-4 start.** Phase-4 start is the scheduled venue for the rows where the owner must pick a direction, so that no slice hits an unresolved fork mid-build. The forks are: the D16 function-table fork (D16 is a locked decision in `business-domain-types.md` granting certain math functions on business types; the fork is add-the-overloads versus strike-the-D16-row, and note the answer changes how much compiler code Slice 4.8 contains); unitofmeasure atomic-only; angle-unit dimension identity; bare-number bounds on magnitude types; and identity-type notempty. Each is settled in an owner conversation **at phase start, before the owning slice (4.8 or 4.10) begins** — not encountered mid-slice. Exit criteria still require a recorded ruling before anything lands.

**Effort class:** **L (12–18 days, parallelizable).** The lexer, primitive, temporal, and business lanes touch largely disjoint type-checker regions and can run concurrently once the Phase-3 discharge surface is stable. Beyond that Phase-3 stability point, the single serializing constraint is the pow-exponent fix landing in Phase 1.

> **No-double-count discipline.** Findings that also appear in the Phase-3 register — choice-default membership (routed to the type checker), the BUG-030 compile-side cancellation factor, qualifier-source mutation, FunctionArg computed-arg totality, and the pow-exponent misroute — are built **once**, in their owning phase. Phase 4 **cross-references** them and ships only the datatype-surface remainder. Each such row is marked **[xref Phase N]** below.

---

### Slice 4.1 — Lexer literal-surface gaps (COMPILER_CODE)

**Work:** Close the six lexer/literal gaps from the audit's Lexer area. Each is a localized parser, lexer, or diagnostic fix against a lexer core that is **fully conforming** — the 130+ token kinds, segmentation, escapes, and mode stack are all probe-verified, so do **not** touch them:

- **Lexer-diagnostic de-dup** (`[diverges]`): every lex-stage diagnostic is currently reported **twice** in `Compilation.Diagnostics`. The cause is two owners: `Parser.cs:52` folds `tokens.Diagnostics` into the manifest, **and** `Compiler.cs:22–30` spreads `tokens.Diagnostics` again. Pick **one** owner — either drop the spread at `Compiler.cs:24` *or* stop folding at `Parser.cs:52` — and add a no-duplicates regression assertion.
- **Empty-interpolation rejection** (`[diverges]`): the interpolated string `"{}"` (and its typed-constant twin `'{}'`) currently compiles clean, but spec §1.3:646 mandates a parser `expected expression` syntax error. Add a check for "StringStart immediately followed by StringEnd/StringMiddle" in `ParseInterpolatedString` (`src/Precept/Pipeline/Parser.Expressions.cs:436–478`) and in the typed-constant twin path (`Parser.Expressions.cs:480+`).
- **`~` on non-string type** (`[missing]`): `field X as ~integer` is silently accepted today, but spec §1.5:519 says the parser/type checker catches it. Gate the Tilde type-qualifier to **string type references only** — that means scalar string plus collections whose inner type is string — in the type-reference checker, and emit a targeted diagnostic.
- **`~ident` diagnostic contract** (`[partially]`): `~foo(...)` is rejected today, but via the generic PRE0030 unknown-function path. Spec §1.1:521 instead pins the specific diagnostic `ExpectedToken('startsWith or endsWith', actual)` over the Tilde-plus-identifier span. Either emit the spec'd diagnostic at the parser's Tilde null-denotation arm, **or** — an owner micro-call — amend the spec sentence if PRE0030 is judged the better wording for the audience. Frame that tradeoff for the owner; do not pick silently.
- **Collection-typed-hole rejection in interpolation** (`[partially]`, from the primitive area): a `set` or `list` value used inside an interpolated string such as `"...{Tags}..."` compiles clean today, but spec §3.6 says collections are a type error inside interpolation. Add collection-typed-hole rejection to interpolation type-checking; no diagnostic for this case exists today.
- **List-literal defaults across all six kinds, plus empty `[]`** (`[partially]`): `default [...]` resolves only when the field is a `list`. Fields typed `set`, `queue`, `stack`, `log`, or `bag`, and the empty literal `[]`, all reject with PRE0018 (the generic type-mismatch diagnostic) — even though `collection-types.md:779` declares the six-kind default table, and even though the proof engine **already** builds count obligations over the `TypedListLiteral` shape (`CollectCountDefaultObligation`, `ProofEngine.Analysis.cs:693`; the sibling element-length collector at `:467` walks the same `TypedListLiteral` default). Add expected-type-directed resolution in `ResolveListLiteral` and default-validation: check each element against the inner type, and coerce the literal to the field's collection kind for all six kinds. Keep `lookup` and `queue of T by P` excluded, per `collection-types.md:779`. **Scope note:** the **collection-kind type-checking** belongs to this slice; the broader collection *constructs* — lookup, cardinality, accessor typing — are Phase 5 work. This slice does only the list-literal default resolution.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- Running `precept_compile` on a single unterminated string emits each lexer code **exactly once** (the de-dup holds); a no-duplicates regression test passes under `dotnet test`.
- `"{}"`, `'{}'`, and the interpolated-typed-constant empty-hole forms each emit `expected expression`; `precept_compile` shows the syntax error.
- `field X as ~integer` and a collection-typed interpolation hole each emit a diagnostic — neither is false-clean any longer.
- `~foo(...)` emits the spec-pinned `ExpectedToken` diagnostic, or else the spec sentence is amended and the owner's micro-ruling is recorded.
- `set/queue/stack/log/bag of T default [...]` and `set of T default []` all **compile clean** with element-wise inner-type checking; an element whose inner type mismatches rejects with the correct kind-aware diagnostic; `list of T default [...]` behavior is unchanged; `lookup` and `queue…by` remain excluded.

**Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/lexer.md` (note the single-owner rule for diagnostic ownership); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (the empty-interpolation, `~ident`, `~`-on-non-string, and collection-in-interpolation codes); `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` §1.1:521 **only if** the owner rules that the `~ident` wording is amended; `docs/language/collection-types.md` (confirm the six-kind default table matches shipped behavior); `docs/language/literal-system.md` (list-literal Layer 4 resolution — Layer 4 is that doc's own name for the list-literal `[...]` layer, the last of its four literal layers).

---

### Slice 4.2 — Numeric-literal resolution soundness (COMPILER_CODE) — high priority

**Work:** Fix the three numeric-literal resolution defects. One of them is a **silent wrong-value soundness bug**, which makes it the highest-priority item in the entire datatype block:

- **Exponent-value soundness bug** (`[diverges]`): every exponent-notation literal (for example `1.5e2`) silently resolves to **0** in *all* contexts — including the `number` context where exponent notation is legal. The cause: `ResolveNumericLiteral` (`TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:194,203`) calls `decimal.TryParse` with only `AllowDecimalPoint` (and `long.TryParse`), and then **discards the parse failure** (the `_ =` discard), leaving the field's default of 0 in place. The result is false defaults and false constant folds. Fix: use exponent-aware parsing (`NumberStyles.AllowExponent`) for number-context literals, and make any `TryParse` failure an **error path** — never a silent 0.
- **Exponent lane restriction** (`[missing]`): exponent notation must be a **type error** in integer and decimal contexts — spec §1.3:629 says the notation is number-only. To enforce this, the literal's surface form (whether the token text contains `e`/`E`) must survive into resolution, and the resolver must reject exponent form in the non-`number` lanes. No enforcement code exists today.
- **No-context numeric diagnostic** (`[diverges]`): a numeric literal that has no resolution context — for example `rule 3.14 == 3.14` — silently falls back to decimal or integer today. Spec §3.3 instead mandates a diagnostic plus an `ErrorType` result. This is the numeric analogue of `UnresolvedTypedConstant`, which exists for typed constants but was never built for numerics. Either add the no-context numeric diagnostic, **or** — with owner sign-off — amend the spec to bless the integer/decimal fallback. Frame that tradeoff; do not pick silently.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `field N as number default 1.5e2` followed by `rule N > 100` **compiles clean** — in particular, no PRE0164 "constraint fails when N=0" — and `precept_compile` confirms the literal resolved to 150, not 0.
- `field D as decimal default 1.5e2` and `field I as integer default 1e3` each **reject** with the number-lane-only exponent error.
- A `TryParse` failure on a numeric literal surfaces as a diagnostic, never a silent 0; a regression test pins this.
- `rule 3.14 == 3.14` (no context) emits the no-context numeric diagnostic — or the spec is amended and the owner's fallback ruling is recorded.

**Doc-touch:** `docs/language/primitive-types.md` (the §literal table — the exponent lane and value resolution, plus the no-context rule); `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` §1.3 (exponent restriction wording) and §3.3 (the no-context rule — amend only on owner sign-off); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (the new no-context numeric code and the exponent-lane code).

---

### Slice 4.3 — Primitive teachable-message + function-arg-constraint fixes (COMPILER_CODE)

**Work:** Message-rendering fixes and dead-diagnostic wiring on the primitive surface. The underlying behavior here is mostly correct; the problem is that the diagnostics either misrender or never fire:

- **`FunctionArgConstraintViolation` (PRE0022) is dead code** (`[missing]`): `round(x, -1)` and `mid(s, 0, -1)` compile clean because PRE0022 has **zero emission sites**. Wire PRE0022 emission for arg-constraint violations that are decidable from literals — for `round`, `places ≥ 0`; for `mid`, `start` and `length` must be positive. **Non-literal arguments** need a prove-or-reject obligation instead — and that is the **FunctionArg computed-arg totality sweep, [xref Phase 3 Slice 3.3]**. This slice does only the literal-decidable emission, and for the non-literal case it must consume the Phase-3 obligation family rather than fork a parallel check.
- **PRE0018 per-shape rendering** (`[diverges]`): a single generic TypeMismatch template misrenders across distinct shapes, among them operator-on-same-type ("Expected a string value here, but got string"), cross-lane mismatch, and function-arg mismatch ("Expected a sqrt value here, but got decimal" — that is the *function name* substituted where the expected type should be). Build per-shape messages: operator-not-supported-on-type, cross-lane-with-bridge-hint, and function-arg-mismatch that names the parameter. This is the same message-rendering family as the PRE0154/0155/0159/0082 raw-AST rendering findings.
- **`sqrt(decimal)` teachable message** (`[diverges]`): the current message is the garbled "Expected a sqrt value here, but got decimal"; the spec wants a suggestion of the form "use `sqrt(approximate(value))`". Fix the function-arg TypeMismatch construction and add the bridge hint. This rides on the PRE0018 per-shape work above.
- **~string empty-literal supplementary hint** (`[partially]`): `Email == ""` emits plain PRE0066 — `CaseInsensitiveFieldRequiresTildeEquals`, the diagnostic that fires when a case-insensitive `~string` field is compared with the case-sensitive `==`/`!=` operators — with no supplementary hint. The spec'd hint is "declare the field `notempty`". Add that supplementary message.
- **~startsWith/~endsWith first-arg-must-be-~string** (`[diverges]`): `~startsWith(Name, "a")` where `Name` is a plain string compiles clean today; spec §3.7 requires a compile error. Add first-argument case-insensitive-flag validation in `ResolveCIFunctionCall` (`TypeChecker.Expressions.Callables.cs:704`), checking `TypedFieldRef.IsCaseInsensitive`.
- **if/then/else CI propagation** (`[diverges]`): `(if B then Email else Name) == "x"` compiles with no PRE0066, because conditional branch unification drops the case-insensitive flag that `~string` carries — so the `==` on the result is silently case-sensitive. Carry `IsCaseInsensitive` through `TypedConditional` branch unification, and have the `==`/`!=` enforcement consult it.

> **Coordination (Phase 1 Slice 1.4):** the **pow-exponent ≥0 misroute**. Today `pow(I, -2)` is correctly rejected but is emitted under PRE0084 `SqrtOfNegative`, because `ProofEngine.Diagnostics.cs:390-392` keys the diagnostic code on the requirement's **shape** — any `≥0` requirement maps to `SqrtOfNegative`. This is fixed **once**, in the registry repartition's requirement-identity routing. **[xref Phase 1 Slice 1.4]** — do not re-touch it here. This slice's `pow` message simply inherits the corrected routing.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `round(N, -1)` and `mid(S, 0, -1)` each emit PRE0022 under `precept_compile`; the non-literal-argument path routes through the Phase-3 computed-arg obligation, verified by a model-shape/proof test — not by a forked check.
- The five PRE0018 shapes render distinct, audience-correct messages — no more "Expected a sqrt value here" — and `sqrt(D)` names the `approximate()` bridge.
- `Email == ""` carries the `notempty` supplementary hint.
- `~startsWith(Name, "a")` with plain `Name` **rejects**; `(if B then Email else Name) == "x"` **rejects** with PRE0066, confirming the case-insensitive flag is preserved through the conditional.

**Doc-touch:** `docs/language/primitive-types.md` (§Teachable Error Messages; §CI operators first-arg rule; §Type unification CI-preservation); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (PRE0022 emission sites; PRE0018 per-shape rendering).

### Slice 4.4 — Choice family completeness (COMPILER_CODE) — soundness-grade

**Work:** Restore the dropped choice validations and close the choice false-cleans. The provenance matters here: **two diagnostic codes were built in the choice-of-T spike (commit `d308bcd4`) and then silently dropped by the catalog-first parser rewrite (commit `004eede1`)** — a classic AI-co-authored regression. Per the recorded practice, an implementation-versus-spec departure with git provenance like this is cleanup, not a decision requiring deliberation — so restore them:

- **`ChoiceMissingElementType` (PRE0090)** (`[missing]`, dead code): `choice("a","b")` written without `of T` compiles clean and behaves as a working choice. Re-emit PRE0090 from `ParseChoiceType` (`Parser.Types.cs:93-104`) when `of T` is absent.
- **`ChoiceElementTypeMismatch` (PRE0088)** (`[missing]`, dead code): `choice of integer("not-a-number")` and `choice of integer(1.5, 2)` both compile clean. Validate the domain-literal kinds against `TypeMeta.ChoiceLiteralTokens` — the catalog already declares the legal literal tokens per element type — inside `ParseChoiceType` (`Parser.Types.cs:120-126`).
- **Element-type trait gate** (`[partially]`): `choice of date` and `choice of money` are rejected today only incidentally — a cascade of PRE0009, `ExpectedToken`, the parser's generic expected-token syntax error, fires because the value literals fail to parse — while the element-type token itself is accepted, because `Parser.Types.cs:98` checks `Types.ByToken` rather than the `ChoiceElement` trait. Gate the element type on `TypeTrait.ChoiceElement` with a targeted diagnostic.
- **Choice-default membership** (`[missing]`, a soundness false-clean): `field E5 as choice of string("a","b") default "zzz"` compiles clean today — meaning an entity can be born holding a value outside its declared domain. **This check is routed to the TYPE CHECKER, not to a proof obligation, in [xref Phase 3 Slice 3.3]** — mis-routing it as a proof obligation would break canon claims C59/C86, "Create from defaults always succeeds." Phase 4 **cross-references** it: the choice-surface work here must confirm the type-checker check fires, and must not rebuild it.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `choice("a","b")` (no `of T`) emits PRE0090; `choice of integer("not-a-number")` and `choice of integer(1.5,2)` emit PRE0088; `choice of date(...)` emits the element-type-trait diagnostic. All are verified via `precept_compile`, and all were previously false-clean.
- `field E5 as choice of string("a","b") default "zzz"` **rejects** — with the Phase-3 type-checker check confirmed as the thing firing, not a duplicate built here.
- The existing live choice codes (PRE0045/0046/0085/0086/0087/0089) are unchanged, and corpus choice declarations that were valid stay valid.

**Doc-touch:** `docs/language/primitive-types.md` (§choice validation — restore PRE0088/0090, the element-type trait gate, and the default-membership cross-reference); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (re-activate the PRE0088/0090 emission rows); `docs/language/catalog-system.md` (confirm `ChoiceLiteralTokens` and the `ChoiceElement` trait are the single source the gate derives from).

### Slice 4.5 — Temporal literal + binary-context resolution (COMPILER_CODE) — flagship-form repair

**Work:** Repair the broken temporal flagship form and the temporal literal-validation surface:

- **`date ± '<quantity>'` / `time ± '<quantity>'` context inference** (`[missing]`, flagship — this is the temporal doc's own motivating example): `DueDate + '30 days'` hard-errors today with a misleading date-format message (PRE0056/0057/0122). The mechanism of the failure: `ResolveBinaryOp` (`TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:984-995`) picks a unique right-hand-side candidate **only when exactly one** catalog candidate exists for the (operator, left-hand type) pair. But `date+` has two candidates (Period, Time), and `time+` has three. So the resolver falls back to `lhs.ResultType`, the typed constant then hard-fails content validation as a malformed date, and the early error-return at `:1016` forecloses the context-retry at `:1037`. The fix: extend the context inference so that when multiple right-hand-side candidates exist, the resolver **tries each candidate's content validation** — preferring quantity candidates for quantity-shaped constants — before committing to the wrong-type diagnostic; and ensure the retry path runs before the error commits. Add the missing test matrix: date and time, plus and minus, with static, compound, and interpolated quantities.
- **PRE0056 template fix** (`[partially]`): the input `'01/15/2026'` currently produces "Dates must be written as YYYY-MM-DD. Use '01/15/2026'" — the template (`Diagnostics.cs:521`) echoes the **invalid** input back as the suggested fix. Suggest the corrected ISO form instead, or drop the Use-clause.
- **Timezone literal validation** (`[diverges]`): `'EST'` is accepted silently, because `TemporalParser.cs:113-121` uses `GetZoneOrNull`, which resolves legacy TZDB aliases. Canon instead mandates an Error carrying an `America/New_York`-style suggestion, plus deprecation **warnings** for aliases. Validate against canonical (non-link) TZDB ids; emit the error, and render the `TEMP017` Region/City suggestion. (TEMP-numbered identifiers — TEMP001 through TEMP017 — are the temporal validation layer's own diagnostic codes: message-and-suggestion texts declared by the temporal parser and validator, distinct from the compiler's PRE-numbered diagnostic codes. A TEMP message reaches the author only when the compiler carries it into a rendered PRE diagnostic.) The TEMP017 suggestion text already exists at `TemporalParser.cs:120` but is dropped during rendering.
- **Integer-magnitude and combination teachable messages** (`[partially]`): `'0.5 days'` and `'2 years 6 months'` are rejected, which is correct — but via PRE0053, `InvalidTypedConstantContent`, the generic catch-all diagnostic for any typed-constant content that fails validation, rather than via a message that tells the author what specifically was wrong. The specific TEMP002/003 messages and the `FractionalUnitValue` diagnostic (PRE0062) exist but never surface, because the Duration and Period validations (`Types.cs:100-112`) declare no Format/SemanticErrorCode. Declare those error codes — or pass the TEMP message and suggestion text through `CreateTypedConstantDiagnostic` — and remove `FractionalUnitValue` from the dead-code allowlist.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `set Deadline = DueDate + '30 days'`, `D - '30 days'`, `T + '3 hours'`, and `D + '{GraceDays} days'` all **compile clean** under `precept_compile`; the new date/time-±-quantity test matrix passes; and the `instant`/`zoneddatetime`/`datetime` left-hand-side forms, which already work, stay green.
- `'01/15/2026'` suggests the corrected `'2026-01-15'`, not the raw input.
- `field BadTz as timezone default 'EST'` **rejects** with the canonical-id error plus the Region/City suggestion; a valid canonical id stays clean.
- `'0.5 days'` and `'2 years 6 months'` render their teachable TEMP messages, not the generic PRE0053.

**Doc-touch:** `docs/language/temporal-type-system.md` (§date/time operators — confirm the flagship form is implemented; §timezone validation; §teachable error tables); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (the PRE0056 template; the timezone-id code; the FractionalUnitValue allowlist removal); `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` §3.4 (temporal-quantity context resolution wording, if drift surfaces).

### Slice 4.6 — Temporal diagnostic wiring + canon reconciliations (COMPILER_CODE + CANON_DESIGN) — Gated

**Gates:** Two owner gates apply to this slice. **GATE-J** is the Phase-0 owner gate over the sqrt §3.2-versus-§3.7 reading — the temporal arm here cross-references the primitive sqrt fix. **Decision 17** is the locked temporal-canon decision that eliminated ISO literal forms; the ISO period/duration row below touches it, which makes that row a Tier-3 owner conversation (the heaviest consultation depth — a conflict with a locked decision, requiring the prior decision quoted verbatim) and requires owner reconciliation before the ISO row builds. Per the Pre-Design Owner Consultation gate, the contested-reading rows below **do not build until the owner rules**; the uncontested wiring proceeds in the meantime.

**Work:**

- **Temporal diagnostic codes 55–62 wiring** (`[partially]`): 4 of the 8 spec'd codes — PRE0059-0062, which are `InvalidTimezoneId`, `UnqualifiedPeriodArithmetic`, `MissingTemporalUnit`, and `FractionalUnitValue` — are dead and allowlisted (`DiagnosticCoverageAllowLists.cs:20-23`). The live behavior fires the generic PRE0053, or the proof-stage PRE0113, instead. For each case, **decide which diagnostic is canonical**: either re-point the spec at the live PRE0113 mechanism (for the unconstrained-period case), **or** wire the type-stage code. Then wire the emissions, remove the 4 allowlist entries, and sync spec §3.4/§3.10. This is the temporal instance of the emit-or-retire discipline that says every declared code either demonstrably fires or is explicitly retired. Phase 6 owns the *general* emit-or-retire sweep; these temporal codes land here because the wiring context lives here — confirm there is no overlap with Phase 6 by tagging these 4 codes as "owned by Phase 4."
- **TEMP message rendering** (`[partially]`): pass the TEMP001-007/010-017 message and suggestion text — the temporal validation layer's own diagnostic codes, described at their first use in Slice 4.5 — through `CreateTypedConstantDiagnostic`, so that author-facing suggestions actually surface. This rides on Slice 4.5's error-code declarations.
- **Field-type-aware cross-type messages** (`[missing]`): every temporal cross-type rejection today renders the generic PRE0018. Canon defines a field-type-aware teachable-message rule — name the field and its declared type, then suggest the concrete fix — with a worked example table (`temporal-type-system.md:1352-1360`), sitting on top of the roughly 16-row cross-type rejection table (`:1333-1350`). Wire teachable-diagnostic selection into binary-operator rejection for the temporal operand families, aware of the field name and its declared type.
- **dayOfWeek ISO numbering** (`[diverges]`, a catalog-as-spec fix): `Types.cs:14` declares `"Day of week (0=Sunday)"`, which is the .NET convention — contradicting the canon's ISO convention (Monday=1, Sunday=7). A faithful NodaTime-based evaluator would produce ISO numbers that contradict the catalog. The fix is a one-line catalog description change plus a 1-7 range note; also verify that any numeric-range proofs over `dayOfWeek` assume 1-7. **Catalog-before-code applies:** the catalog string is the machine-readable spec that feeds hover and MCP — fix it at the catalog.
- **sqrt §3.2-versus-§3.7 (GATE-J)** (`[diverges]`): spec §3.7 says integer input to sqrt is a type error; the implementation accepts it via §3.2's integer→number widening (`SelectOverload` IsAssignable, `Callables.cs:752`). The canon is internally inconsistent, so the owner picks the reading. If §3.7 stands, add an explicit integer-argument rejection for sqrt. The **likely resolution is doc-side** — drop "integer" from the §3.7 type-error claim — but the choice is **owner-gated**.
- **ISO period/duration notation (Decision 17, Tier-3)** (`[diverges]`): `'P30D'` is accepted for period while `'PT72H'` is rejected for duration — and the canon contradicts itself, because the locked Decision 17 eliminates ISO literal forms while the summary parse-shape table still lists them. `TemporalValidator.cs:17-23` has an explicit `NormalizingIso` fallback for Period only. An **owner ruling is required**. The likely direction is: remove the fallback and the `'P30D'` catalog example to align with locked Decision 17, then make period and duration symmetric. Because this touches a locked decision, quote the decision verbatim in the consultation.
- **Single-component temporal unit-hole** (`[diverges]`): the canon grammar claims a `'{n} {u}'` form for duration and period, but the implementation supports unit holes only for quantity and unitofmeasure. This is **likely a doc-side over-claim — strike the form** from the temporal grammar table. If the owner instead chooses "implement," that is **NEW language surface and it gates**: a temporal-unit-valued expression type does not exist in the language today. Surface it as a Tier-2 owner conversation (the consultation depth for new language surface); the conservative default is strike.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- PRE0059-0062 are each either **live** (they emit under `precept_compile`) or **retired** with the allowlist entry removed and a recorded disposition; `DiagnosticCoverageScanner` shows no dead temporal code; spec §3.4/§3.10 match the outcome.
- Temporal cross-type rejections render field-type-aware messages, sampled via `precept_compile` — not bare PRE0018.
- The `Types.cs` dayOfWeek description reads ISO (Monday=1, Sunday=7); hover and MCP reflect it; any dayOfWeek range proof asserts 1-7.
- **Gated rows:** `sqrt(I)` behavior matches the owner's recorded §3.2/§3.7 reading; the ISO period/duration surface matches the owner's Decision-17 ruling, made symmetric in whichever direction was chosen; and the temporal unit-hole form is struck from canon — or, if the owner chose to implement it, it is routed to `/design`. Each gated row's exit is "matches the recorded owner ruling," verified via `precept_compile` against the post-ruling spec.

### Slice 4.7 — Business-domain soundness false-accepts (COMPILER_CODE) — high priority

**Work:** Close the compile-time business-domain false-accepts — the highest-correctness items in the business block. These build **on** Phase 3's discharge surface (reuse-not-parallel) and must route through the existing qualifier-discharge machinery rather than fork it:

- **Cross-counting-unit PRE0137 over the full operator family** (`[diverges]`, soundness): `Each + Box`, `Each == Box`, and `Each < Box` all **prove clean** today — a wrong-magnitude admission. Two mechanisms combine to cause this. First, `TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:1295-1296` early-returns before the PRE0137 block whenever the catalog operator carries a `QualifierCompatibilityProofRequirement`. Second, the proof-engine discharge matches at **dimension** granularity — and count units all share `DimensionVector.None` with factor one — so the obligation is falsely recorded as Proved. PRE0137 (the counting-unit mismatch diagnostic) currently fires only on the function-argument path (`Callables.cs:927`) and the division path (`:563`). The fix: make the proof-engine unit-qualifier discharge **unit-exact inside the count dimension** — or, alternatively, route operator-path counting-unit pairs to PRE0137 before the proof engine runs. This has a **shared root cause with BUG-030**, the UCUM cancellation-factor bug — coordinate the fix. The BUG-030 compile-half cancellation-factor is **[xref Phase 3 Slice 3.3]**; this slice does the count-unit operator-path arm.
- **Interpolated-qualifier-hole fail-open** (`[diverges]`, soundness): `set Out(money in 'USD') = '{Amount} {Cur2}'`, where `Cur2` is an arbitrary event-argument currency, compiles with **zero** proof obligations — so an EUR-valued hole can flow into a USD-pinned field. This is a verified member of the fail-open-position family (interpolation holes with no default arm in the obligation walk). **The position-totality work that closes this lives in Phase 2** — the `ChildExpressionPositions` walk plus the creation-exhaustiveness checker. Phase 4's job is confirmation: verify that the **currency/unit/from/to interpolation-hole slots** are on the checker's axis list, and that an `AssignmentQualifier` obligation is stamped for every dynamic hole slot. **[xref Phase 2 Slice 2.2/2.3]** — add the hole-slot axis there; do not build a parallel walker.
- **Qualifier-source mutation false-Proved** (`[diverges]`, soundness): the business-domain instance of the qualifier-source mutation obligation — an interpolated-qualifier source field is reassigned mid-chain, leaving the dependent facts stale. **[xref Phase 3 Slice 3.2]** — the obligation is built once in Phase 3; Phase 4 confirms the business-domain `'{Cur2}'`-hole case is covered, and does not rebuild it.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `Each + Box`, `Each == Box`, and `Each < Box` each **reject** — via PRE0137 or via unit-exact discharge failure — under `precept_compile`; `min(Each, Box)` and `Each / PerCase`, which already reject, stay rejecting; legal same-counting-unit operations stay clean.
- `set Out(money in 'USD') = '{Amount} {Cur2}'` with an unresolved `Cur2` **emits a qualifier obligation** — no longer zero-obligation — while the static form `'{Amount} USD'` stays clean. Verified via `precept_compile` obligation inspection.
- The qualifier-source-mutation business case rejects — confirmed as the Phase-3 machinery firing, not a duplicate.

**Doc-touch:** `docs/language/business-domain-types.md` (§counting units — PRE0137 operator-family coverage; §expression-position — the interpolation hole-obligation rule); `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` (the unit-exact-within-count-dimension discharge; cross-reference the Phase-2 hole-slot axis and the Phase-3 qualifier-source obligation); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (PRE0137 operator-path emission). **No `evaluator.md` capture is owed by this slice** — these are pure compile-time rejections. The corresponding value-application is BUG-030's tail, and it is captured in Slice 4.9.
