### Slice 4.8 — Business-domain type-derivation + overload-selection (COMPILER_CODE; value tails captured in 4.9)

**Work:** Fix the dead and over-rejecting derivation rows. The framing that governs this slice: **type derivation and overload selection are compiler-pure and ship here.** For each row, the compiler **admits** the operation — the result type derives, the obligation discharges — while the *value* computation each derived result implies has its **enforcement deferred** to `evaluator.md` (written in Slice 4.9) with a named capture pointer. This is admit-now with the enforcement deferred and captured — not a rejection held against the unbuilt runtime.

- **money/money cross-currency → exchangerate** (`[stub]`): the catalog row `MoneyDivideMoneyCrossCurrency=70` (`Operations.cs:462-470`, declaring `QualifierMatch.Different → ExchangeRate`) is unreachable, because the dispatcher always selects the same-currency overload; today `Usd / Eur` emits PRE0070 — `CrossCurrencyArithmetic`, the cross-currency arithmetic rejection — plus PRE0018. Implement qualifier-identity-aware overload selection so that `QualifierMatch.Different` rows actually dispatch, and suppress PRE0070 for the Divide-derivation shape.
- **quantity/quantity cross-dimension → compound quantity** (`[stub]`): the catalog row `QuantityDivideQuantityCrossDimension=79` is unreachable — the same overload-selection gap — and additionally the compound result-qualifier synthesis (producing a `kg/L` unit) is absent. Wire the Different-match dispatch plus compound-unit result inference.
- **money × exchangerate commutativity** (`[diverges]`): `Amount * Fx` over-rejects at proof stage even though the catalog declares `BidirectionalLookup: true` (`Operations.cs:704`); only the rate-first operand order works today. Fix `ParamSubject` resolution for the `BidirectionalLookup` operand order inside the `QualifierChain` discharge. To unpack that sentence: `ParamSubject` (`ProofRequirement.cs:18`) is the proof-subject shape that names which catalog-declared parameter a proof requirement binds to, and the proof engine resolves it to a concrete operand at discharge time; the `QualifierChain` discharge is the proof engine's discharge of `QualifierChainProofRequirement` (`ProofRequirement.cs:122`), the cross-type, cross-axis qualifier proof — here, the proof that chains the money operand's currency against the exchange rate's from and to currency axes. The fix makes that subject-to-operand resolution respect both operand orders that `BidirectionalLookup: true` declares legal, instead of only the rate-first one.
- **Inverse compound division** (`[missing]`): `Each / PerCase` — that is, a value in `each` divided by a ratio in `each/case`, which should yield `case` — emits PRE0137 plus PRE0018 today. D6 — the locked decision in `business-domain-types.md` § D6 that entity-scoped conversion factors are typed compound quantities — says division inverts the ratio (its rationale states the inverse direction explicitly: `each ÷ each/case → case`). Implement compound-denominator-aware division: invert the ratio, cancel the numerator. Exempt this shape from the PRE0137 counting-unit guard, coordinating with Slice 4.7 so the two changes do not fight.
- **price × duration cancellation** (`[diverges]`): `Hourly('USD/hours') * (ShiftEnd - ShiftStart)` emits PRE0114 Unresolved today. The obligation's shape is correct; the discharge simply never resolves the duration operand. Wire the duration-side discharge: a time-denominated price times a duration is proved for hours/minutes/seconds denominators, and emits PRE0073 `DurationDenominatorMismatch` for denominators of days and above.
- **Time-denominator compound quantity** (`[missing]`): declaring `quantity in 'kg/hour'` emits PRE0075 — `InvalidUnitString`, the invalid-unit-string rejection — at the declaration site. Level B is `business-domain-types.md`'s tier of single-ratio compound quantities — units of the form `X/Y` where each side is a single unit — as distinct from Level A, the named compound types `price` and `exchangerate`. The whole time-denominator branch of that Level-B family is unbuildable until UCUM qualifier parsing gains a NodaTime-vocabulary bridge for denominators. Build the **UCUM↔NodaTime denominator bridge**, covering declaration, literal, derivation-result naming, and compound×period cancellation. **Scope check:** this is type and qualifier derivation, which is compiler work; the magnitude value of any resulting rate computation defers to Slice 4.9.
- **period .basis → temporal-dimension bridge** (`[partially]`): `when OpenPer.basis == 'hours'` does not discharge a temporal-dimension chain today — it emits `PRE0114 Unresolved`. Bridge single-time-class basis facts to the temporal-dimension axis: a basis fact naming a single time class implies dimension `'time'`.
- **clamp with typed-constant args** (`[partially]`): `clamp(Q, '0 kg', '100 kg')` emits PRE0052, because typed-constant arguments in function position never receive context-born typing. Extend context-born typed-constant resolution into function-argument positions, taking the expected type from the overload's parameter.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `set Fx2 = Usd / Eur` derives **exchangerate** (no PRE0070/PRE0018); `Kg / Liters` derives a **compound quantity** with a synthesized `kg/L` qualifier; `Amount * Fx` **proves**; `Each / PerCase` derives **case**; `Hourly * duration` **proves** for an `hours` denominator and emits PRE0073 for `days`; `quantity in 'kg/hour'` **declares clean** and the literal `'5 kg/hour'` resolves; `when OpenPer.basis == 'hours' then Hourly * OpenPer` **proves**; and `clamp(Q, '0 kg', '100 kg')` **resolves**. All of these are verified via `precept_compile` type and obligation inspection.
- **No exit criterion in this slice asserts a computed magnitude** — nothing here checks "12.00" or "the ratio value." Those are the deferred value tails, captured in Slice 4.9. Every row here exits at "the result **type** is derived / the obligation **discharges**," never at "the value is computed."

**Doc-touch:** `docs/language/business-domain-types.md` (the overload-selection rows for money/money, quantity/quantity, and money×exchangerate; inverse compound division; price×duration per D15, the locked price×duration decision; the time-denominator Level-B family; the basis→dimension bridge; clamp context-born arguments); `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` (`BidirectionalLookup` operand resolution; the duration-side discharge; the basis→temporal-dimension fact bridge); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (the PRE0073 duration path). **The `evaluator.md` capture owed by this slice lands in Slice 4.9** — every derived-value tail.

### Slice 4.9 — Runtime-canon design capture: cross-unit reduction rule + edge-value + executor dispatch (CANON_DESIGN; RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER pointers)

**Work:** Write the runtime-canon design that captures every value-computation tail forced by Phase 4's type-derivation work, so the deferred runtime build inherits a complete spec. **This is the highest-priority `evaluator.md` extraction in the plan**: the cross-unit reduction rule — how the runtime computes a value when the units of the operands differ — is *never written down anywhere* today. Per §11's consolidation discipline, author the cross-unit material as **one coherent reduction-rule section**, not as scattered edits.

- **Cross-unit reduction rule (MO-DSO-05)** — the `evaluator.md` section, not yet written, that specifies how the runtime computes a value when a price's unit differs from the quantity's unit — for example, 4.00 USD per foot times 36 inches yields 12.00. MO-DSO-05 is the key under which the rest of this plan — the Phase-3 doc-touch, the §11 handoff summary, and the coverage ledger — cross-references this capture obligation. The rule: the conversion factor is `k = ScaleToBase(u_q)/ScaleToBase(u_p)`, computed in decimal, target-directed to the price's denominator unit, with rounding at maxplaces, and `k=1` in the same-unit case. This section is the value-application home for two things. First, **BUG-030** — the worked example is `4.00 USD/ft × 36 in → 12.00`; the compile-side cancellation factor is **[xref Phase 3 Slice 3.3]**. Second, the **count-pair PRE0137 magnitude** — the emission-gating sliver of that fix is COMPILER_CODE in Slice 4.7; only the magnitude's value-application defers.
- **Derived-value families** — the money/money exchangerate value, the quantity/quantity compound value, the inverse-division value, and the time-denominator cancellation value. Each family gets an explicit capture line under the reduction-rule section. The **type derivation** for each is Slice 4.8 compiler work; the **value** defers. *This closes what was the prior plan's blocking silent-loss gap: findings BIZ-10/11/12/13 named "runtime value defers" with no capture obligation attached — every tail now has a pointer.*
- **D8 dimension-narrowed / commensurable cross-unit assignment — admit now, defer the conversion's enforcement.** D8 is the locked decision on assigning a quantity in one unit to a field declared in a different but commensurable unit of the same dimension. The D8-versus-unit-exact question itself is CANON_DESIGN. The **static-fold subset** — both operands compile-time constants — is compiler-pure and admits in Slice 4.8. The **dynamic-write case** is *also fault-safe*: target-directed conversion of a commensurable same-dimension quantity multiplies by a finite UCUM scale factor (`k = ScaleToBase(u_q)/ScaleToBase(u_p)`). That is a total operation: there is no fault category under which a complete runtime could fail to perform it. The only overflow category it touches is the PARKED representability family under D1, and D1 is never a ground for rejection. A fully-built runtime performs this conversion, so the operation is admissible — therefore the compiler **admits the dynamic-write case now**, and the conversion's **enforcement defers** to the runtime, with its contract captured in `evaluator.md §Intake-Boundary Normalization` (§ 11). This is the same admit-now-defer-capture treatment given to the reclassified bands and to the derived-value tails — not a rejection held against the unbuilt runtime. Pre-release, the admission creates no live fault: the runtime is a pure stub (`Evaluator.cs` is all `NotImplementedException`), so nothing executes, and the obligation to build the conversion is captured in § 11. For the capture itself: record the D8 target-directed conversion contract by **extending the existing intake-boundary normalization** — the normalize-on-intake invariant at the "Where it happens" / `TypeRuntime<Quantity>.FromClr` seam — to cover assignment-**target**-directed conversion. Reuse the established normalization machinery; do not author a parallel conversion path.
- **Edge-value semantics** — banker's rounding, the left/right/mid clamp directions, ∞/NaN faults, and integer-conversion overflow faults (the `[stub]` primitive edge-value row). These land in an `evaluator.md` edge-value section plus `fault-system.md` (the non-finite and integer-conversion-overflow codes). **The fault-code disposition depends on GATE-O**, the Phase-0 owner gate that settles whether the ∞/NaN and integer-conversion-overflow codes fall inside the D1 representability park, form a new compile-time-prevented lane, or take their own explicit park. Apply GATE-O's answer here; note that the wording of DoD-9 — the ninth Definition-of-Done criterion, whose contract, stated in `docs/runtime/evaluator.md` §10, permits a post-clean-compile evaluator fault only for the parked decimal/temporal representability family — depends on that answer.
- **D18 executor-module dispatch and D13a serialization** — D18 is the locked decision in `docs/language/business-domain-types.md` that the CLR types are pure data records, with all computation in executor modules: MoneyOperations, QuantityOperations, PriceOperations, and ExchangeRateOperations. The dispatch sits at the boundary where the evaluator hands arithmetic on money, quantity, price, and exchange-rate values to those per-type operation modules — the Door-2 boundary. D13a is the locked serialization decision. The executable model is undesigned today — capture the module layout and the serialization string forms.
- **Conditional-totality opcode contract** — the `BRANCH_FALSE`/`BRANCH_TRUE`/if-lowering semantics. The opcode table exists at `evaluator.md:1006-1007`, but the four pending opcode semantics at `:993` — `LOAD_ARG` behavior when the argument is absent or null, `BRANCH_FALSE` zero-falsy behavior, `RETURN` fall-through, and stack pooling — must be **locked**. Also resolve the `&&`/`||`/`?:` notation drift. *(The conditional-totality **proof-engine half** is Phase 5 work; the **spec/evaluator-contract half** is split between this slice and Phase 5 — this slice owns the temporal and business opcode rows that Phase-4 derivations exercise.)*
- **D9 clock seam applied** — D9 is the locked decision requiring an injectable per-operation clock. Record that requirement against the temporal value contract, so that `now()` is deterministic and testable. The decision itself was captured in Phase 0; here it is **applied** to the temporal execution contract. Lands in the `evaluator.md` temporal execution contract plus `runtime-api.md`.
- **Temporal execution contract** — LocalDate.Plus, Period.Between, nanosecond bridging, InZoneLeniently, the NodaTime System.Text.Json round-trip, and fire-boundary timezone validation (this is the `[stub]` temporal runtime row); plus D6.2 composite-period lowering — the previously-deferred decision, recorded during the composite-period-basis work, that composite periods (multi-component bases such as `'hours + minutes'`) lower through NodaTime's `Period.Between` at runtime. That D6.2 belongs to the composite-period decision family; it is **not** a sub-decision of `business-domain-types.md` § D6, the conversion-factor decision Slice 4.8 cites.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable — this slice's exits are design-capture, not execution):**
- `evaluator.md` contains the cross-unit reduction-rule section as **one** coherent design (MO-DSO-05) — the section specifying how the runtime computes a value when a price's unit differs from the quantity's unit — with a capture line for each derived-value family (money/money, quantity/quantity, inverse-division, time-denominator). No tail is left uncaptured.
- The D8 dynamic-write conversion is captured as an extension of the intake-boundary normalization invariant (§ 11), with an explicit note that the conversion's **enforcement defers** to the runtime. `precept_compile` confirms the dynamic-write case **admits** — the conversion is fault-safe, its enforcement deferred and captured in § 11 — and that the static-fold subset (Slice 4.8) admits too. No over-rejection is held against the unbuilt runtime.
- The edge-value section, the D18 executor dispatch, the D13a serialization, the locked opcode semantics, the D9 clock seam, and the temporal execution contract each have a written `evaluator.md` section — and `fault-system.md`/`runtime-api.md` sections where those docs are named above.
- `dotnet build` is green. This slice is doc-only from the compiler's perspective; it lands no compile-side rejection — the D8 dynamic-write case admits, with the conversion's enforcement deferred to the captures above.

**Doc-touch (all IN SCOPE — this is runtime canon):** `docs/runtime/evaluator.md` (the cross-unit reduction-rule section, new; the derived-value capture lines; the D8 intake-boundary extension; the edge-value section; D18 executor dispatch; the locked opcode semantics plus the conditional notation; the D9 clock; the temporal execution contract); `docs/runtime/fault-system.md` (the non-finite, integer-conversion-overflow, and band-not-fault rows); `docs/runtime/result-types.md` (derived-value outcome shapes; D13a serialization); `docs/runtime/runtime-api.md` (the D9 injectable clock); `docs/language/business-domain-types.md` (the D18 module layout and D13a — as a doc-follows-impl pointer to the runtime capture).

### Slice 4.10 — Business-domain canon reconciliations (CANON_DESIGN; doc-follows-impl + owner forks) — partially Gated

**Gates:** **GATE-K** is the Phase-0 owner gate over the log-unit cross-unit policy — how the decibel/neper/bel family may mix across units. It is Tier-2 language surface, requiring an owner consultation before any `/design` run. An interim soundness floor already holds for the log units: they carry `ScaleIsRational=false` — the per-unit catalog flag recording that a unit's scale-to-base factor is not an exact rational suitable for amount conversion — which keeps the compiler from treating any log-unit scale factor as an exact conversion factor while the policy is unruled. Because that floor holds, GATE-K is non-blocking for the rest of the slice. The **D16-versus-spec function-table forks** — floor/ceil/truncate plus abs/round/clamp on money/quantity/price — are owner forks, resolved at Phase-4 start per the dependency note above. By contrast, the doc-drifts the owner has **already ruled on** — exchangerate, maxplaces `currency.minorUnit`, exchangerate-slash syntax — are **apply-the-ruling** work, not re-litigation.

**Work:** Reconcile the business-domain canon contradictions and apply the doc-follows-impl promotions. Wherever a row locks a contested reading, it carries a four-leg rationale, per the Per-Decision Rationale rule:

- **Apply the already-ruled doc-drift (execute, do not re-gate):** three items. First, the exchangerate declaration/literal form `in X to Y` with from/to literal order — the owner already ruled that the implementation's form is canonical, and the `business-domain-types.md` §exchangerate tables are stale; rewrite the declaration, interpolation, constraint, and semantics tables, plus the spec §1.3:1311 example, to the from/to form. Second, the exchangerate-slash syntax — owner-ruled but unbuilt; execute it. Third, the maxplaces `currency.minorUnit` form — an owner-ratified phase-4 feature that exists in code (`Modifiers.cs:543-561` plus PRE0152) but that the canon never gained; promote it into `business-domain-types.md` §maxplaces and the spec §2.4 modifier-value rules.
- **unitofmeasure atomic-only** (`[diverges]`, owner call): canon says compound unit expressions are rejected (via `InvalidUnitString`, PRE0075); the implementation accepts full UCUM expressions, and the catalog's own examples are compound. The owner picks: enforce-atomic (the rationale being injection prevention) versus amend-the-doc. Apply whichever is picked.
- **dimension partition comparison** (`[partially]`): verify — or implement — the rejection of cross-type dimension comparisons, meaning comparisons across the temporal partition and the UCUM partition. The original probe was masked by a presence error, so this needs a clean re-probe, and likely an emission.
- **D16-versus-spec function-table forks** (owner): two forks. First, floor/ceil/truncate on money and quantity: D16 grants them, spec §3:1605 omits them, and the implementation follows the spec. Second, abs/round/clamp/min/max on price: D16 plus the §price prose promise them, the spec omits them, the implementation follows the spec — and the current not-supported guidance points authors at a `round` function that does not exist. The owner picks add-the-overloads (honoring D16) or strike-the-D16-row; apply the pick. **If the pick is "add overloads," the type-derivation work is COMPILER_CODE** and joins Slice 4.8; if the pick is "strike," the change is pure doc.
- **angle-unit dimension identity** (`[diverges from locked allow+surface]`): the locked posture for the angle units (deg/rad/gon) is allow-and-surface — allow angle-unit arithmetic by giving those units a real dimension identity, and surface the approximation their π-based scale factors carry through the `ScaleIsRational` flag, rather than rejecting. The implementation diverges: angle units currently resolve to an empty dimension name, so even same-unit angle operations reject. Reconcile the implementation against the locked allow-and-surface posture.
- **bare-number bounds on magnitude types** (`[partially]`): the canon is internally split — the constraint table allows a bare number `N` as a bound, while D16 demands domain-typed bounds — and the implementation is inconsistent too: quantity accepts a bare bound while money rejects it with a misleading PRE0133 message. Decide once, make money and quantity consistent, and fix the false PRE0133 message.
- **identity-type notempty model** (`[partially]`): canon claim C57 rejects `notempty` on identity types; the implementation instead emits a redundancy warning, which is arguably the better behavior. Record the implied-notempty-plus-warning model in the identity-type constraint table.
- **log-unit policy (GATE-K):** allow-with-what versus restrict; owner consultation before `/design`. The interim floor holds, so this is non-blocking; it is carried as a gated reconciliation.
- **Teachable business messages** (`[partially]`): operator-misuse rejections route to the generic PRE0018 (producing text like "Expected a money value here, but got money"); PRE0114 carries internal jargon ("FromCurrency↔Currency: unresolved"); and the double-quote qualifier mistake needs a single-quote hint. This is a message-quality pass. The compiler-side rendering work may co-locate with the relevant Slice 4.7/4.8 rows; the *policy* — what the messages should say — is the CANON_DESIGN piece owned here.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- The already-ruled doc-drifts are applied: `business-domain-types.md` §exchangerate matches the `in X to Y` + from/to implementation; maxplaces `currency.minorUnit` and the exchangerate-slash syntax are documented and built; `precept_compile` on the canonical exchangerate sample stays clean, and the docs no longer contradict it.
- Each owner fork — unitofmeasure atomic-only, the D16 function table, angle-unit, bare-number bounds, identity notempty, log-unit — has a **recorded owner ruling** with four-leg rationale; the chosen direction is applied, whether as a doc amendment or as compiler code folded into Slice 4.8; `precept_compile` matches the ruling.
- The dimension partition-comparison rejection fires on a clean re-probe — or is recorded as already-correct.
- The business teachable-message policy is documented, and the false PRE0133-on-money message is fixed: `precept_compile` shows no spurious "require qualifier 'in'" message when `in` is in fact present.

**Doc-touch:** `docs/language/business-domain-types.md` (the §exchangerate rewrite; the §maxplaces minorUnit promotion; §unitofmeasure atomic-only; §dimension partition; the D16 function table; angle-unit; bare-number bounds; the identity-type constraint table; the teachable-message tables); `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` §1.3 (the exchangerate literal example) and §2.4 (maxplaces minorUnit and the function-table modifier rules — **edited only per recorded owner rulings**); `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` (the PRE0133 message fix; teachable-message rendering).

### Phase 4 PARKED (not built; documented as known limitation only)

Per D1 (overflow parked), **do not re-add** any of these in this phase. Each is documented as a known limitation and is never claimed prevented:

- **Decimal-representability overflow** across the datatype surface — a known-limitation doc entry only.
- **Temporal-arithmetic overflow** — the same representability genus as decimal overflow, so D1's "same representability genus" reasoning governs it. **Reconcile the prior plan's "P4-TEMP-11 NOT parked" framing to PARKED.** **Do not** build a `FaultCode.TemporalOverflow` and do not build a temporal-overflow obligation. The Phase-0 temporal-overflow probe feeds the known-limitation note, not a build.
- **maxplaces compile-time precision proof** — parked together with overflow; D1 places it in the same number-model class of parked work as the number-model redesign (per the Boundary classification above). **Add a maxplaces honesty-doc obligation:** with the compile-time precision proof parked **and** the runtime maxplaces points 2-3 deferred (§11), maxplaces over-precision is **jointly unenforced** — neither the compiler nor the stub runtime catches it. Document that as a known limitation, mirroring the overflow doc.
- **Compiler self-protection carve-out (this one is NOT parked):** the compiler's own **checked constant-fold** — ensuring a literal expression like `2e28 * 2e28` cannot crash the pipeline itself — is compiler robustness, which is distinct from the parked runtime representability obligation. **First verify whether the compiler folds such literals at all** before scoping the work; if it does fold them, the checked-fold stays COMPILER_CODE in this phase, riding on Slice 4.2's numeric-resolution work.

---

### Phase 4 exit

- All 67 datatype-surface gaps (Lexer 6 + Primitives/choice 22 + Business 28 + Temporal 11) are either closed **or** carried as a recorded owner-fork ruling. The soundness false-accepts — exponent-value, choice field-vs-field and choice-default, cross-counting-unit, interpolated-qualifier-hole — all reject via `precept_compile`.
- Every derived-value tail and the D8 dynamic-write conversion are **captured in `evaluator.md`** (Slice 4.9) as admit-now / defer-enforcement obligations: the compiler **admits** each operation — the type derives, and the conversion is fault-safe — while the runtime value-application and target-directed-conversion **enforcement defers** to § 11. No rejection is held against the unbuilt runtime.
- The gated rows — GATE-J sqrt, Decision-17 ISO, GATE-K log-unit, the D16 function-table forks, and unitofmeasure atomic-only — each match a recorded owner ruling, and no spec edit landed without one.
- The PARKED items are documented as known limitations and never claimed prevented; **no `FaultCode.TemporalOverflow`, no representability obligation, and no maxplaces precision proof** were built.
- `dotnet build` is green; the full test suite is stable; the corpus breakage from the soundness fixes (the compat wave) is routed to Phase 2 Slice 2.4 for fixture repair.
- **No exit criterion in this phase asserts a runtime-execution outcome.** Every band, derivation, and conversion row exits at "compiles clean," "derives the type," "emits the proven-violation diagnostic," or "captures the runtime contract" — never at "governance refuses the write" or "the magnitude cancels to 12.00."

## 8. Phase 5 — Constructs spec completeness (collections / modifiers / expression / rules / states / binder / graph)

**Goal:** Bring the compiler to 100% of the canonical spec for every construct surface that is not a datatype-literal surface (datatype literals are Phase 4's job). Seven construct families are in scope: the nine collection kinds together with their accessors and cardinality rules; the eleven constraint modifiers and how each participates in proof; the full expression language (operators, functions, quantifiers, presence checks, member access); rules and ensures in every scoping form; states, transitions, events, actions, and access modes; the name-binder and type-checker; and the graph analyzer.

This is the breadth phase, and it deliberately runs against a floor that now holds. By the time Phase 5 starts, three things are true. First, the obligation-creation walk is position-total — Phase 2 has made it visit every catalogued child-expression position. Second, Phase 1 has made the fault-code registry honest. Third, the known False-Proved holes — obligations recorded as discharged when the fault is not actually ruled out — are closed by Phase 3. New constructs therefore land against a sound floor instead of widening it.

The single largest item in the phase is 2c-ii — the second half of the locked relational-rules-and-bounds design, which makes field-referencing bound modifiers participate in proof (tracked as BUG-020). It is the largest unbuilt locked design in the codebase.

Every slice in this phase is type-checker, parser, obligation-walk, or graph work — all compile-side. None requires the runtime to execute. Where a construct's full governance does need the future runtime sweep — element-ingress checking, the collect-all sweep (the runtime pass that evaluates every declared constraint and collects every violation rather than stopping at the first), cross-transition ensure enforcement, omit-clears (the access-mode rule under which a field declared `omit` in a state is cleared — reset to its default value — on any transition into that state), or the firing of synthesized rules — the compiler half ships here and the runtime half is deferred with a named `docs/runtime/*.md` capture pointer recorded in §11 (the Runtime-Coding Handoff).

**Dependencies:**

- **Phase 2 floor (positions + checker).** Four position families matter here: the because-clause interpolation slots; the guard slots (rule, ensure, transition, hook, and access-mode `.Guard`); the default-expression interiors; and `TypedMemberAccess.Arguments`. These are exactly the positions that Phase 2's `ChildExpressionPositions` projection and its creation-exhaustiveness checker catalogued and closed. Phase 5's per-construct fixes — the because-clause binding, the BUG-031 guard obligations (faulting arithmetic in a guard currently collects no obligation), and the BUG-032 accessor-argument obligations (a faulting expression inside an accessor argument currently collects no obligation) — all register against that single walk. Building them before Phase 2 would re-introduce the per-construct fork the checker exists to prevent; this is Frank finding A3, the catalog-discipline finding from the adversarial plan review, summarized as "derive, don't proliferate switch arms."
- **Phase 3 proof machinery.** 2c-ii's numeric discharge reuses two shipped pieces: the 2c-i relational narrowing (the already-shipped first half of the relational-rules-and-bounds design, which lets declared rules narrow each other during proof) and the BUG-027 default-fold. The conditional-totality proof half reuses the intra-guard short-circuit narrowing that landed in Phase 2 Slice 2.2 and Phase 3.
- **Phase 1 honest registry + Slice 1.2 synthesis decision.** 2c-ii desugars each bound into a synthetic `TypedRule`, and that desugar rides the same `DesugarsToRule` synthesis seam as Phase 1 Slice 1.2 (the slice that turns the catalog's DesugarsToRule metadata into synthesized typed rules in the compiled model). It also inherits that slice's unconsumed-artifact guard — the rule that synthesized rules may land only if a real compile-time consumer reads them. 2c-ii is the named compile-time consumer that justifies the synthesis being compiler-pure.
- **GATE-M (resolves at Phase-5 start).** GATE-M is the one lettered owner-decision gate that is **not** a Phase-0 gate — it resolves at Phase-5 start instead. It asks which single stage owns emission of PRE0141, the bound-expression diagnostic. The expectation is that GATE-M resolves as a side effect of 2c-ii's bounds-desugar-to-rules. Confirm this at Phase-5 start and feed the disposition to Phase 6 Slice 6.4, the single-stage ownership analyzer. Fallback if 2c-ii does not in fact resolve it: carry the split ownership explicitly into Phase 6 as the analyzer's one carve-out.
- **Owner fork resolved at Phase-5 start:** Slice 5.7 contains a choice element-type fork — either implement the PRE0088/PRE0090 declaration checks, or strike the corresponding spec rows in favor of untyped-choice inference. That fork is settled in an owner conversation at phase start, before Slice 5.7 begins.
- **No cross-phase hard ordering.** The one hard ordering in this entire plan is that the Phase-1 registry honesty must land before the Phase-2 creation-exhaustiveness checker first reads it, per owner decision D3 (atomicity relaxed). Phase 5 sequences after Phases 2 and 3 by smallest-safe-increment preference, not by any atomic coupling.

**Effort:** L (14–20 days, parallelizable across the seven construct families once 2c-ii lands).

---

### Slice 5.1 — 2c-ii field-reference bound enforcement (BUG-020) — the flagship

**Work:** Build the largest unbuilt locked design. The subject is field-reference bound modifiers — shapes like `min Floor`, `max Ceiling`, and the relational `min/max Expr` forms. Today these are silently accepted and then ignored: verified at `TryGetComparableModifierValue` (`TypeChecker.Validation.Modifiers.cs:462-480`), which handles only literal, unary-minus, and typed-constant modifier values and drops everything else. For every such bound, this slice desugars it into a span-tagged synthetic `TypedRule` stamped at the field declaration, and then applies prove-or-reject at compile time — the definition either proves the bound holds or is rejected. The mechanics:

- Numeric discharge goes through the shipped 2c-i relational narrowing — the octagon-style single-pass narrowing from the locked relational-rules-and-bounds design. Numeric default-containment goes through the shipped BUG-027 default-fold.
- The length/count band-edge discriminated union is resolved at discharge time: the bound's `LengthBound` versus `CountBound` shape is decided where the obligation is discharged, not at parse.
- Self-reference and mutual-reference cases must terminate. A field whose bound references itself, or a `min A` / `min B` cycle between two fields, must terminate the narrowing rather than loop.
- The rejection message is rich and uses domain vocabulary — no raw-AST render.
- This closes the binder half of BUG-020. The name-resolution half already landed: `min Nonexistent` produces PRE0017 via `NameBinder.cs:498-509`, commit `e2b7c9c1`. What is still missing is the enforcement: `field Amount as number min Floor` with `Floor default 10` compiles clean today and the bound is inert — verified by probe P28, one of the numbered `precept_compile` probes.
- **GATE-M side effect:** confirm that desugaring the bound to a `TypedRule` resolves PRE0141 (`UnprovedAssignmentQualifierCompatibility = 141`, `DiagnosticCode.cs:187`) bound-expression ownership as a single-stage owner. Record the disposition for Phase 6 Slice 6.4.

**⚠ Unconsumed-artifact guard (couples to Phase 1 Slice 1.2):** confirm that 2c-ii's compile-time prove-or-reject actually consumes the synthesized `TypedRule`, rather than discharging purely through relational narrowing. If discharge goes only through narrowing, then the Phase-1 synthesis is dead model state until the runtime ships. In that case, co-defer the synthesis together with the runtime sweep and record the reason — consistent with Slice 1.2's caveat. Decide at slice start; surface to the owner if the consumer is uncertain.

**Compiler prove-or-reject (permanent); runtime ingress is a separate deferred feature.** These are two distinct things and the slice must keep them distinct. Where the compiler cannot prove the bound holds, it rejects. That is genuine fault-safety prove-or-reject: an unprovable bound is permanently unprovable, a fully-built runtime cannot make it provable, so the rejection is correct forever and has nothing to do with whether the runtime exists. It is not an admit-when-the-runtime-arrives hold — the compiler never admits the unprovable case. Separately, the runtime ingress enforcement of the synthesized bound — refusing a commit that violates a proof-carrying field-reference bound on a running entity, i.e. governing externally-supplied and cross-event values — is a deferred runtime feature, classified RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER. The compiler exit criterion is prove-or-reject only; it must **never** assert "a running entity's out-of-bound write is refused."

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- The locked design's enumerated rejection cases all reject at compile time, verified via `precept_compile`. Four cases are enumerated. The default-fold violation: a field default that provably violates the field-reference bound. The dependent-operation discharge cases: a fault-prone operation, such as a division by the bounded field, depends on the field-reference bound for its safety proof, and the bound as declared cannot discharge it. The empty-band contradiction: the bound's resolved edge contradicts the field's own declared band, leaving an empty band, which rejects as a contradiction rather than passing as vacuously satisfied. And the unbounded-reference identity-degradation case: the referenced field is unbounded in the demanded direction, so the bound yields no narrowing at all ("identity-degradation") and the obligation stays unprovable — which rejects rather than being treated as safe. A provably-satisfying configuration compiles clean. This criterion is scoped to the cases the locked design enumerates. The design locks the numeric proof requirements unchanged and leaves the scalar band edge null for a field-reference bound, so a write-site (`set`) rejection is not among the enumerated cases — see the owner fork below.
- The relational `min Expr` shapes that compile clean and inert today (probe P28) now participate in proof — verified via `precept_compile` showing the obligation present in the compiled model.
- Self-reference and mutual-reference cases terminate (the build completes with no hang) and emit a coherent message rather than looping.
- `dotnet build` green; the synthesized `TypedRule` is read by 2c-ii's discharge (model-shape test green), or the co-defer decision is recorded instead.
- No exit criterion asserts a runtime refusal — that is the deferred ingress enforcement.

**⚠ EXPLICIT OWNER FORK — the provably-violating scalar `set` case:** a provably-violating scalar write against a field-reference bound — for example `field Amount as number min Floor` with `Floor default 10` and `set Amount = 5` — is not among the locked design's enumerated rejection cases, and it compiles clean at HEAD. The reason is that the write-site machinery is scalar-only: the set-site obligation generator gates on declared scalar bounds, and those are null for a field-reference bound. Two options, surfaced to the owner at slice start: (a) the case stays out, per the locked design as written; or (b) the owner authorizes a design delta extending write-site numeric enforcement to field-reference bounds, routed through `/design` and never patched into the build inline. **Do not silently widen the exit criterion to cover it.**

**Sequencing note (verified at HEAD):** the Phase-1 Slice-1.2 `DesugarsToRule` synthesis seam has no pipeline consumers at HEAD — its only consumers are cosmetic/display, namely the GrammarGen color lane and the MCP formatter. This slice therefore builds the desugar itself rather than riding a pre-existing seam. Check the land-or-co-defer decision recorded at Slice 1.2 at slice start.

**Gate:** GATE-M confirmed resolved-as-side-effect at slice start, or carried explicitly to Phase 6 if 2c-ii does not in fact resolve it.

**Doc-touch:**
- `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` — the bound-desugar-to-rule discharge (Strategy section); self/mutual-reference termination.
- `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` — the proven-violation bound code, plus the PRE0141 ownership disposition.
- `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` §2.4 / §3.8 — field-reference bound semantics: the modifier participates in proof identically to the equivalent rule.
- `docs/language/business-domain-types.md` — qualified-bound interaction note.
- `docs/tooling/mcp.md` plus the `tools/Precept.Mcp/Dtos/CompileToolDtos.cs` projection, and `docs/tooling/language-server.md` (hover on the synthesized bound) — only if the compiled-model surface changes.
- `docs/Working/bugs.md` — flip BUG-020 to Fixed.
- **Runtime-canon capture (deferred ingress enforcement):** `docs/runtime/evaluator.md §7.6 + §7.1` — the modifier→`TypedRule` constraint-plan entry, the §0.7 "operation-blind" governance refusal — "operation-blind" is the spec's own §0.7 term: governance upholds a field's declared contract whether or not any fault-prone operation references the field — and the Length/Count/Interval runtime trap behavior; plus a `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` cross-link to the `DesugarsToRule` consumer contract. This is the deferred runtime-ingress feature. It does not smuggle a runtime exit criterion into this slice — the compiler's prove-or-reject is permanent and independent of it.

### Slice 5.2 — Conditional-totality proof-engine half + computed-field default fold

**Work:** Two coupled proof-engine completeness items.

- **Conditional-totality (proof-engine half):** implement intra-guard short-circuit narrowing via `AssumedConditions` / `EffectiveGuard`. This is per-branch narrowing: it replaces the current model in which all branches share one narrowing environment unioned together (the Γ-shared UNION), so that a guard's narrowed facts flow into the guarded body. Two idioms must discharge as a result: the canonical optional idiom `when X is set and X > 5`, and the spec's own recommended `Arg is not set or Arg.length > 0`. This is the compile-side half only, and it ships as proof-engine unit tests. **DO-NOT-RESURRECT** the deleted ProofChecker / ProofWitness "verify-don't-trust" architecture — the design, deleted by owner directive, in which a separate pass re-verified the proof engine's already-proved results after the fact instead of trusting prove-or-reject. Ground this slice's narrowing work against the fault-floor outcome instead — the fault-floor definition research's conclusion that the engine is prove-or-reject, not verify-after-the-fact. The narrowing is minimum discharge power, not a re-verification pass.
- **Computed-field default fold:** wire the `IsComputed` arm in `BuildDefaultEnvironment` (`ProofEngine.Analysis.cs:138`). The arm exists for `DefaultExpression is not null || field.IsComputed`, but the computed value is not folded into the default environment that the rule/ensure fold reads. Implement acceptance criteria 1–8 from the locked design. Re-scope BUG-028 — the default-satisfiability fold completeness bug, where the fold is skipped entirely when a construction handler exists, even for fields the handler does not assign — now that its prerequisite BUG-027 shipped (commit `210229ff`). Then run, across the corpus, the soundness-tax sweep this fold produces.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `rule T is not set or T.length > 0` on a field, the same expression in `ensure` position, and `if T is set then T.length else 1` all compile clean where today they emit PRE0116, the `UnprovedPresenceRequirement` diagnostic for a presence requirement the proof engine could not discharge (verified probes) — the spec's own recommended non-empty-if-present idiom now discharges.
- A computed field's value participates in the default fold: a global rule provably violated by a computed default is rejected (a PRE0164-class diagnostic), and a satisfied one is clean — verified via `precept_compile`.
- BUG-028's "fold skipped when a handler exists" coarseness is narrowed per the re-scope; proof-engine unit tests green; `dotnet build` / `dotnet test` green.

**Doc-touch:**
- `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` — the `AssumedConditions`/`EffectiveGuard` per-branch narrowing; the computed-field default-fold arm.
- `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` §0.6 (the default fold now runs against computed values) and §3.6 (the recommended present-or-empty idiom now compiles).
- `docs/Working/bugs.md` — BUG-028 re-scoped.
- **Runtime-canon capture (conditional-totality runtime contract half — the opcode lowering):** `docs/runtime/evaluator.md` — the BRANCH_FALSE / BRANCH_TRUE / if-lowering opcode contract, which is the conditional-totality spec half; this fixes `evaluator.md:993` plus the `&&`/`||`/`?:` notation drift. This capture is CANON_DESIGN in scope here — the doc-and-design work happens now; the executable lowering itself is RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER.

### Slice 5.3 — Collections: typing holes, list-literal defaults, accessors, cardinality

**Work:** Close the collection-kind compile-side gaps — 22 audit rows. These are predominantly fail-open write-site typing holes (write sites where a wrong type slips through with no diagnostic) plus the multi-kind list-literal-default gap. All are type-checker or obligation-collector fixes:

- **List-literal defaults across all 6 kinds plus empty `[]`:** the canon (`collection-types.md` §List Literal Defaults) allows `default [...]` on set, queue, stack, log, bag, and list. The implementation accepts it only on `list of T` — verified: `set of string default []` produces PRE0018 (`TypeMismatch`, the general type-mismatch diagnostic). Teach default-position assignability that a `TypedListLiteral` is assignable to any of the 6 kinds when its elements unify with `T`. The root cause is two-fold: `ResolveListLiteral` hard-types the literal as `TypeKind.List` at `TypeChecker.Expressions.Callables.cs:632`, and `IsAssignable` is kind-equality plus `WidensTo` with no literal→collection rule. This is a pure type-checker fix — the proof engine already generates the default-count obligation on the very declaration the type checker rejects (probe-confirmed: `CountContainment` Proved on that declaration). Also reject `default [...]` on the keyed kinds (queue-by, lookup) per canon, with a dedicated message.
- **PRE0044 (`ListLiteralOutsideDefault`) position-gating:** gate the diagnostic on expression position (any non-default position), not on `ctx.CurrentEventName`. Today a list literal in a global `rule` body compiles clean, because rule bodies have no current event (`TypeChecker.Expressions.Callables.cs:620-628`).
- **Write-site element/key typing holes (fail-open):** wire the `PRE0105`-class element-type check into the by-keyed and two-target actions that bypass the single-input path — `EnqueueBy` / `AppendBy` primary and secondary values, the `dequeue/pop into G [by H]` targets, `put F K = V` key plus value, and list `insert` element. Today each of these compiles clean on a wrong type (verified probes).
- **`into`-target write-site visibility:** register `into`-targets as establishing write sites. `GraphAnalyzer.FieldWriteSites.cs:112-121` matches only `action.FieldName`, so `into`-writes are invisible and produce a false PRE0158 ("has no write site").
- **Lookup `F for K` key-presence obligation:** add the access-side `KeyPresenceProofRequirement` to `LookupAccess` — `Operations.cs:95-100` carries none — so that an unguarded `set X = Lk for K` generates a key-presence obligation dischargeable by a `when F contains K` guard. The discharge strategy (`Strategies.cs`) and the PRE0099 mapping (`ProofEngine.Diagnostics.cs:181-186`) already exist; PRE0099 is dead code until this collector is added. This is the same family as the 10 Phase-2 obligation-position holes.
- **`P must be orderable` validation** for queue-by and log-by ordering keys — reuse the `TypeTrait.Orderable` check that PRE0104 already uses for `.min`/`.max`.
- **`ascending`/`descending` kind-restriction:** the direction modifier is silently accepted on every collection kind and ignored (`Parser.Types.cs:192-198`). Restrict it to queue-by per canon and emit an `InvalidModifierForType`-class diagnostic elsewhere.
- **BUG-032 collection-accessor-argument obligation:** recurse into `TypedMemberAccess.Arguments` so that `coll.at(A / B)` collects the divisor obligation. The verified hole: the `TypedMemberAccess` arm at `ProofEngine.cs:319-323` walks `ma.Object` but not the argument sub-expressions. This cross-references Phase 2 — the systemic fix is the child-position checker; this is the collection-accessor instance of it.
- **Cross-kind action misuse and contains messages:** reconcile the `CollectionOperationOnScalar` / `ScalarOperationOnCollection` mapping between code names and the scenarios they actually fire on; fix `contains` mismatch messages that render the collection kind where the element type is meant.
- **Message rendering pass** over the collection codes — PRE0100's `"access at index 'index'"`, PRE0101's `"Key 'element'"`, PRE0135's literal `?` — using the same `DescribeExpression` approach as the BUG-029 fix.
- **Collections rejected in string interpolation:** wire the interpolation-segment type check. PRE0051 (`InvalidInterpolationCoercion`) is defined but has zero emission sites — a dead diagnostic.

**Boundary note (compile-side prove-or-reject + deferred ingress enforcement):** two collection items belong to the qualifier-source mutation family and are built elsewhere. The wrong-denomination element write (an `add money in EUR` into a `set of money in USD`) and the qualifier-sensitive orderability matrix are the collection face of that family. The compile-side qualifier validation at the collection write-site is in scope — route collection write values through `ValidateAssignmentQualifiers` — but it is closed in Phase 3 Slice 3.2; build it once there, and this slice only cross-references it. The element write-site proof — a provably-out-of-bound `add` rejected, per spec §249/§1123 (line numbers in `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md`, not section numbers — both lines state that an element write-site must prove its source within the declared bound) — is permanent compiler prove-or-reject and ships here, independent of the runtime. Distinctly, the element-ingress governance — every element entering via add, enqueue, push, insert, put, append, or a default literal being governed against the inner-type bound, with an out-of-bound value recoverably discarding the working copy — is a deferred runtime feature, RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER. Rule-2 and Rule-4 are two of the numbered semantic rules of the collection element-bound design: Rule 2 is the ingress governance just described — every element entering the collection is checked against the inner-type bound — and Rule 4 is the read-site discharge, under which a value read out of a collection is taken to satisfy the inner-type bound. The compiler admits the shipped Rule-4 read-site discharge now, relying on the §0.7 ingress governance (Rule 2) the runtime will enforce; that ingress enforcement is the deferred half captured in §11. This is admit-now / defer-enforcement, not a compiler over-rejection awaiting the runtime.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `default []` / `default ["a"]` on set, queue, stack, log, and bag compile clean (and on `list` as today); the same default on queue-by or lookup rejects with the canonical message — verified via `precept_compile`.
- A list literal in a global `rule` body emits PRE0044.
- `enqueue PQ Go.V by Go.V` (wrong element type), `put Lk Go.V = Go.T` (wrong key and value), and `insert Lst Go.V at 0` (wrong element) each emit a type-mismatch where they compile clean today.
- An `into`-target no longer emits a false PRE0158.

- `queue of string by string` (a non-orderable key) and `set of string ascending` (a direction modifier on a non-queue-by kind) each emit a diagnostic where they compile clean today.
- `coll.at(A / B)` collects the divisor obligation (the BUG-032 instance) — confirmed via `precept_compile`.
- A `set` in an interpolation hole (`"All: {Tags}"`) emits PRE0051; `dotnet test` green.

### Slice 5.4 — Modifiers: compatibility matrix, flag-modifier proof participation, ordered-comparison routing

**Work:** Close the 11 modifier-surface rows. The compile-side modifier work is proof participation, compatibility enforcement, and duplicate detection:

- **Flag-modifier write-site proof participation** (`nonnegative` / `positive` / `nonzero`): today these flags are read-side facts only — `set N = -1` on a `nonnegative` field compiles clean. Verified at `ProofEngine.Intervals.cs:208-216`, which folds the flag into operand intervals but never creates a rejection; `nonzero` contributes nothing at all, the NotEquals hole at `:225`. The fix generates the write-site lower-bound containment obligation from the flags' `ProofSatisfactions` metadata (`Modifiers.cs:73-112`). The `nonzero` disequality is covered by the locked octagon-style narrowing from the relational-rules-and-bounds design; `nonnegative` and `positive` are the ≥0 and >0 lower bounds respectively. This is the modifier-equivalent of the band-edge enforcement — preserve the carries-proof distinction: these are genuine proof-carrying modifiers, not the reclassified bands of owner decision D2.
- **`ordered`-modifier comparison routing:** comparing values of an unordered choice is enforced as a proof obligation (PRE0112), and that obligation fails open in `when` guards — one of the 5 guard-slot holes closed by Phase 2's walk. This slice confirms the modifier obligation registers at the newly-walked guard slot. Also reconcile the spec §3.8 row, which names a type-stage TypeMismatch, to the actual proof-stage PRE0112 mechanism — a doc-drift fix — and fix the raw-AST `''Low''` render.
- **Modifier-type compatibility matrix:** complete the per-type compatibility enforcement. Extend duplicate-modifier detection to collections — PRE0036 (`DuplicateModifier`) fires for field modifiers but not collection modifiers. Handle non-literal arithmetic modifier values: `min 2 + 3` is silently accepted and inert today — either constant-fold it into `DeclaredMin` or reject it, and file the currently-unfiled case.
- **min/max emission split:** declared-bound containment violations currently emit PRE0078 with the "exceeded the representable range" message. Splitting that code into declared-bound containment versus representability is the Phase-1 PRE0078 split/rename; confirm it lands exactly once — coordinate with Phase 1 Slice 1.3 and do not duplicate the work.

**Boundary note:** the `DesugarsToRule` generated-rationale text in fault messages is RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER — the rationale's consumer is the runtime fault message. Verified: `DesugarsToRule: true` on all 11 modifiers at `Modifiers.cs`, with zero pipeline-synthesis consumers and 2 cosmetic/display consumers. The compile-side proof participation is in scope here; the because-rationale surfacing defers.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `set N = -1` on a `nonnegative` field, `set P = 0` on a `positive` field, and `set Z = 0` on a `nonzero` field each emit a proven-violation where they compile clean today — these are the provable cases; unprovable cases reject — verified via `precept_compile`.
- `state C terminal terminal` and a duplicated collection modifier both emit PRE0036.
- `min 2 + 3` either folds into `DeclaredMin` and is then enforced, or rejects — it is no longer silently inert.
- An unordered-choice comparison inside a `when` guard now emits PRE0112, closed by the Phase-2 guard walk; the message renders source text, not raw AST.
- No declared-bound containment violation carries the "representable range" message; `dotnet build` / `dotnet test` green.

**Doc-touch:**

### Slice 5.5 — Rules + ensures: condition typing, scope enforcement, because-interpolation binding, guard-aware satisfiability

**Work:** Close the 18 rules/ensures rows. These are type-checker, parser, and satisfiability-scan fixes:

- **Rule / ensure condition must be boolean:** `rule Amount because "x"` and `in Draft ensure Amount …` (a non-boolean condition) compile clean today. `TypeChecker.Normalization.cs:95/152/230` resolve the condition with no `ResultType==Boolean` check, even though the guard IS checked. Add the boolean-result check in `PopulateRules` / `PopulateEnsures`, mirroring the guard's existing PRE0018 path — PRE0018 is `TypeMismatch`, the general type-mismatch diagnostic, which the guard check already emits. This is governance-relevant: a non-boolean rule's runtime verdict is undefined.
- **Rules cannot reference event arguments; state ensures are field-scope:** `rule Submit.approver != "" because …` and `in Draft ensure Submit.approver …` compile clean today. PRE0050 (`EventArgOutOfScope`) fires only when a different event is in scope; with no event in scope there is no gate at all. Extend the PRE0050 gate to the no-event-in-scope case.
- **because-clause interpolation binding (the obligation-position fix):** today the interpolation holes in a `because` message are discarded at parse time — `src/Precept/Pipeline/Parser.Expressions.cs:545-573` flattens interpolated because-messages to text with every `HoleSegment` replaced by the literal `"{}"`. As a result, `because "{NoSuchField} {Tags} {Amount + "x"}"` — an undefined name, a collection, and a type error — emits zero diagnostics. The fix: retain the interpolation segments in `BecauseClauseSlot`, name-bind and type-check each hole (scalar-only per §3.6), and carry a `TypedInterpolatedString` into `TypedRule`/`TypedEnsure.Message`. This is the "2 because-message slots" obligation-position family from Phase 2 — register the holes as walked positions. The same treatment applies to the `reject` reason slot, a third slot of the same shape, handled in Slice 5.6.
- **Guard-aware satisfiability scans:** make the contradiction scan (PRE0155) guard-aware. Today guards are stripped before pair intersection, so `rule Amount >= 10 when LoanType == "conventional"` plus `rule Amount <= 3 when LoanType == "jumbo"` — provably-disjoint guards — emit a false PRE0155 (verified by probe Test10, one of the numbered `precept_compile` probes). Minimum fix: only compare unguarded-versus-unguarded pairs, or conjoin the guards. The same family touches the unconditional-relational-rule discharge (spec §0.6 item 2): `TryFlowNarrowingProof` makes the guard-sourced and rule-sourced branches either/or (`ProofEngine.Strategies.cs:1127-1129`). A "row" here is a single declared rule or ensure line; because of the either/or, when the row carrying an obligation has any guard at all — even one unrelated to the obligation — that row guard suppresses all rule-sourced relations. Make it union/fall-through. This is the Phase-3 relational-rules work — cross-reference it, and build it once: in Phase 3 if it lands there, otherwise here.
- **BUG-031 guard obligation:** faulting arithmetic in a rule, ensure, or transition guard collects no obligation today. This is closed by the Phase-2 guard-slot walk; this slice confirms rule/ensure-guard registration. Cross-references Phase 2.
- **Satisfiability message rendering** (PRE0154/0155/0159/0082/0153/0164): route the sibling `FormatGuardText` callers through `DescribeExpression`, as the BUG-029 and BUG-027 fixes did. Today they embed raw `TypedExpression` ToString output — hundreds of characters of AST dump.

**Boundary note (Frank-flagged CANON_DESIGN fork):** whether a provably-unsatisfiable cross-transition ensure — a `to`/`in` ensure on a never-written field, where every fire is statically guaranteed to fail (verified by probe Test11, another of the numbered `precept_compile` probes) — should be a compile-time definition-incoherence error is a §0.6 obligation decision, not a clean runtime punt. It is genuine prove-or-reject territory: the definition is incoherent regardless of runtime, and a complete runtime cannot rescue a never-satisfiable ensure. Surface it as a CANON_DESIGN §0.6 decision. Distinctly, the runtime enforcement of a satisfiable cross-transition ensure — evaluating it against the post-transition working copy at fire time and refusing on actual failure — is RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER under admit-now / defer-enforcement, with a §11 capture; this is the same treatment bands get and it is not a compiler over-rejection, because a fully-built runtime evaluates the ensure and refuses only when it actually fails. **Do not build a new compile-time obligation here without routing the §0.6 decision through `/design`** — per the Pre-Design Owner Consultation gate, this is new obligation surface.

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `rule Amount because "x"` and the non-boolean `in Draft ensure Amount …` each emit a TypeMismatch — verified via `precept_compile`.
- `rule Submit.approver != "" because …` and its state-ensure analog each emit EventArgOutOfScope where they compile clean today.
- `because "{NoSuchField} …"` emits the undefined-name, collection-in-interpolation, and type-error diagnostics for its holes — the holes are now bound and type-checked — verified via `precept_compile`.
- Provably-disjoint guarded rules no longer emit a false PRE0155; an unconditional `rule X > Y` discharges a dependent obligation sitting in a row — a declared rule or ensure line — that carries an unrelated guard.
- A faulting guard collects the divisor obligation — the BUG-031 instance, via the Phase-2 walk.
- Satisfiability diagnostics render source text, not raw AST; `dotnet test` green.

**Doc-touch:**
- `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` §2.2 (rule/ensure condition must be boolean), §3A.1 (rules are field-scope and cannot reference event args), §3.5 (the expression-scope table), §0.6 items 7-11 (guard-aware contradiction/satisfiability; reconcile the "Specification-only" mislabel against HEAD).
- `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` — PRE0155 guard-awareness; the satisfiability rendering pass; the because-hole diagnostics.
- `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` — the rule-sourced/guard-sourced union for flow narrowing.
- `docs/Working/bugs.md` — flip BUG-029 (satisfiability rendering); the BUG-025/026 disposition rides Slice 5.7.
- **Runtime-canon capture (deferred):** `docs/runtime/evaluator.md §7.6/§10` — cross-transition ensure enforcement against the post-transition working copy; and record in `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` that compile-time proof of cross-transition ensures is NOT obligated under the current §0.6, pending the surfaced §0.6 fork.

### Slice 5.6 — States / transitions / events / actions / access-modes

**Work:** Close the 25 states/actions rows. These are parser, type-checker, and structural-validation fixes:

- **Duplicate state modifier** (`state C terminal terminal`): extend PRE0036 (`DuplicateModifier`) emission to state-modifier lists.
- **Wildcard mixed into a comma list** (`from any, A` / `in any, A`): today this dies in a PRE0009 parse-error cascade, and the PRE0128 (`StateListContainsWildcard`) checker branch at `TypeChecker.Normalization.cs:299-309` is dead code, because `ParseStateTarget` (`Parser.cs:849-900`) returns on `any` and breaks out of the comma loop. Either teach the parser to consume `any` inside a list so PRE0128 fires, or emit PRE0128 from the parser directly. Either way, kill the cascade, per the §3.9 one-diagnostic-per-root-cause rule.
- **`reject <reason>` outcome typing plus interpolation:** name-bind and type-check the reject-reason interpolation holes — a third obligation-position slot of the same shape as because-clauses; `reject "reject {NotAField} bad"` compiles clean today. Also decide the StringExpr-versus-literal-only canon fork: the spec says any StringExpr with a TypeMismatch backstop, while the implementation and catalog allow only a string literal with interpolation (`src/Precept/Pipeline/Parser.Expressions.cs:779`). Surface the StringExpr-versus-literal fork to the owner — it is a canon-internal contradiction, classified CANON_DESIGN — before changing the parser surface.
- **Action write-site typing holes** — the states-face of the collection holes in Slice 5.3, expressed per-action: the `remove F at N` index type; the `enqueue/append … by P` value and key types; the `into G` capture-binding type and optionality; `put F K = V` key and value; and the `insert F E at N` index type together with the literal-`0`-discharge fix — a literal index `0` always satisfies the `[0,count]` range but emits a spurious PRE0100. Separately, `dequeue F by H` priority-queue semantics is a canon-internal contradiction — the spec describes a key-match selector while the catalog hover describes an output capture. Surface the semantics fork to the owner before wiring (CANON_DESIGN).
- **Access-mode completeness:** implement the two missing halves of `RedundantAccessMode`. The rule numbers here are the numbered access-mode rules in `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md`: rule 4c's readonly-on-readonly-baseline half and rule 4b's guarded-readonly-requires-editable-baseline half are the two missing pieces. Today `Structural.cs:69-87` fires only the writable-plus-editable case and skips all guarded modes. Also: a computed field in `modify … editable` emits PRE0038 (`ComputedFieldNotWritable`); and fix the `writable`/`editable` spec naming drift — the implementation keyword and diagnostic are `editable` / `EditableOnEventArg` (PRE0041).
- **Initial-event correctness:** key initial-event uniqueness on the `evt.IsInitial` declarations rather than on construction rows — the current PRE0147 escape is that two `initial` events, where only one has an `on` row, compile clean. Delete the `hasLegacyInitialPath` shim (`Structural.cs:133-137`) so PRE0145 (`InitialEventInTransitionRow`) fires on the legacy lane. Reword the message of PRE0146 (`ZeroConstructionRows`, the diagnostic for a precept declaring no construction rows). Remove the dead PRE0014 (`EventHandlerDoesNotSupportGuard`) or re-wire it — and surface the handler-guards canon fork to the owner: spec §2.2 forbids handler guards while the catalog and §3A.5 allow them.
- **Construction-row grammar exclusion message:** emit a targeted diagnostic ("construction rows cannot transition") instead of a bare PRE0009 resync.
- **States-face satisfiability message rendering** (PRE0153/0154): route through `DescribeExpression`, the BUG-029 family.

**Boundary note (admit-now / defer-enforcement — §11 capture):** three items are RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER: omit-clears-on-entry — the behavior that a field declared `omit` in a state is reset to its default value on any transition into that state, which is rule 5 of the same numbered access-mode/omit rule list in `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md`; the readonly/editable runtime patch enforcement (§3A.2 outcome 8); and the Create verdict space (Created/Rejected). For each, the compiler admits the definition — it carries the access-mode and omit tables, with `TypedAccessMode` in `SemanticIndex` — and the enforcement is a runtime-execution feature deferred with a named capture. That is the same treatment the bands reclassified under owner decisions D2/D3 get: admitted at compile time, enforcement deferred to the runtime. There is no compiler rejection held against the missing runtime: these are governance and execution actions, not compile-time provability obligations. Separately, actions-before-reject — pre-reject mutations being semantically dead — is a CANON_DESIGN fork between refusing at compile time and correcting the catalog shape. **Surface it; do not silently pick.**

**Exit criteria (compiler-completable):**
- `state C terminal terminal` emits PRE0036; `from any, A on Go` emits a single targeted PRE0128 (or a clean dedicated parse diagnostic), not a cascade.
- The action write-site typing holes — `remove … at` with a non-integer index, `enqueue … by` with wrong types, `into G` with a wrong type, `put`/`insert` with wrong types — each emit a type mismatch where they compile clean today; `insert … at 0` with a literal index is discharged, with no spurious PRE0100.
- Access-mode: readonly-on-readonly-baseline and guarded-readonly-on-non-editable both emit PRE0043; a computed field in `modify … editable` emits PRE0038.
- Two `initial` events, one without an `on` row, emit PRE0147; the legacy initial-state transition-row lane emits PRE0145.
- The reject-reason interpolation holes are type-checked — `reject "reject {NotAField} bad"` emits the undefined-name diagnostic — verified via `precept_compile`; `dotnet test` green.

**Gate:** four owner forks — the StringExpr-versus-literal `reject` fork, the `dequeue by H` semantics fork, the handler-guards fork, and the actions-before-reject fork — are each surfaced to the owner (CANON_DESIGN, at Tier-2/3 consultation depth) **before** the corresponding parser or catalog surface change. The conservative default — keeping the rejecting, stricter behavior — keeps the code on the safe path until each fork resolves.

### Slice 5.7 — Binder / type-checker + graph analyzer + the diagnostic-policy bundle

**Work:** Close the binder/type-checker rows (17) and the graph-analyzer rows (10), plus the unspecified diagnostic-policy cluster.

*Binder / type-checker:*
- **Bare-identifier resolution order:** `NameBinder.ResolveIdentifier` (`NameBinder.cs:748-763`) checks event args before fields. A bare name that collides with both is therefore recorded as `ArgTarget` in `SymbolTable.References`, while the type checker resolves it to the field — so language-server navigation and semantic-token consumers get the wrong target. Align the binder to field-before-arg (or record bare arg references as unresolved), and rewrite `name-binder.md §7.3`, which documents the stale "args shadow fields" model.
- **Computed expression type must match field type:** `field D as string <- A + 1` (with A an integer) compiles clean today — the computed path (`TypeChecker.cs:1037-1074`) never calls `IsAssignable`, unlike the default path (`:937`). Add the check plus a TypeMismatch.
- **Choice element-type checks on declarations** — PRE0090 (`ChoiceMissingElementType`) and PRE0088 (`ChoiceElementTypeMismatch`): `field P3 as choice("x","y")` with no `of T`, and `field P5 as choice of integer("nope")`, both compile clean. Either wire the parse-stage checks, or strike the spec rows if untyped-choice inference is now intended — an owner decision. This cross-references Phase 3: the choice-default membership check on the default slot is built there, not here.
- **Binder shadowing / unused-binder lint**, plus the BUG-022 consumer-`*Kind` exhaustiveness family in the binder discriminated-union walks. At `NameBinder.cs:371/547/597`, the `ParsedExpression`/`ParsedAction` DU switches have no default arm — a new subtype would be silently unwalked, meaning fail-open reference loss. The analyzer extension itself is Phase-6 work; this slice enrolls and genericizes the binder sites.

*Graph analyzer:*
- **True reverse-reachability for irreversible states (PRE0110):** today `BuildBackEdgeViolations` (`GraphAnalyzer.cs:626-668`) inspects only direct edges, so a multi-hop return path — A→B[irreversible]→C→A — compiles clean despite structurally violating irreversibility. Implement forward-reach-set ∩ ancestor-set = ∅; the reverse adjacency and BFS machinery already exist in `ComputeDeadEnds`. Decide and align the ancestor notion: BFS-tree ancestor versus any-earlier-reachable ancestor.
- **Stateless `UnhandledEvent` (PRE0081):** the stateless path (`GraphAnalyzer.cs:31-69`) builds coverage entries but never emits PRE0081 for a handler-less stateless event — emit it.
- **Catalog-driven event-modifier dispatch:** either wire `InitialEventCompatibility`'s analysis through the dispatch — `GraphAnalyzer.cs:190-217` has an empty placeholder arm — or remove the dead `GraphAnalysisKind` hook. This is catalog discipline, the same family as BUG-022.
- **Dead-end → dead-end vacuous-Proved candidate:** `ProofEngine.cs:1186-1196` marks obligations on dead-end→dead-end transitions as `Disposition=Proved/Strategy=Literal`. But dead-end states are reachable and those transitions execute — this is a candidate False-Proved entry. Route it to an honest disposition. The unreachable-FROM suppression at `:1175-1184` is genuinely vacuous and gets `ProofStrategy.Vacuous` from Phase 1; the dead-end variant is **not** vacuous — flag and fix it as a soundness item. This cross-references the Phase-1 `ProofStrategy.Vacuous` work and the Phase-3 False-Proved sweep.
- **Graph-analyzer doc syncs:** reconcile the `graph-analyzer.md §4` proof-forwarding consumption table — 3 of the 5 fact subtypes are produced and then dropped: `DominancePathFact`, `EventCoverageFact`, and `TerminalCompletenessFact` are never read. Also reconcile the `GraphEvent.IsInitial` / `EventHandlers` construction-event drift, the `StateGraph` record-shape drift, and the Appendix diagnostic-code inventory (add codes 119/125/126/158). For the construction-event items the direction is doc-follows-impl: the implementation is the deliberate, owner-tracked current canon.

*Diagnostic-policy bundle:*

For example, PRE0159's unsatisfiable-rule Warning-versus-Error choice was never explicitly decided — it is "defaulted by analogy," carried over from resembling diagnostics rather than set by a recorded ruling — and BUG-025 concerns relational-leaf extraction. This is a `/design`-sized cluster, at Tier-2/3 owner-consultation depth, and it is guarantee-adjacent.
