### Phase 5 coverage note (no construct dropped silently)

Every construct-surface input is placed. **Collections (22 rows)** land in Slice 5.3, with the qualifier-source faces cross-referenced to Phase 3 Slice 3.2 and element-ingress deferred to §11. **Modifiers (11 rows)** land in Slice 5.4, with the PRE0078 split coordinated with Phase 1 Slice 1.3 and the because-rationale deferred. **Expression (23 rows)** are distributed across four slices — Slice 5.2 (presence and conditional narrowing), Slice 5.3 (collection accessors, `F for K`, contains), Slice 5.4 (ordered comparison, flag participation), and Slice 5.5 (guard obligations, rule-sourced discharge) — because the expression-surface rows are construct-spanning by nature and each lands in the construct slice that owns it. **Rules/ensures (18 rows)** land in Slice 5.5. **States/actions (25 rows)** land in Slice 5.6. **Binder/type-checker (17 rows)** and **graph analyzer (10 rows)** land in Slice 5.7.

The 2c-ii flagship (Slice 5.1) is the locked-design-unbuilt item, BUG-020; the conditional-totality proof half plus the computed-field fold (Slice 5.2) are the remaining locked-design-unbuilt items in this phase.

The PARKED items remain parked. The first is two-axis element bounds — element bounds on the second axis of the keyed collection kinds (the queue-by ordering key `P`, and the lookup key `K` / value `V`); the grammar slot is reserved, and nothing further is built. The second is the sign-set element-band gap. A sign-set proof is a discharge that rests on what is known about a value's sign — division-by-zero is ruled out when the divisor is known to exclude zero, and an index lower-bound is discharged when the index is known nonnegative. Such proofs over a quantifier binding still read the field's own modifiers rather than the element band (the bound declared on the collection's elements), so a discharge the element band would justify is missed; this errs in the safe over-reject direction, is low priority, and may ride along here as COMPILER_CODE if cheap.

The deferred runtime features — 2c-ii ingress enforcement, element-ingress governance, cross-transition ensure enforcement, omit-clears and readonly patch enforcement, because-rationale surfacing, and the conditional-totality opcode lowering, each under admit-now / defer-enforcement; plus the collect-all sweep as a plain RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER — each carry a named `docs/runtime/*.md` capture pointer in §11. The compiler admits the fault-safe operation now and defers the enforcement, holding no over-rejection against the unbuilt runtime. **Nothing dropped silently.**

---

**Phase 5 exit:** the seven per-slice exit criteria hold, by reference — Slice 5.1 (2c-ii field-reference bounds prove-or-reject, termination, the GATE-M disposition recorded), Slice 5.2 (conditional-totality narrowing plus the computed-field default fold), Slice 5.3 (the collection typing holes closed; PRE0099 and PRE0051 live), Slice 5.4 (flag-modifier write-site proof participation; the PRE0036 and PRE0112 extensions), Slice 5.5 (rule/ensure condition typing, because-hole binding, guard-aware satisfiability), Slice 5.6 (states/actions write-site typing, access-mode completeness, initial-event correctness), and Slice 5.7 (the binder resolution order, graph reverse-reachability, and the diagnostic-policy bundle settled) — each verified per its slice via `precept_compile`, `dotnet build`, or `dotnet test`. The surfaced owner forks carry recorded rulings. Every deferred runtime half named in the coverage note carries its §11 capture pointer. **No exit criterion requires the runtime to execute** — everything above is verifiable with the `Evaluator.cs` stubs still throwing `NotImplementedException`.

---

## 9. Phase 6 — Diagnostic completeness + ownership analyzers

**Nature:** COMPILER_CODE + CANON_DESIGN. This phase is pure compiler and build-time work. Every exit criterion is verifiable by one of three means: `dotnet build` (the Roslyn analyzers this phase adds run when the compiler itself is compiled), `dotnet test`, or `precept_compile` (demonstrating that a diagnostic emission now fires on a `.precept` probe file). **No runtime dependency**: the reciprocal check — that the runtime fault actually fires when the guarantee is absent — belongs to the deferred evaluator's acceptance facet in §11 (Runtime-Coding Handoff), and is deliberately not a Phase-6 exit criterion.

**Goal:** Bring the diagnostic surface to canon-completeness, and make emission **ownership** structurally enforced rather than convention-enforced. Concretely, the phase does five things:

- (a) Apply emit-or-retire to every declared `DiagnosticCode`, and verify that every `[StaticallyPreventable]` code is live rather than dead. A dead preventable code is a hole in Principle 11 — the compiler claims a guarantee ("this runtime fault is statically prevented") that it does not actually back.
- (b) Close 43 spec gaps in total: the 28 proof-engine/diagnostic spec gaps, plus the 15 remaining gaps in the pipeline tail — header handling, parser recovery, `Compilation` orchestration, grammar generation, and catalog metadata. This 43 is distinct from the separate register of 43 obligations recovered from the triage of prior working documents — the distinction Frank finding A5 flagged.
- (c) Stand up two new Roslyn analyzers: the single-stage-ownership analyzer (each diagnostic code is emitted only by the pipeline component its metadata declares) and the BUG-022 consumer-`*Kind` exhaustiveness analyzer (a new catalog member forces every consuming switch to handle it).
- (d) Enrich the witness and message surface attached to rejected proofs — this is diagnostic-inspectability, serving Principle 4, the commitment that the domain author can see why a proof failed — without resurrecting the deleted re-verification pass.
- (e) Merge the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers — this phase's emit-or-retire dispositions, the conformance suite's `[Covers]` coverage tags (Phase 7), and the emission-ownership audit (the exhaustive audit of which pipeline component emits each diagnostic code, carried in the prior working documents under the label "Stage-2") — into ONE register.

**Dependencies:**
- **GATE-M (PRE0141 bound-expression ownership)** — GATE-M is the gate on which pipeline component owns diagnostic PRE0141, the bound-expression code whose ownership is currently split; among the lettered owner-decision gates, GATE-M is the one that resolves at Phase-5 start rather than in Phase 0. It must be resolved at Phase-5 start because the Slice-6.4 ownership analyzer's single carve-out depends on the answer — on whether Phase 5's 2c-ii work, bounds desugaring to rules, dissolved PRE0141's split ownership. If GATE-M resolved it, the analyzer ships with **zero** carve-outs; if it did not, the analyzer ships with exactly one.
- **Phase 1 Slice 1.4 (honest registry)** — the `[StaticallyPreventable]` liveness sweep in Slice 6.2 asserts against the fault-code registry as it stands after the Phase-1 repartition, together with the post-trim bijective core. Two registry snapshots are in play here, and they must not be conflated. As of HEAD today, `FaultCode.cs` carries 15 members; the Phase-1 repartition then removes the Length and Count codes and adds the Index and Key codes. Slice 6.2's 15-member enumeration names the family as it stands today; the sweep itself runs against the repartitioned registry, in which the repartition-removed codes no longer carry `[StaticallyPreventable]` and so must not appear as live-preventable. Running the sweep before the registry is honest would mean chasing codes that are still moving.
- **Phase 5 floor/construct completeness** — the emit-or-retire sweep should run after the construct-completeness emissions land. Phases 4 and 5 wire roughly 6 codes that are currently on the coverage allow-list — `Gate1AllowList` in `DiagnosticCoverageAllowLists.cs`, the list of declared diagnostic codes exempted from the build-time every-code-has-an-emission-site gate, which Slice 6.1 walks entry by entry. The roughly 6 codes are `KeyPresenceSafety`, `KeyUniquenessGuard`, and the choice and temporal-precision codes. Sequencing the sweep after them means the residual no-emission set this phase resolves is the genuine pipeline-tail remainder, not work Phase 4/5 already owes.
- There is no hard ordering against Phase 7. The conformance program (Phase 7) consumes this phase's ONE register.

**Effort class:** L (12–18 days). The bound is set by two things: the emit-or-retire sweep, in which each allow-list entry gets an explicit emit-or-retire disposition, and the two new analyzers, each of which needs its own Roslyn test fixture.

---

### Slice 6.1 — Emit-or-retire sweep: every declared code lives or is retired (COMPILER_CODE)

- **Work:** Reconcile the declared-versus-emitted diagnostic surface against the committed HEAD of the repository, and drive the set of codes with no emission site to zero — each one either gains a real emission or is explicitly retired.
  - **Re-derive the count against HEAD, not memory.** `diagnostic-system.md:404` currently states **164 active codes**; the "148-vs-162" figure carried by the prior plan is stale. Recompute the live count from `DiagnosticCode.cs` together with the `DiagnosticCoverageScanner`. A recorded lesson applies here: a literal grep for `Diagnostics.Create(DiagnosticCode.X)` is incomplete, so the count must come from the scanner's real detection patterns. Pattern 1 finds literal `Diagnostics.Create` call sites. Patterns 2 and 2b find catalog-mediated fields — codes carried on `CIDiagnosticCode`, `FormatErrorCode`, `SemanticErrorCode`, and `ProofRequirementMeta` — per `DiagnosticCoverageScanner.cs`. The scanner does **NOT** detect dynamically-dispatched emission sites; there is no third pattern. Those sites must be enumerated by hand per the dynamic-dispatch caveat, or the count silently misses exactly them. The over-claim at `diagnostic-system.md:531` about the scanner's patterns was corrected on 2026-07-02 — the doc now states Patterns 1/2/2b plus a hand-enumerated dynamic-dispatch residue; this slice's doc-touch verifies that correction has not regressed. Finally, update the count at `diagnostic-system.md:404` to match HEAD.
  - **Walk the `Gate1AllowList` (in `DiagnosticCoverageAllowLists.cs`) entry by entry** — that allow-list is the concrete emit-or-retire backlog. Each entry gets exactly one disposition, and the whole family is enumerated rather than only the entries someone happened to trip over (a recorded lesson). The dispositions:
    - **Wire — the emission is owed, not yet built, and not owned by another phase:** `MissingOrderingKey` (its reserved code is PRE0151; today the condition is caught generically by `ScalarOperationOnCollection`), `NonOrderableCollectionExtreme`, and `ChoiceElementTypeMismatch` / `ChoiceMissingElementType`. *Confirm these are not already owed by Phase 5's collections/choice work. If they are owed there, this slice only removes the allow-list entry and adds the no-regression test — the existing stale-allow-list gate (`Precept0029`/`PRECEPT0029` StaleAllowList) already fires once the emission lands.*
    - **Retire — subsumed by another code, or never to be built.** Three entries fall here. First, `NullInNonNullableContext`: the allow-list says "retired, subsumed by PRE0116", PRE0116 being the unproved-presence diagnostic. The disposition is to remove the enum member. One cross-slice note attaches to it: the stale `NullInNonNullableContext` partner entry in `fault-system.md` was already reconciled in Phase 1 Slice 1.5 against `FaultCode.cs:23 UnprovedPresenceRequirement`. Second, `EventHandlerDoesNotSupportGuard`: the allow-list says "retired — guards now valid on all on-rows". Third, `FunctionArgConstraintViolation` / `InvalidInterpolationCoercion`: the allow-list says `TypeMismatch` fires instead, as a precision upgrade. For each of those two, this slice must decide whether to keep the code as the higher-precision target and wire it, or retire it; the one forbidden outcome is leaving the claim half-made.
    - **Park-honest — no emission, but the code stays, documented as a known-limitation backstop:** `DivisionByZero` / `SqrtOfNegative`. The allow-list says "no emission site wired," but these are the proof-engine fault-link codes — the targets of the bijective fault-to-diagnostic pairing in `StaticallyPreventableMap` — not type-stage diagnostics an author sees directly. Confirm that their role is the bijective-fault target rather than a directly-emitted author diagnostic, and record that disposition instead of forcing a spurious emission.
    - **Catalog-mediated — already live, but invisible to the scanner:** `InvalidTypedConstantContent`. It is already emitted through the Pattern-2 fallthrough; the allow-list entry reflects a scanner limitation, not a gap. Keep the entry, with the explanatory comment.
    - **Owned elsewhere — Phase 4/5:** the four temporal-precision codes (`InvalidTimezoneId`, `UnqualifiedPeriodArithmetic`, `MissingTemporalUnit`, `FractionalUnitValue`) plus `KeyPresenceSafety` / `KeyUniquenessGuard`. Phases 4 and 5 wire these. This slice only verifies that the allow-list entries disappear once those phases land — the `PRECEPT0029` StaleAllowList gate enforces that automatically.
  - **The `NonAssociativeComparison` label** — a parse-stage code (the prior plan cited it as PRE0xxx without pinning the number; its entry is at `Diagnostics.cs:103`): apply the label/message reconciliation the prior plan noted. This is a drift-only fix — it reconciles the code's recorded label and message text, and changes no behavior.
  - Each disposition is recorded as one ledger row. No allow-list entry survives the slice without an explicit disposition.
- **Exit criteria:**
  - `dotnet build` is green with both coverage analyzers clean: `PRECEPT0027` (every declared code has an emission site or an allow-list entry) and `PRECEPT0029` (no stale allow-list entry).
  - The `Gate1AllowList` contains only entries with a live root-cause disposition — owed-elsewhere wiring, catalog-mediated-but-scanner-blind, or documented backstop. Every entry that said "no emission site wired" is either wired (verified by a `precept_compile` probe that now produces the code) or retired (the enum member removed).
  - The active-code count in `diagnostic-system.md` matches the freshly recomputed HEAD count.
  - No exit criterion asserts a runtime fault firing.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` — the active-code count in §DiagnosticCode Registry; the registry row of any code wired or retired, plus the `NonAssociativeComparison` label fix; and **verify the scanner-pattern statement at `:531`** — it was corrected on 2026-07-02 to state Patterns 1/2/2b only, plus hand-enumerated dynamic-dispatch sites, and this slice confirms no regression. Also confirm `fault-system.md` carries no residual `NullInNonNullableContext` reference (Phase 1 Slice 1.5 already reconciled the member-count drift). Any retirement that removes an enum member follows the catalog-as-spec discipline: the member is removed first, and everything downstream derives from the catalog.

### Slice 6.2 — `[StaticallyPreventable]` liveness sweep: no dead preventable code (COMPILER_CODE)

- **Work:** Enumerate the **whole** `[StaticallyPreventable]` family on `FaultCode.cs` — 15 members as the registry stands at HEAD today, before the Phase-1 repartition, each carrying a `[StaticallyPreventable(DiagnosticCode.X)]` partner attribute naming the compile-time diagnostic that prevents it — and give every member an explicit live-or-dead disposition. A `[StaticallyPreventable]` code with **no live emission** is a **hole in Principle 11**: the registry claims "this runtime fault is statically prevented by code X" while code X never fires, so the guarantee is unbacked. (A recorded lesson makes this explicit: every `[StaticallyPreventable]` code must be verified live, not dead or stubbed.)
  - For each of the 15 partners, cross the partner `DiagnosticCode` against the emitted set Slice 6.1 established. The partners, enumerated from today's pre-repartition registry (the next bullet reconciles this list against the Phase-1 repartition), are: `DivisionByZero`, `SqrtOfNegative`, `UnprovedPresenceRequirement` (which maps to `UnexpectedNull`), `UnguardedCollectionAccess`/`Mutation`, `QualifierMismatch`, `FunctionArgConstraintViolation`, `TypeMismatch`/`UndeclaredField`/`InvalidMemberAccess`/`FunctionArityMismatch`, `NumericOverflow`, `OutOfRange`, `LengthBoundViolation`, and `CountBoundViolation`.
  - **Reconcile against the Phase-1 registry repartition.** After Phase 1 Slice 1.4, `OutOfRange`, `LengthBoundViolation`, and `CountBoundViolation` are removed from `[StaticallyPreventable]` and from the bijective core — that is the band reclassification of owner decision D2 — so they must **not** appear in this sweep as live-preventable. `NumericOverflow` is kept, but as a **documented known-fault backstop whose `[StaticallyPreventable]` partner is an honest gap** — that is owner decision D1, overflow parked. Its partner diagnostic is intentionally missing, and the sweep records it as such rather than counting it as a Principle-11 violation. The sweep must encode this distinction structurally, so that a parked-by-design gap is never flagged as a hole and a genuinely dead code is never excused by pointing at the parked one.
  - **Frank finding B1 — reconcile the `PRECEPT0002` analyzer's description string.** The catalog enforces the prevention invariant in **code**: `src/Precept.Analyzers/Precept0002FaultCodeMustHaveStaticallyPreventable.cs:19–24` asserts, of **every** `FaultCode` member, that its diagnostic "prevents it at compile time" and "makes this fault unreachable at runtime." After D1, that sentence is **false canon-in-code for `NumericOverflow`**. The attribute itself correctly stays — `PRECEPT0002` structurally requires it, and the code is still in the bijective pairing and still emitted — but the attribute carries two distinct meanings: the **linkage** (this fault pairs with this diagnostic — still valid) and the **delivered guarantee** ("unreachable at runtime" — now false for the one parked member). `PRECEPT0002`'s text conflates the two. The fix: reword `PRECEPT0002`'s message and description to assert the **linkage invariant** ("every fault must reference its preventing diagnostic") and **stop asserting universal delivered prevention** (drop "makes this fault unreachable at runtime") — OR add an explicit **`known-not-prevented` carve-out** keyed to the D1 parked set, mirroring the existing "design-time linkage" docstring that `StaticallyPreventableMap.cs:22–27` already uses for `UnexpectedNull`.
  - **Frank finding A1 — the general honesty-sweep principle:** when a decision relaxes a guarantee, **every surface that *enforces* that guarantee** is in scope for the honesty sweep — the prose canon **and** the analyzer family (`PRECEPT0002`, `0027`, `0029`, `0019`) — never the docs alone. The B1 fix above is the first application of this principle; the next time a guarantee is relaxed, the same rule applies.
  - Where Slice 6.1 found a partner code dead with no parked justification — for example, a `FunctionArgConstraintViolation` that `TypeMismatch` superseded — the disposition is one of two: **wire it** (restore the guarantee) or **drop the `[StaticallyPreventable]` attribute** (honestly retract the claim). A silent dead preventable is never left standing.
  - **Mechanize the check** (Frank finding A3 — derive, don't hand-maintain): fold the liveness check into the existing coverage-gate family rather than keeping a parallel hand-maintained list. The cleanest home is a meta-test or analyzer that intersects the keys of `StaticallyPreventableMap.InverseAttributeMap` with the `DiagnosticCoverageScanner`'s emitted set, and fails on any empty intersection that is not explicitly parked. Reuse `Precept0027`'s scanner; do not fork a second scanner.
- **Exit criteria:**
  - A meta-test (or analyzer) asserts that every `[StaticallyPreventable]` partner `DiagnosticCode` is either in the emitted set OR carries an explicit parked-known-fault disposition — `NumericOverflow` only, per D1. `dotnet test` is green.
  - Each of the 15 enumerated partners has a recorded live/parked disposition row; there are zero silent dead preventables.
  - **(B1)** `PRECEPT0002`'s description and message no longer assert universal delivered prevention — either they assert only the linkage invariant, or they carry the `known-not-prevented` carve-out for the D1 parked set. A string-grep over `src/Precept.Analyzers/*.cs` finds **no in-code sentence claiming `NumericOverflow` is prevented or unreachable**.
  - Everything is verifiable today, via compiling the compiler plus `dotnet test`. The reciprocal check — that the runtime actually emits the fault — is **out** of scope; it belongs to the deferred evaluator facet in §11.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` §The `[StaticallyPreventable]`-derived fault map and §Enforcement-what-catches-what — record the liveness gate and the one parked partner; cross-link `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` where the bijective fault chain is described. `docs/runtime/fault-system.md` — confirm that the `NumericOverflow`-as-known-not-prevented disposition, already written in Phase 1 Slice 1.5, is the single source the sweep cites; this slice introduces no new runtime code. **(B1) Code-as-canon edit:** `src/Precept.Analyzers/Precept0002FaultCodeMustHaveStaticallyPreventable.cs` — the message/description reconciliation. This is an in-code honesty edit owed by D1, not a docs-only touch.

### Slice 6.4 — Single-stage diagnostic-ownership Roslyn analyzer (COMPILER_CODE) — **GATE-M-gated**

- **Work:** Build the catalog-declared single-stage-ownership analyzer. The **canon basis** is `diagnostic-system.md:159-161`: `DiagnosticStage` is a *producing-component classification* — "each code is owned by exactly one producing component, and the meta `Stage` names that component honestly." The analyzer makes that invariant structural: for every `Diagnostics.Create(DiagnosticCode.X, …)` emission site, the pipeline component containing the site must equal `GetMeta(X).Stage`.
  - **One carve-out, gated on GATE-M.** The candidate carve-out is `TypedConditional` PRE0141, the bound-expression code whose ownership is split. GATE-M is the gate on that ownership question, and it resolves at Phase-5 start. If Phase 5's 2c-ii work — bounds desugaring to rules — dissolved PRE0141's split ownership, the analyzer ships with **zero** carve-outs; if not, exactly one, recorded with its reason. **Confirm GATE-M's resolution at this slice's start**, before fixing the carve-out count.
  - **Pre-cleanup pass — required before the analyzer can go green, and verified buildable at HEAD.** The 0-or-1 carve-out arithmetic does not hold against HEAD as it stands. Beyond PRE0141, **five** additional live stage disagreements exist. First: `EmptyChoice` is emitted by the type checker while its metadata declares `DiagnosticStage.Parse`. Second through fifth: four proof-engine emissions are declared as `DiagnosticStage.Type` — `CompoundPeriodDenominator`, `IndexBoundsGuard`, `UnguardedCollectionAccess`, and `UnguardedCollectionMutation`. Four of the five are mechanical one-line metadata re-stages, plus syncing `diagnostic-system.md` and the tests that assert stages. But `CompoundPeriodDenominator` is **genuinely dual-emitted** from two components — the type checker and the proof engine — so no single `Stage` value can satisfy both sites. Resolving it requires moving one emission, splitting the code into two, or taking a second carve-out; whichever is chosen, a per-case disposition is recorded. Budget this cleanup inside the slice. The canon invariant already dictates what the fixes must achieve, and forcing exactly this cleanup is the analyzer's stated purpose.
  - **Mediated-code (Pattern-2) policy decision.** Several sites pass a non-literal code — via `CIDiagnosticCode`, `FormatErrorCode`/`SemanticErrorCode`, the proof-requirement subtype-fixed codes, and the numeric dispatch through `GetNumericRequirementDiagnosticCode` — so the per-site check "component equals `GetMeta(code).Stage`" cannot be evaluated from the call site alone. The analyzer needs an explicit policy for these: either resolve the set of codes a mediated site can carry from the literal catalog assignments and require all of them to match the site's component, or perform the ownership check at the catalog-assignment site instead. Decide and record the policy inside the slice.
  - **Tooling emission-site disposition.** Exactly one Tooling-stage emission site exists — `McpToolInternalError` in `tools/Precept.Mcp/Tools/McpToolSafeInvoke.cs` — and it lives in a project the analyzer cannot see, because only `src/Precept/Precept.csproj` carries the analyzer reference. Either add the one-line analyzer reference to `Precept.Mcp.csproj`, or record Tooling-stage sites as explicitly out of the analyzer's scope in this slice's doc-touch. Canon already places that site outside the compile pipeline, so exclusion is defensible — but it must be recorded, not left silent against the "every `Diagnostics.Create` site" exit criterion.
  - **Frank finding A3 — derive, don't switch:** route ownership through the attribute-derived map, i.e. `GetMeta(code).Stage`. The residual hand-written switch in `CreateFaultSiteLink` (in `ProofEngine.Diagnostics.cs`) must **shrink, not grow** — do not trade a backstop smell for the forbidden switch-on-`*Kind`-identity smell. **Zero allow-list** for this analyzer; the only exception mechanism is the single structural carve-out, gated as above.
  - Implement it as a new `PreceptNNNN` analyzer in `src/Precept.Analyzers/`, alongside the existing family (`Precept0027`, `Precept0019`, `Precept0029`), reusing the `DiagnosticCoverageScanner`'s emission-site walk to find each `Create` site and its containing component. One prerequisite: the scanner's public `ScanResult` must be **extended** first. Today it exposes only name sets, with no per-site location or containing symbol, so it must grow per-site symbol and location data before this analyzer can consume it. The walk's internals already hold a semantic model per node, so the extension is contained.
- **Exit criteria:** The analyzer fails `dotnet build` if any `Diagnostics.Create` site's component disagrees with `GetMeta(code).Stage`. The pre-cleanup pass has resolved the five additional HEAD stage disagreements, each with a recorded per-case disposition — a metadata re-stage, a moved emission, a split code, or an additional recorded carve-out for the dual-emitted `CompoundPeriodDenominator`. The analyzer ships with the GATE-M-determined carve-out count (0 or 1, plus the `CompoundPeriodDenominator` carve-out only if that disposition was taken) and zero allow-list. The mediated-code policy and the Tooling-site disposition are both recorded. The existing test corpus plus a deliberately-mismatched fixture prove the analyzer both fires and clears correctly. The line count of the hand-written switch in `CreateFaultSiteLink` did not grow, verified by diff. All verification is compile-of-compiler.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` §DiagnosticStage and §Enforcement-what-catches-what — add the new analyzer to the enforcement table next to `PRECEPT0019`; `docs/compiler/README.md` analyzer inventory, if one exists. No runtime capture.

### Slice 6.5 — BUG-022 consumer-`*Kind` exhaustiveness analyzer + Precept0019 exemption model (COMPILER_CODE; small CANON_DESIGN tail)

- **Work:** Build the consumer-side `*Kind` exhaustiveness analyzer — the BUG-022 family. It gates every switch marked `[HandlesCatalogExhaustively]`, and every consumer of a catalog `*Kind`, so that adding a new catalog member forces every consumer to handle it. This is the consumer-side analog of the existing producer-side analyzer, `Precept0019PipelineCoverageExhaustiveness`. Reuse `Precept0019`'s catalog-member-enumeration machinery and the existing `HandlesCatalogExhaustivelyAttribute` (`src/Precept/HandlesCatalogExhaustivelyAttribute.cs`); do not fork a parallel exhaustiveness walker.
  - **The Precept0019 exemption model** is a small `/design`-sized decision — Tier-1 in the owner-consultation sense, because it is compiler-internal policy. The problem: some consumers legitimately handle only a subset of a catalog discriminated union. Settle the exemption model — an explicit per-consumer opt-out attribute versus an allow-list — as CANON_DESIGN; the downstream emission and gate are then COMPILER_CODE. Per the consultation gate this is **compiler-internal policy, not language surface**, so it is Tier-1 and needs no owner conversation gate. But the exemption model itself still gets a four-leg rationale in its design note: Rationale, Alternatives considered and rejected, Precedent, and Tradeoff accepted.
- **Exit criteria:** The consumer-`*Kind` analyzer fails `dotnet build` when a catalog member is added without updating a consuming exhaustive switch — proven via a fixture that adds a member and a switch that omits it. The exemption model is documented with its four-leg rationale, and the analyzer honors it. `dotnet test` is green. Verification is compile-of-compiler only.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` and `docs/language/catalog-system.md` §Enforcement — add the consumer-`*Kind` analyzer and the exemption model. The design note for the exemption model lands in `docs/Working/` and then promotes per `/promote` once shipped. No runtime capture.

### Slice 6.6 — Witness richness as diagnostic-inspectability (COMPILER_CODE + CANON_DESIGN)

- **Work:** Enrich the message and witness the compiler **already produces** when it rejects a proof, so that a rejection is inspectable per **Principle 4** — the domain author can see *why* the proof failed. This is **diagnostic-inspectability**, and it is categorically distinct from — and must **not** resurrect — the deleted `ProofChecker`/`ProofWitness` verify-don't-trust re-verification architecture. That architecture is marked DO-NOT-RESURRECT; the slice re-grounds against the fault-floor outcome instead — the settled position that the engine is prove-or-reject, not verify-after-the-fact.
  - Scope strictly to enriching the existing rejection messages: surface the obligation's subject, the bound or requirement it failed against, and the discharge strategy that was attempted. All of that data already lives on `ProofObligation`, `FaultSiteLink`, and `ConstraintInfluenceEntry` (`proof-engine.md:271/333/365`); the work is surfacing it, not re-running a checker over the witness. Also address the spec-audit message-quality rows in this family — for example BUG-029, raw AST text appearing in a message. The `DescribeExpression` fix for that bug lives in Phase 3 and is cross-referenced; this slice owns only the proof-rejection message enrichment.
  - The work ships as proof-engine unit tests asserting the richer message content. No new proof verdict is introduced, and no re-verification pass.
- **Exit criteria:** A rejected proof's diagnostic message names the subject, the failed requirement, and the attempted strategy — asserted by proof-engine unit tests plus a `precept_compile` probe on a precept known to reject. `dotnet test` is green. **No `ProofChecker` or `ProofWitness` type is reintroduced** — verified by diff; this is the DO-NOT-RESURRECT guard. No runtime-execution exit criterion.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/proof-engine.md` §Component Mechanics and §Output — the enriched rejection-message contract, with an explicit note that this is inspectability, not re-verification, and that the deleted checker stays deleted; `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` for the message-quality rows touched. No `docs/runtime/*.md` capture — the witness is a compile-time, author-facing artifact.

### Slice 6.7 — Merge the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers into ONE register (CANON_DESIGN + COMPILER_CODE)

- **Work:** Collapse the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers into ONE register, so the diagnostic surface has one source of truth instead of three trackers drifting apart (the reuse-not-parallel principle, a recorded lesson). The three trackers are: (1) this phase's emit-or-retire dispositions from Slices 6.1–6.2, (2) the conformance program's `[Covers]` coverage tags from Phase 7, and (3) the emission-ownership audit — the exhaustive per-code audit of emitting components introduced under goal (e) at the top of this phase, carried under the "Stage-2" label. The register maps each `DiagnosticCode` to four facts: its emission disposition, its `[StaticallyPreventable]` liveness-or-parked status, its owning stage, and its `[Covers]` conformance status.
  - This is the diagnostic-surface analog of §11's move to consolidate the governance-sweep captures into ONE design section at `evaluator.md §7.6`. Build it as a meta-test/registry that all the consumers read — the `Precept0027` coverage analyzer, the ownership analyzer, the consumer-`*Kind` analyzer, and the Phase-7 conformance `[Covers]` meta-test — rather than three independent gates each maintaining its own list.
- **Exit criteria:** One register/meta-test is the single consumer for emit-or-retire status, `[StaticallyPreventable]` liveness, stage ownership, and `[Covers]` status. The three prior trackers no longer maintain parallel lists. `dotnet test` is green. Everything is verifiable at compile and test time.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md` §DiagnosticCode Registry and §Enforcement — document the ONE register as the single source; cross-link the Phase-7 conformance doc. No runtime capture.

---

### PARKED in Phase 6 (not built; documented with reason — nothing dropped silently)

- **Witness-CHECKING resurrection** — the deleted `ProofChecker`/`ProofWitness` verify-don't-trust architecture. **DO-NOT-RESURRECT.** Slice 6.6's witness *richness* — inspectability, serving Principle 4 — is categorically distinct and is retained as COMPILER_CODE. The re-verification pass stays deleted. The parked-with-reason record: it was re-grounded against the fault-floor outcome — the engine is prove-or-reject, not verify-after-the-fact.
- **F-LANG-SPEC-08 — the ten graph-analyzer modifiers** — deferred to post-runtime Compiler 1.1, the label for the follow-on compiler release planned for after the runtime ships. This is new language surface, so if it is ever taken up it goes through the Pre-Design Owner Consultation gate at Tier-2 and then `/design`. It is carried here as a stub deferral, not as Phase-6 work.

### Phase 6 exit

Every declared `DiagnosticCode` either emits or is retired (Slice 6.1). Every `[StaticallyPreventable]` code is live rather than dead, or is an explicitly-parked-by-D1 gap (Slice 6.2). The 15-item pipeline-tail remainder is closed or carries an explicit disposition (Slice 6.3). The single-stage-ownership analyzer (Slice 6.4, with its GATE-M carve-out count fixed) and the consumer-`*Kind` analyzer (Slice 6.5) both gate `dotnet build`. Rejection messages are inspectable without the deleted checker having been resurrected (Slice 6.6). The three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers have become ONE register (Slice 6.7). All of it is verifiable via `dotnet build`, `dotnet test`, or `precept_compile` with the runtime still stubbed. **No runtime-execution exit criterion; no parked item — overflow, representability, temporal, maxplaces, the number model, or the deleted witness-checker — is re-added; and no admit-now / defer-enforcement obligation is introduced** — this phase admits no new runtime-dependent operation, because it is a diagnostic-surface and analyzer phase.

---

## 10. Phase 7 — Conformance program + API solidity + gate + handoff

**Goal:** This phase closes out the plan. It stands up the compiler-side conformance program as ONE register — meaning the program's coverage census reads the merged diagnostic register rather than starting a new tracker. The program has three parts: three-edge coverage (catalog↔impl, spec↔impl, spec↔catalog), the `[SpecRef]`/`[Covers]` test attributes plus a coverage meta-test that polices them, and the runtime facet authored as a Skip'd v1 acceptance spec — skipped test cases that are themselves the acceptance specification the future runtime build must turn green. Alongside the conformance program, this phase solidifies the public API surface as the explicit runtime precursor: typed field descriptors, the removal of the `RestoreConstraintsFailed` outcome subtype (which can land now, with the runtime still stubbed), and the small parser and type-checker API findings. It burns down the remaining polish residue. It **confirms** — but does not author — that the runtime-canon captures written in Phase 0 actually landed — GATE-I (the fault-delivery contract), OD-1 (the constraint that the runtime evaluator and compile-time constant folding share one expression-evaluation core), Point C (which outcome type owns each external-input value violation), and D9 (the injectable clock seam) among them; Slice 7.6 carries the full confirmation roster. Finally, it redefines the runtime-ready gate, enumerates the runtime-coding handoff (§ 11), and obtains owner sign-off. Everything here is **compiler-completable or canon-design**: every exit criterion is verifiable via `dotnet build` / `dotnet test` / `precept_compile`, or is an assertion that a design capture exists in a named doc — **no exit criterion asserts a runtime-execution outcome**. The runtime facet of the conformance suite is *authored* in this phase, as Skip'd cases that ARE the acceptance spec; turning those cases green is runtime coding and is therefore deferred to § 11.

**Dependencies:** All prior phases must be green before this phase runs, because the conformance suite's census asserts against their outputs: the post-Phase-6 honest registry (the honest registry is Phase 1 Slice 1.4's product — the fault-code registry with its three dishonesties fixed — and "post-Phase-6" names the state that registry reaches once Phase 6 has run over it), the closed obligation positions from Phase 2, the right-sized bands from Phase 1, the closed False-Proved holes from Phase 3, and the diagnostic set after Phase 6's emit-or-retire pass. One ordering inside this dependency set matters specifically: Phase 6's single-register reconciliation (Slice 6.7) — merging the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers (the emit-or-retire dispositions, the conformance suite's coverage tags, and the emission-ownership audit) into the merged diagnostic register — is the direct input to the conformance `[Covers]` census, so that register merge must precede Slice 7.1. Separately, the Phase-0 runtime-canon captures (GATE-I; OD-1; Point C; D9; the standing declines, meaning the proof-power techniques Phase 0 permanently declined; and the Restore dissolution, meaning the settled reading that the Restore operation substantially dissolves — hydrated data is trusted as valid at the time it was persisted, is not re-validated on restore, and is re-governed by the next operation) are the inputs that Slice 7.4 *confirms* — Phase 0 writes them; this phase only verifies they are present and self-consistent. Beyond "register merge (Phase 6) before the conformance census," there is no HARD ordering inside Phase 7.

**Effort:** M (8–12 days). The bound is the conformance-program infrastructure — the test project, its lane, the attributes, the meta-tests, and the harvester (the component, built in Slice 7.1, that enumerates the surfaces the coverage census runs against) — plus the gate and handoff write-up. The API edits are small, and the polish work is a triage burn-down rather than new construction.

---

### Slice 7.1 — Conformance program infrastructure (own-lane allowed-red project + attributes + meta-tests)

- **Work:** Stand up `test/Precept.Conformance.Tests/` as an **own-lane, allowed-red** project. It is added to `Precept.slnx` alongside the existing five test projects, but gated out of the default `dotnet test` green bar, so that authored-but-failing conformance cases — including the Skip'd runtime facet — never block the build. Build the attribute infrastructure the program needs. None of it exists today; this was verified — there are no `[SpecRef]` or `[Covers]` attributes anywhere in the tree. Two attributes are needed: a `[SpecRef("<spec-section>")]` attribute that pins a test to a canonical spec clause, and a `[Covers(...)]` attribute that pins a test to the catalog or diagnostic surface it exercises. Add the coverage **meta-test**: a test that reflects over the suite and asserts the attributes are well-formed — every conformance test carries at least one `[SpecRef]` or `[Covers]`, and every referenced spec section or catalog member actually resolves. Build the **harvester**, the component that enumerates the spec↔impl and spec↔catalog surfaces the census will be run against. Build a **determinism fixture** asserting that the same definition plus the same input always yields the same diagnostic set — this is the compile-time half of the determinism guarantee; the runtime determinism facet is Skip'd. Reuse the existing xUnit + FluentAssertions conventions; do **not** fork a second test framework. **Reuse-not-parallel (verify):** the `[Covers]` census must read the **ONE merged register** produced by Phase 6's merge of the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers (Slice 6.7), and it must not stand up a second coverage tracker of its own.
- **Exit criteria:** `test/Precept.Conformance.Tests/` builds and is in the solution. The project is demonstrably in its own lane: a deliberately-red authored case does not fail the default green bar, which is verifiable by adding a temporary failing or Skip'd fixture and confirming that `dotnet test` of the *other* projects stays green. The `[SpecRef]`/`[Covers]` attributes compile and the coverage meta-test runs. The harvester enumerates the census surface. The compile-time determinism fixture passes. **No exit criterion asserts a runtime outcome** — the runtime facet is authored Skip'd in Slice 7.2.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/tooling/README.md` (or the conformance entry point) — record that the conformance program exists, the own-lane allowed-red convention, the `[SpecRef]`/`[Covers]` attribute contract, and the fence-annotation convention — the rule, carried from the conformance design, that every `` ```precept `` code fence in `docs/language/*.md` carries exactly one annotation declaring its expected compile outcome (`expect=ok`, `expect=PRE####`, `fragment`, or `illustrative`); the harvester reads the annotation, and an un-annotated fence fails a lint test. `CONTRIBUTING.md` — the conformance-authoring contributing note, covering how to add a `[SpecRef]` case and the allowed-red lane rule.

### Slice 7.2 — Three-edge coverage census + runtime facet authored as Skip'd v1 acceptance spec

- **Work:** Author the three-edge coverage per the locked conformance design. That design is carried from the superseded `conformance-suite-plan-2026-06-01.md` as an input, not as standing structure. The three edges are: **catalog↔impl** — every catalog member has an exercising test, or an honest no-coverage row; **spec↔impl** — harvest the canonical spec clauses and census them against shipped behavior; **spec↔catalog** — reconcile the spec's claims against the catalog metadata. Author hand-asserted `PRE####@stage` negative cases: each one asserts that a given malformed precept emits a specific diagnostic at a specific pipeline stage, verified at compile time via `precept_compile`/`Compiler.Compile`. **Author the runtime facet as the Skip'd v1 acceptance spec:** the observable-outcome cases for Fire, Update, InspectFire, InspectUpdate, and Restore; the governance-refusal cases; the reclassified-band-enforcement cases; and the Kleene-prospect inspection cases — cases asserting the three-valued Certain / Possible / Impossible inspection verdict (`Prospect`), where "Kleene" names the three-valued evaluation, which must not throw on an unresolvable subexpression — are all **written as `[Skip]`'d xUnit cases**. The cases ARE the acceptance spec that the deferred runtime build must turn green. **Admit-now / defer-enforcement discipline:** some operations compile as fault-safe only because the future runtime will enforce something. Two such families appear here. The first is the D8 dynamic-write dimension-narrowed conversion: converting a dynamically-written quantity that is commensurable with its target and already narrowed to the same dimension into the target's unit. (D8 is the canonical `business-domain-types.md` decision on commensurable arithmetic with deterministic unit resolution.) The second is the committed-state tier-2 discharge: discharging proofs against values persisted by earlier, separate operations. For each family, the *compiler* facet asserts the **admission** — the operation compiles clean, or the discharge holds — and a Skip'd *runtime* case asserts the deferred **enforcement** — the conversion or governance actually executes and refuses on a real violation. The pair sits visibly in one place, so the deferred runtime build inherits both the compiler admission and the enforcement acceptance case it must turn green. **Sequence the census by area** — BusinessDomainTypes, then Temporal, and so on per the carried area sequencing — so the census is reviewable in coherent batches rather than as one undifferentiated mass.
- **Exit criteria:** the three-edge census exists and runs. Every compile-time negative `PRE####@stage` case passes — meaning the diagnostic fires at the asserted stage — or carries an honest allowed-red row. The runtime facet is fully **authored as Skip'd**: the count of Skip'd runtime cases is recorded, and xUnit reports them Skipped, never failing the green bar. The admit-now / defer-enforcement families (the D8 dynamic-write conversion and the committed-state discharge) each show a compiler-**admission** case that passes today, paired with a Skip'd runtime-**enforcement** case. **The runtime facet's green-ness is explicitly NOT an exit criterion** — authoring the cases is the deliverable; turning Skip'd cases green is runtime coding and belongs to § 11.
- **Doc-touch:** the conformance entry point — record the three-edge model and the convention that the runtime facet is authored as a Skip'd acceptance spec. Cross-link each Skip'd runtime family to its `evaluator.md §7.1/§7.2` and `result-types.md` capture, so the deferred build inherits both the spec text and the executable acceptance cases. **Runtime-canon capture pointer (the Skip'd cases ARE the spec):** `evaluator.md §7.1/§7.2` (the observable-outcome contract) and `result-types.md` (the outcome-variant catalog) — recorded in § 11 as the conformance-runtime-facet handoff row.

### Slice 7.3 — Conformance program's five carried open decisions (resolve into the design doc, four-leg each)

- **Work:** The carried conformance design has **five open decisions**, verified present in the working-docs triage register. They are: (1) **derived-AC triage cadence** — when and how derived acceptance criteria get triaged into concrete test cases; (2) **existing-7000 relationship** — how the conformance suite relates to the existing `Precept.Tests` corpus ("7000" is the carried conformance design's count of that corpus's test cases): subsume it, cross-reference it, or leave the two parallel; (3) **agent-authoring QC** — what quality gate applies to conformance cases authored by agents; (4) **`[Covers]` exclusion hook** — the mechanism by which a surface is honestly marked out-of-census rather than silently uncovered; (5) **board reconciliation** — "board" is the carried conformance design's name for the prior readiness plan's work board, which that design flagged as needing reconciliation against the suite; the decision is how to reconcile the conformance program's diagnostic-coverage discovery role against the Stage-2 exhaustive audit ("Stage-2" is the carried label for the exhaustive emission-ownership audit — the third of the three diagnostic-completeness trackers Phase 6 merged) so that both feed ONE register rather than two parallel trackers; feeding ONE register is the reuse-not-parallel guarantee. Resolve each as a **CANON_DESIGN** decision carrying the full four-leg rationale: Rationale, Alternatives rejected, Precedent, and Tradeoff accepted. All five are conformance-program-internal policy — Tier-1 in the owner-consultation sense, so no language-surface gate applies — and they are resolved in the conformance design doc, not inline in chat.
- **Exit criteria:** each of the five open decisions has a recorded resolution carrying all four rationale legs. Decision (5), board reconciliation, explicitly states that the conformance census reads the ONE merged register from Phase 6, not a second tracker — this closes the loop, on the conformance edge, of Phase 6's merge of the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers.
- **Doc-touch:** the conformance design/entry doc (the five resolved decisions); cross-reference Phase 6's merged-register doc so the single-register guarantee is visible from the conformance side as well.

### Slice 7.4 — API solidity: typed field descriptors + the landable API-surface edits (the runtime precursor)

- **Work:** Solidify the public API surface that the eventual evaluator will build against. This is the explicit **runtime-phase precursor** — "the runtime builds against the final API shape" — and it is in scope for a compiler-only plan because it is API-surface *design and type/signature* work, not runtime *execution*.
  - **F-API-01 typed field descriptors** (F-API-01 is the carried API finding asking for a typed descriptor surface): solidify the descriptor surface — `FieldDescriptor` and its sibling types. **Verify reuse, do NOT fork** — `descriptor-types.md` records the shapes as *Implemented* (`FieldDescriptor`/`StateDescriptor`/`EventDescriptor`/`ArgDescriptor`/`FaultSiteDescriptor` in `Descriptors.cs`; `ConstraintDescriptor` in `SharedTypes.cs`) with **builder population pending**. This slice solidifies the *type and signature surface* — the descriptor shapes the API exposes via `Precept.Fields`/`Precept.States`/etc. It does not do the builder population, which feeds the deferred evaluator, and it does not write any executable body. Confirm against `Descriptors.cs` that this surface is the single source — no parallel descriptor definition may exist.
  - **`RestoreConstraintsFailed` DU removal (landable NOW — COMPILER_CODE, not runtime coding):** remove the `RestoreConstraintsFailed` discriminated-union subtype. It lives at `RestoreOutcome.cs:18` and is consumed in the `Restore` stub at `Precept.cs:121` and in the `runtime-api.md` example. Verified live: it is a real API-surface DU subtype. It is **superseded by the trusted-hydration decision** — spec §0.7 establishes that Restore does not re-validate, so a constraint-failure outcome on restore no longer applies; the supersession is documented at `runtime-api.md:270`, which says the subtype "should be removed when the Restore API is finalized". This is a pure **API-surface type removal** — it does not require the runtime to execute anything, since the `Restore` body stays a stub — so it lands here, NOT at the deferred "Restore-API finalization." Keep `RestoreOutcome` carrying the parse and state-resolution failures: the `RestoreInvalidInput` and `Restored` variants remain.
  - **F-PAR-04 / F-TC-02 / F-TC-03:** the small carried parser and type-checker API findings. F-PAR-04 asks that the parser's defensive enum-exhaustion throws — switch statements over catalog-derived enums that throw on an unhandled member — be covered by the build-time catalog-exhaustiveness guarantee instead of runtime guards. F-TC-02 covers the three type-checker sites where the D26 invariant is asserted by runtime throws rather than made unbreakable by construction. F-TC-03 covers the three type-checker sites that null-check `SecondaryExpression` at the use site with throws, where a discriminated-union split per action shape would make the field structurally non-nullable. Apply each; all are compiler-side and Tier-1 against locked spec. Disposition each one explicitly: apply, re-verify-then-apply, or re-scope with a recorded reason.
- **Exit criteria:** `dotnet build` is green with `RestoreConstraintsFailed` removed and its consumers updated — the `runtime-api.md` example no longer references it, and `Precept.cs`/`RestoreOutcome.cs` compile. The typed-descriptor surface is solidified and confirmed single-source against `Descriptors.cs`, with no forked descriptor type. F-PAR-04, F-TC-02, and F-TC-03 are each applied or dispositioned, and `dotnet test` is green. **No exit criterion asserts a runtime outcome** — `Restore`/`Fire`/`Update` remain stubs; this is API-shape work only.
- **Doc-touch:** `docs/runtime/descriptor-types.md` — confirm and solidify the descriptor shapes as the API source, and note that builder population stays pending and is deferred. `docs/runtime/runtime-api.md` — remove the `RestoreConstraintsFailed` reference at `:261`/`:270` and the superseded design note, and record the trusted-hydration Restore outcome shape: `Restored` / `RestoreInvalidInput` only. **Runtime-canon capture pointer (deferred tail):** the descriptor *builder population* and the `Restore` body stay RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER — captured at `evaluator.md §7.1` (the Restore dissolution — the settled reading that Restore does not re-validate hydrated data, the next operation re-governing it instead — plus the structural-consistency backstop — the structural check that remains Restore's job after the dissolution: Restore still refuses hydrated data whose structure does not match the definition, even though it no longer re-validates constraint rules against that data) and `descriptor-types.md` (the builder-population pass), both already enumerated in § 11.

The review-skill dev-tooling backlog is dev-tooling, not compiler-readiness. Each is recorded as parked-with-reason, not dropped.

- **Work:** **Confirm** — do not author; Phase 0 authored these — that the between-floor-and-runtime design captures — the design questions sitting above the compile-time fault floor but short of runtime coding, which Phase 0 answered as written doc captures rather than as code — landed in the runtime canon and are self-consistent, so the deferred runtime build inherits a complete, contradiction-free spec:

The specific verified contradiction: `result-types.md:51` says a fault "throws `FaultException`", while `:104/:117/:127` and `evaluator.md §7.6` say the fault arrives as `EventOutcome.Faulted`. This MUST be specified before the runtime build starts.

Confirm the requirement is recorded in `evaluator.md` and `runtime-api.md`, so that `now()` stays deterministic.

A confirmation row per item records "present + consistent" together with the doc:section pointer. These are **doc-capture assertions**, not runtime-execution outcomes.

- **Work:** Finalize the **Definition of Done** as a gate that is compile-time plus design-capture-complete (§ 12), and produce the runtime-coding **handoff** (§ 11) as the explicit backlog for the separate future initiative:

Re-derive the runtime-ready exit checklist so it carries **no runtime-execution acceptance** — criteria like "governance sweep enforces bounds", "Fire returns X", and "committed-state discharge sound" are all explicitly OUT of the gate and live in § 11 instead.
  - **Enumerate the runtime-coding handoff (§ 11):** confirm that every RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER item carries its named `docs/runtime/*.md` capture pointer. The items are: the governance/ingress sweep, consolidated into ONE `evaluator.md §7.6` design; value-application — the runtime's actual computation of the values an operation writes, the enforcement half of work the compiler only admits — and the runtime operation bodies, Fire, Update, InspectFire, InspectUpdate, and Restore (five named operations, four-to-five in effect because Restore substantially dissolves under the trusted-hydration reading); band governance enforcement; the committed-state tier-2 discharge; the cross-unit reduction rule — the `evaluator.md` section, not yet written, that specifies how the runtime computes a value when a price's unit differs from the quantity's unit (for example, 4.00 USD per foot times 36 inches yields 12.00); the D8 dynamic-write conversion; element-ingress governance; the mincount Create-time refusal (`evaluator.md §7.1 Create`); and the conformance runtime facet. **Consolidation discipline:** verify that the captures targeting §7.6 read as ONE coherent constraint-plan/sweep design, not as 8-plus uncoordinated edits. **A defer with no capture pointer is a silent loss and fails the gate.**
  - **Confirm-out / parked at the gate:** four items are confirmed out of scope or parked, each with its reason recorded. GATE-N, the definition-evolution deploy lane, is OUT — it is its own future decision. The niche/flag layer is post-runtime — it needs the shipped runtime as its error-promotion oracle. The future-candidate proof-power list — ten candidate proof-power techniques, ordered "Karr-first," meaning Karr's linear-equalities analysis is the first candidate — and the expressiveness lists — five expressiveness candidates, maintained aggregates first — are owner-gated Tier-2 stubs, carried as stubs rather than as work. F-LANG-SPEC-08's ten graph modifiers (the carried language-spec finding proposing ten graph-shaped modifiers) are post-runtime, targeted at Compiler 1.1 — the label for the post-runtime follow-on compiler release.
  - **Owner sign-off** on the gate.
- **Exit criteria:** the runtime-ready gate is redefined with zero runtime-execution acceptance criteria. Every § 11 RUNTIME_CODE_DEFER item has a verified capture pointer, with no orphaned defer. The §7.6 captures are confirmed consolidated into ONE design. Each confirm-out or parked item carries its reason. Owner sign-off is recorded. The whole gate is **compile-time + design-capture verifiable** — `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` green, every capture present, and the handoff enumerated.
- **Doc-touch:** the plan's § 12 Definition of Done and § 11 Runtime-Coding Handoff, both finalized; `docs/compiler-and-runtime-design.md` — the runtime-ready gate definition plus the pipeline→runtime handoff boundary; and cross-links from each named `docs/runtime/*.md` back to § 11's capture pointers, so the deferred initiative's spec is reachable from canon.

---

**Phase 7 exit:** the conformance program stands up as ONE register — three-edge coverage green for the compile-time facet, and the runtime facet authored Skip'd as the acceptance spec, never asserted green here. The API surface is solidified as the runtime precursor: the typed descriptors are single-source, `RestoreConstraintsFailed` is removed, and the small parser and type-checker findings are dispositioned. The polish is burned down — each carrier dispositioned, F-CAT-01 re-verified against HEAD, and the `/audit` in-gate-or-separable question surfaced to the owner. The Phase-0 runtime-canon captures are confirmed present and consistent (GATE-I, OD-1, Point C, D9, and the standing declines). The runtime-ready gate is redefined with no runtime-execution acceptance. The runtime-coding handoff is enumerated with every capture pointer verified — no orphaned defer. Owner sign-off is obtained. **No exit criterion in this phase asserts a runtime-execution outcome; admit-now / defer-enforcement holds — every admitted fault-safe-but-runtime-dependent operation has its Skip'd runtime-enforcement case authored, which is the deferred enforcement, not a flipped compiler rejection.**

---

## 11. Runtime-Coding Handoff (the deferred initiative)

Every item in this section is runtime code. For each one, the correctness argument, the soundness argument, or the exit criterion requires the stubbed evaluator or the future governance sweep to actually execute. None of it is compiler-pure, so all of it sits outside this plan's build scope and moves to a separate runtime initiative. What stays in scope here is each item's capture pointer — the named `docs/runtime/*.md` (or `docs/language/*.md`) section that must hold the item's design, so the eventual runtime build inherits a complete acceptance spec.

**Verified ground truth — the single reason every item below defers.** In `src/Precept/Runtime/Evaluator.cs`, the five operations `Fire` (`:80`), `Update` (`:99`), `InspectFire` (`:121`), `InspectUpdate` (`:132`), and `Restore` (`:155`) are all `throw new NotImplementedException()`, and each is tagged *"once the executable model is designed."* There is no constraint/governance sweep. There is no ingress lane. The executable model is not designed. The sorting rule follows directly: any item whose exit criterion asserts a runtime-execution outcome lives in this section, not in a phase.

### Handoff disciplines (apply to every row)

1. **Mandatory capture pointer.** Every row names the `docs/runtime/*.md` section that must hold its design. A defer with no pointer is a silent loss and is disallowed (this is §0 boundary discipline 2). The capture itself — writing the design into the runtime canon — is CANON_DESIGN work and is in scope for this plan. Only the coding defers.

2. **Admit-now / defer-enforcement (§0 boundary discipline 3).** Some operations are fault-safe once the runtime performs its part: the dynamic-write conversion locked in canonical decision D8, element-ingress governance, committed-state discharge, and the reclassified bands. For those, the compiler admits the operation now, and the §11 row captures the deferred enforcement half — the same treatment the reclassified bands already receive under D2. The compiler does not keep rejecting merely because the runtime is unbuilt. This is distinct from genuine prove-or-reject: a fault the compiler cannot prove safe — for example an unprovable field-reference bound, or a faulting guard the obligation walk never visited — is permanent compiler behavior, named at its owning slice, and is not a §11 handoff matter. Each row below names its runtime half explicitly. What keeps the defer honest is the capture pointer of discipline 1, not a compiler rejection held in place as a placeholder.

3. **No runtime-execution exit criterion leaks back into this plan.** The rows for reclassified bands exit at "the runtime refuses the write" — and that exit belongs to the runtime initiative, never to this plan. This plan's band slices (Phase 1) exit at "compiles clean" or "emits the proven-violation warning."

4. **Consolidation directive.** This is the runtime analog of Phase 6's directive to merge the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers — the emit-or-retire dispositions, the conformance suite's coverage tags, and the emission-ownership audit — into one register. The governance-sweep capture obligations in §11.A all target `evaluator.md §7.6` (`evaluator.md:1326`, *Constraint Evaluation* — the section heading already exists). They MUST be authored as one coherent constraint-plan / sweep design pass, not as 8+ uncoordinated edits. The full consolidated obligation list, grouped by target doc, lives in the companion `compiler-readiness-plan-2026-06-16-design-revision-notes.md` § B; §11 here is the enumerated, capture-pointered handoff face of the same content.

---

### 11.A — The governance / ingress sweep (author as ONE consolidated `evaluator.md §7.6` design pass)

These ten obligations are the single largest deferred surface, and the one with the strictest consolidation requirement. They describe one mechanism — a constraint plan built at compile time, then loaded and fired by the runtime at every commit and every ingress lane — not ten independent features. The §7.6 design pass must produce one constraint-plan / sweep model from which all ten fall out as facets.

| Deferred runtime-code item | One-line scope | Capture pointer |
|---|---|---|
| **Governance / ingress constraint sweep (umbrella)** | Enforce every declared value-level constraint at Fire, Update, and Create. This is Part 2 of the guarantee contract — spec §0.7, *The Compile-Time and Runtime Guarantee Contract*, which delivers Precept's guarantee through two distinct mechanisms: Part 1 is fault prevention, established entirely at compile time, and Part 2 is governance, the runtime enforcement of every declared constraint on every value entering the entity from outside the definition. This governance half is the executable sweep that does not exist today. | **`evaluator.md §7.6` (CONSOLIDATED, `:1326`)** — constraint-plan construction, per-commit/ingress enforcement, and atomic working-copy discard; the `ConstraintsFailed` shape in `result-types.md`; the ingress surface in `runtime-api.md`; spec §0.7 (the target is already recorded there). |
| **DesugarsToRule sweep-firing** | Fire the `TypedRule`/`TypedEnsure` constraint objects that Phase 1 Slice 1.2 synthesizes from modifier/bound metadata, at every commit and ingress lane. The synthesis is compiler work; the firing needs the sweep. | `evaluator.md §7.6` constraint-plan load and enforcement; `§5` dual-lane ingress — the two lanes (JSON and CLR typed-builder) by which arguments enter the evaluator; cross-link `proof-engine.md` (the DesugarsToRule consumer/synthesis seam) and `compiler-and-runtime-design.md` (the pipeline handoff). |
| **Reclassified-band governance enforcement** | When a write goes out of band on a non-proof-carrying field, the runtime issues a recoverable governance refusal — **NOT** `EventOutcome.Faulted`, because after D2 bands are no longer faults for these fields. | `evaluator.md §7.6` (the recoverable in-domain refusal lane, kept distinct from the fault lane); `fault-system.md` (OutOfRange/LengthBound/CountBound omitted from the impossible-path table for these fields); `result-types.md` (a recoverable-refusal outcome shape distinct from `Faulted`). **In-scope compiler counterpart — this row is the canonical model of admit-now / defer-enforcement:** Phase 1 Slices 1.3–1.4 reclassify the compiler side, so an out-of-band write on a non-proof-carrying field compiles clean; the runtime's recoverable refusal is the deferred enforcement half. The compiler is not withholding a rejection while it waits for the runtime. |
| **Committed-state tier-2 discharge enforcement** | Any constraint that a proof discharges against committed state — state persisted by earlier, cross-event or cross-transaction operations — must have been enforced at **every prior commit**. That prior-commit enforcement is the premise that makes committed-state discharge sound. | `evaluator.md §7.6 + §10` (the committed-state invariant); `proof-engine.md` (the runtime precondition of the carries-proof contract). **In-scope compiler counterpart:** Phase 3 Slice 3.1 ships write-aware discharge for the same-transaction (intra-chain) case, which is genuine prove-or-reject. The committed-state discharge, by contrast, is ADMITTED, relying on this captured prior-commit enforcement obligation — defer-enforcement, the same posture as bands, sound once the runtime holds the invariant a fully-built runtime would hold. No compiler over-rejection is held in place pending the runtime, and pre-release this admission creates no live fault (D3). |
| **Collect-all sweep + ingress / post-mutation ordering** | Gather all constraint violations without short-circuiting on the first one (spec §3A.1:1899 collect-all; spec §3A.4:1965 mutation atomicity; canonical Decision 7, "collect all, don't short-circuit"). Verifying this requires Fire/Update execution. | `evaluator.md §7.6 + §7.1/§7.2` (collect-all-violations semantics; the ordering of ingress checks versus post-mutation checks); the `ConstraintsFailed` shape in `result-types.md`; align spec §3A.1:1899/§3A.4:1965. |
| **Element-ingress governance (Rule 2 / AC 7)** | Govern every element entering a collection — via add, enqueue, push, insert, put, append, or a default literal — against the collection's inner-type bound; an out-of-bound element discards the working copy. The "Rule 2 / AC 7" numbering comes from the collection inner-type element-bound design: its semantic Rule 2 is element governance at ingress, and its acceptance criterion 7 (AC 7) is that design's runtime-governance criterion. | `evaluator.md §7.6` element-ingress contract, plus the runtime-canon note recording that the compiler accepts collection-element reads as safe today only because the future runtime will check every value entering a collection; the note keeps the two halves explicitly paired. The shipped read-site half is the same design's semantic Rule 4, the read-site reach: a value read out of a bounded collection (via `.peek`, `.first`, and the other element accessors) is treated as carrying the collection's declared inner-type bound, so a proof at the read site discharges from that bound. The element-ingress enforcement is the deferred half — admit-now / defer-enforcement, not a compiler over-rejection held in place. |
| **2c-ii field-reference-bound ingress enforcement** | Refuse a commit that violates a proof-carrying field-reference bound on a running entity. This is the deferred runtime tail of 2c-ii — the second half of the locked relational-rules-and-bounds design, in which field-referencing bound modifiers participate in proof (Phase 5's prove-or-reject work). | `evaluator.md §7.6 + §7.1` (the modifier-to-`TypedRule` constraint-plan entry; the spec §0.7 governance refusal, which the spec terms *operation-blind* because it upholds the field's declared contract whether or not any fault-prone operation references the field; Length/Count/Interval trap behavior); `proof-engine.md` DesugarsToRule consumer contract. |
| **Cross-transition ensure enforcement** | Evaluate to/in/from ensures against the post-transition working copy at fire time, and refuse on failure. | `evaluator.md §7.6/§10` (post-transition working-copy evaluation flowing into `ConstraintsFailed`). **Surfaced CANON_DESIGN fork — this is not a clean runtime punt:** record in `proof-engine.md` that cross-transition ensure proof is NOT a compile-time obligation under the current spec §0.6, AND flag the *provably-unsatisfiable* cross-transition ensure as a candidate compile-time definition-incoherence detection. That is a §0.6 obligation decision to surface to the owner, not something to defer silently. |
| **omit-clears-on-entry (rule 5) + readonly/editable patch enforcement** | Enforce omit-clears-on-entry — rule 5 of the language spec's access-mode semantics, under which an `omit` field's value resets to default on any transition into the omitting state — plus the readonly/editable patch outcome (spec §3A.2 outcome 8) and the Create verdict space (Created/Rejected). All three require access-mode handling plus Create execution. | `evaluator.md §7.5` (access modes) + `§7.1` (the Create verdict space). |
| **Modifier-desugar because-rationale in fault messages** | Surface the generated because-rationale text of spec §2.4 in the runtime refusal message. The rationale's only consumer is the runtime fault message. | `fault-system.md` / `evaluator.md §9` (a modifier-sourced refusal carries the §2.4 generated rationale). |

### 11.E — The consolidated `evaluator.md §7.6` governance-sweep design pass (single deliverable)

The §11.A obligations are not nine edits; they are **one design**. Before the runtime initiative begins, `evaluator.md §7.6` (`:1326`, *Constraint Evaluation*) must be written as a single coherent constraint-plan / sweep model, in which all of the following are facets of one mechanism:

1. **Constraint-plan construction** — the `TypedRule`/`TypedEnsure` objects synthesized at compile time (Phase 1 Slice 1.2) are loaded into a per-precept constraint plan, keyed by field and operation.
2. **Per-commit/ingress enforcement** — the plan is evaluated against the working copy at every commit and every ingress lane; the umbrella value-constraint sweep of spec §0.7 IS this evaluation.
3. **Recoverable governance-refusal lane** — reclassified bands refuse *recoverably*, NOT via `Faulted`; the refusal outcome shape is distinct from the fault outcome.
4. **Committed-state tier-2 invariant** — every prior commit enforced its discharging constraint. That invariant is what makes the compiler's committed-state discharge sound: the compiler admits the discharge now, relying on this captured enforcement, and the enforcement is the deferred half.
5. **Collect-all** — no short-circuiting; all violations are gathered (spec §3A.1 / canonical Decision 7).
6. **Element-ingress governance** — Rule 2 / AC 7 (the element-governance-at-ingress rule and runtime-governance acceptance criterion of the collection inner-type element-bound design, per the §11.A row), the deferred-enforcement partner of the shipped (admitted) Rule-4 read-site reach — the same design's semantic Rule 4, under which a value read out of a bounded collection is treated as carrying the collection's declared inner-type bound.
7. **2c-ii field-reference-bound ingress** — the synthetic `TypedRule`s enforced at ingress; each is span-tagged, meaning Phase 5's desugar stamps the synthetic rule with the source span of the field declaration whose bound modifier it lowers.
8. **omit-clears-on-entry + readonly/editable patch enforcement** — integrated with access modes and the Create verdict space.
9. **Cross-transition ensure** — evaluation against the post-transition working copy.

Authoring these as 8+ uncoordinated edits would re-fragment the runtime spec the same way diagnostic completeness was once fragmented across the three separate diagnostic-completeness trackers (the emit-or-retire dispositions, the conformance suite's coverage tags, and the emission-ownership audit). The consolidation requirement here is the runtime-canon analog of merging those three trackers into ONE register. This single §7.6 pass is the load-bearing capture deliverable of the entire handoff.

> **Boundary restated:** every item in §11 exits in the **runtime** initiative, at a runtime-execution outcome. This plan's Definition of Done (§12) accepts §11 as *enumerated-with-capture-pointers*, never as *executed*. No "governance sweep enforces bounds," no "Fire returns X," and no committed-state discharge soundness claim appears anywhere in this plan's exit criteria.

## Coverage ledger — every input register reconciled against the compiler-only plan (one row per finding, no double-counts)

**Purpose.** This ledger is the single accounting surface that proves no input obligation was lost when the prior plan was re-scoped to the compiler/runtime boundary and reshaped by the four owner decisions: D1 (overflow parked — decimal, temporal, and the maxplaces precision proof), D2 (band split), D3 (atomicity relaxed), and D4 (Frank fixes folded). Every item from every input register lands in exactly one of three dispositions:

- **→ Phase N** — the item is in-scope compiler code or canonical design work, and it is placed in a numbered phase of this plan.
- **→ §11 handoff** — the item is runtime coding. It is deferred to the separate runtime initiative, and it carries a named `docs/runtime/*.md` capture pointer. No item may be deferred without a pointer — that is § 0 discipline 2.
- **PARKED (reason)** — the item is a documented known limitation or an owner-deferred decision. It is never built, and it is never claimed prevented or done.

**De-duplication note (Frank / adversarial).** The prior register listed several findings three to four times each, once per parallel pass: the floor-gap view, the scale-back view, the registry view, the spec-audit view, the research-doc view, and the triage view. Here each finding is collapsed to one row, with cross-references where a duplicate view added something. Where a row below names the duplicate countings it is collapsing, it cites them by the prior registers' own row IDs, which follow four schemes. A bare `P3` marks the finding's appearance in the prior plan's Phase-3 build list. `FG-` plus a number is a floor-gap row of the fault-floor-definition register — the same numbering as this ledger's §A rows, so FG-05 names the same gap as §A row 5. `FF` marks the finding's appearance in the fault-floor research document's own re-pass listing (the Findings / Floor / Scale-backs / Registry / Conclusions re-pass of that document). `P4-` plus an area tag and a number is a prior-plan Phase-4 register row in the named datatype area: P4-PRIM-11 is primitives row 11, P4-BIZ-26 is business-domain row 26, and P4-TEMP-11 is temporal row 11. The grounding-research reconciliation is tracked separately, in Phase 0 Slice 0.4 and the design-revision companion — the companion document `compiler-readiness-plan-2026-06-16-design-revision-notes.md`, which holds the concrete fault-floor research-doc edits and the consolidated runtime-canon capture-obligation list; that work edits the source document itself, so it is not a second build of the same item. Some findings genuinely have two halves — a compiler half and a runtime-enforcement half. The D2 bands, DesugarsToRule, 2c-ii, committed-state discharge, and BUG-030 all have this shape. For those, the compiler half is placed in a phase and the enforcement half is handed off to §11 with a pointer. That is the compiler-half-now / enforcement-deferred split, not a double count.

**Provenance (Frank finding B3, applied throughout).** The obligation register is the 43 obligations recovered from the triage of 32 prior working documents — 28 superseded and 4 archived. The obligations transferred out of the 4 archived documents are present in that 43-item register; this was verified in the triage summary. The FaultCode member counts cited below are reconciled against the live source file `src/Precept/Language/FaultCode.cs`, which has **15 members** — so the "13-vs-15" drift in `fault-system.md` resolves to 15 live members. One distinction matters for the registry trim: Phase 1 removes 3 band codes from `[StaticallyPreventable]` status and from the bijective core, but those codes are not deleted from the enum.
