### C. Registry corrections (10) — `FaultCode.cs` / `StaticallyPreventableMap.cs` register

All ten corrections land in Phase 1 Slice 1.4 as ONE pass. That pass is the producer side of the plan's one hard ordering, because the Phase-2 creation-exhaustiveness checker reads whatever the registry holds when it first runs.

1. REMOVE `LengthBoundViolation` and `CountBoundViolation` from `[StaticallyPreventable]` status and from the bijective core. This applies to the Length and Count codes only — per Frank finding A2 (see item 2 for why OutOfRange differs).
2. Strip the `[StaticallyPreventable]` partner off OutOfRange. OutOfRange was never in the bijective core, so for the bijective core this is a no-op.
3. **`NumericOverflow` is kept as a documented known-limitation defense-in-depth trap — it is NOT retargeted** to a representability obligation. That is D1. Its `[StaticallyPreventable]` partner is an honest gap, disclosed rather than papered over.
4. ADD `FaultCode.IndexOutOfBounds`, and route PRE0100 to it — fixing the false "collection was empty" message.
5. ADD `FaultCode.KeyNotFound`, and route PRE0099 — the key-presence diagnostic, which fires when a keyed lookup's key may be absent — to it.
6. ADD the UnexpectedNull row to the bijective map, and route PRE0116 to it — fixing the bug where the presence trap reports a divisor message.
7. Route PRE0101 to `DuplicateKey` **only** if owner gate GATE-D resolves to require-absence semantics. Under put-semantics, the case exits to governance instead — the compile-side exit lands in this pass, and the enforcement goes to §11. Either way, the false collapse into `CollectionEmptyOnMutation` is fixed regardless of how GATE-D resolves.
8. RETIRE the generic `_ => DivisionByZero` backstop in favor of per-family honest routing. This includes fixing the misroute where the pow-exponent ≥0 case falls through to the division-by-zero diagnostic. Land it once, and coordinate with Phase 4.
9. **`TemporalOverflow` is NOT added.** Under D1 it parks together with the rest of overflow.
10. KEEP the 4 type-checker traps — `TypeMismatch`, `UndeclaredField`, `InvalidMemberAccess`, and `FunctionArityMismatch`, the fault codes kept as defense-in-depth backstops behind the type checker's totality, with no proof obligation owed — and add a one-line disposition for evaluation-stack depth — an explicit recorded outcome for that question, one line long, rather than silence. ADD `ProofStrategy.Vacuous` — a proof-ledger honesty fix riding this same registry pass: it replaces the mislabel where vacuously-proved ledger entries were recorded as `Literal // vacuously proved`.

**Guardrail (Frank A3):** honest routing must extend the attribute-derived bijective map. The residual hand-written switch in `CreateFaultSiteLink` must **shrink, not grow** — trading the backstop smell for a switch-on-`*Kind` smell is not acceptable. 10/10 placed.

- The cross-unit reduction rule — the `evaluator.md` section, never yet written, that specifies how the runtime computes a value when a price's unit differs from the quantity's unit (e.g. 4.00 USD per foot times 36 inches yields 12.00). ## Calibration / confidence

Each load-bearing claim this plan rests on carries one of three confidence ratings. **HIGH** means the claim was ground-truthed against code or canon and is cited verbatim. **MEDIUM** means the claim is sound by argument and consistent with the verified facts, but the supporting evidence is partial or a design choice remains owner-gated. **MEDIUM-LOW** means the claim is still UNPROBED: the disposition is recorded as intent, and the evidence-gathering or the owner conversation is still owed before any slice may build on it. A rating is a routing instrument, not a decoration. A MEDIUM-LOW item must not silently harden into MEDIUM or HIGH inside an execution slice — each one carries a named probe or a named gate that must clear first.

### HIGH — ground-truthed, verbatim-cited

- **The runtime is a verified pure stub.** In `src/Precept/Runtime/Evaluator.cs`, all five runtime entry points — `Fire`, `Update`, `InspectFire`, `InspectUpdate`, and `Restore` — each `throw new NotImplementedException()`, and each is tagged *"once the executable model is designed."* There is no governance/constraint sweep, and the executable model is not designed. This is the structural fact the whole compiler/runtime boundary stands on. Because the fact is observable in the source today, the boundary is not a judgement call. **Reason for the rating:** the claim was read directly from the source, with all five entry points confirmed throwing.
- **The runtime-code-free boundary holds for the IN-SCOPE work.** Every retained item is compiler-pure: it can be fully verified via `precept_compile`, `dotnet build`, or `dotnet test` while the runtime stays stubbed. As concrete evidence, the two fail-open-position bugs BUG-031 and BUG-032 were both confirmed via `precept_compile` against the stub. Nothing in scope dereferences `Fire`, `Update`, or `Restore`. **Reason for the rating:** the boundary is defined as "verifiable while the evaluator throws," and the verified-stub fact above makes that line objective rather than aspirational.
- **The band reclassification is compiler-pure.** The reclassification takes three band checks — the PRE0078 band check `IntervalContainment`, the PRE0135 check `LengthContainment`, and the PRE0136 check `CountContainment` — and moves them from fault-floor prove-or-reject treatment to a proven-violation warning. That change alters only what the *compiler* emits; it requires no runtime to execute. The compile-time exit criterion — "an out-of-band write on a non-proof-carrying field compiles clean; a provable violation emits the warning" — is verifiable via `precept_compile`. **Reason for the rating:** the change is an edit to diagnostic severity plus registry membership. The enforcement half — runtime governance refusing the out-of-band write — is the part that needs the runtime, and that half is explicitly the deferred enforcement partner recorded in the Runtime-Coding Handoff (§ handoff); it is not part of this claim.
- **The OutOfRange bijective-removal asymmetry (Frank finding A2).** Frank is the adversarial review of the prior plan whose accepted findings owner decision D4 folds in; in the review's numbering, A-numbered findings were advisory (non-blocking) and B-numbered findings were blocking. The `DiagnosticCode` for `OutOfRange` was **never** in the bijective core. The frozen set at `StaticallyPreventableMap.cs:28-37` lists exactly `DivisionByZero`, `SqrtOfNegative`, `UnguardedCollectionAccess`, `UnguardedCollectionMutation`, `NumericOverflow`, `LengthBoundViolation`, and `CountBoundViolation` — and no `OutOfRange`. Therefore the instruction "remove OutOfRange from the bijective core" is a **no-op**; the bijective removal is real only for the Length and Count entries. **Reason for the rating:** the frozen set was read verbatim, and the executor must not hunt a nonexistent OutOfRange row.
- **`NumericOverflow` is still in the bijective core today.** Confirmed at `StaticallyPreventableMap.cs:34`. This is why the registry consequence of owner decision D1 — the decision that parks decimal and temporal overflow as a documented known fault — is "keep the code, demote its `[StaticallyPreventable]` partner to an honest documented gap" rather than "delete it." The code is live and consulted. **Reason for the rating:** read verbatim from the same frozen set.
- **The one hard ordering that binds scheduling is registry-honest-before-checker.** The fault-code registry must become the honest registry before the Phase-2 position-totality checker — the creation-exhaustiveness checker, whose own slice is Slice 2.3 — first asserts against it, because the checker asserts against the fault codes as Slice 1.4 leaves them, not the pre-fix set. Making the registry honest requires two slices: the band reclassification (Slice 1.3) and the registry fix itself (Slice 1.4, the slice that produces the honest registry). The plan names three other must-precede relationships. First, Slice 0.4 — the Phase-0 slice that reconciles the stale grounding-research doc `research/architecture/compiler/fault-floor-definition-2026-06-11.md` — must land before any Phase-1-or-later work. Second, the Phase-6 register merge, which collapses the three diagnostic-completeness trackers into the one merged diagnostic register, must land before the Phase-7 conformance census — the conformance suite's coverage census that reads that merged register. Third, the honest registry must land before the Phase-6 `[StaticallyPreventable]` liveness sweep — Slice 6.2, the check that every fault code marked `[StaticallyPreventable]` has a live, actually-firing compile-time diagnostic behind it. Each of those is satisfied by the phase order as written. Everything else sequences incrementally with no atomic-merge coupling, per owner decision D3 (atomicity relaxed). **Reason for the rating:** the dependency is a literal data dependency — the checker consumes the registry's codes — not a deployment-window concern. And with the runtime stubbed, there is no live enforcement window that any reordering could expose.

### MEDIUM — sound by argument, evidence partial or choice still owner-gated

- **The registry adjudication (Slice 1.4) lands honestly in one pass.** This is the same Slice 1.4 named in the hard ordering above — the slice that produces the honest registry. The slice's shape is: REMOVE the Length and Count entries from `[StaticallyPreventable]` and from the bijective core; keep `NumericOverflow` as a documented known-fault defense-in-depth trap; ADD `IndexOutOfBounds`, `KeyNotFound`, and the `UnexpectedNull` bijective row; RETIRE the generic `_ => DivisionByZero` backstop in favor of per-family routing; and ADD `ProofStrategy.Vacuous`. That shape is grounded in verified call sites: the backstop at `ProofEngine.Diagnostics.cs:488-491`, the Vacuous mislabel at `ProofEngine.cs:1181/1194`, and the UnexpectedNull exclusion at `StaticallyPreventableMap.cs:22-27`. **The rating is MEDIUM rather than HIGH because** several routings depend on Phase-0 gate answers not yet recorded. Routing PRE0101 — the diagnostic for adding an element whose key is already present in a keyed collection — to `DuplicateKey` is conditional on GATE-D, the owner gate on require-absence semantics. The per-family routing of PRE0157 — the diagnostic that fires when `quantity × quantity` produces a dimension outside the curated business-domain set — needs the owner call on whether it belongs to the TypeMismatch genus or gets a new code. And the guardrail from Frank finding A3 — "`CreateFaultSiteLink` must shrink, not grow" — is a discipline to be verified in the diff, not a fact established yet.
- **The parked-overflow honesty carve-out is a true honesty exception, not a relocation.** Owner decision D1 parks decimal-representability overflow, together with its temporal twin (the same representability genus), as an accepted, documented, never-claimed-prevented known fault. This is distinct from the band relocation of owner decision D2, as Frank finding A6 established: bands move their enforcement to future runtime governance, so prevention is relocated rather than removed, whereas overflow is genuinely relaxed. The consequence is correctly captured: the claim in `evaluator.md §10` that "no diagnostics ⇒ no evaluator faults" is now **false for the parked family** and must be amended to except it. **The rating is MEDIUM rather than HIGH because** the canon edit that makes the carve-out honest is **owner-gated**. That edit means softening the absolute-overflow claims in Principle 11 (in §0.1 Design Principles) and §0.7 — both sections of `docs/language/precept-language-spec.md` — plus the `philosophy.md` reconciliation. The edit is surfaced, not applied. And because the core-guarantee category genuinely changed — overflow is now a disclosed limitation — the owner must ratify the philosophy reconciliation before this claim can be HIGH.
- **Admit-now / defer-enforcement keeps the compiler honest while halves defer.** Where an operation is fault-safe once the runtime performs its part — the dimension-narrowed conversion on dynamic writes (decision D8 in `docs/language/business-domain-types.md`); tier-2 discharge against committed-state values across events; governance of the reclassified bands — the compiler **admits** the operation now, and the runtime **enforcement** job is captured in §11, the Runtime-Coding Handoff. The compiler does not keep rejecting merely because the runtime is unbuilt. Genuine fault-unprovability is a separate matter: that stays permanent prove-or-reject, never a runtime-absence hold. **The rating is MEDIUM because** correctness rests on each deferral actually keeping its named `docs/runtime/*.md` capture pointer — no silent loss — and the prevention guarantee re-engages only when the runtime build turns the captured §11 obligations green. Pre-release, the transient enforcement gap creates no live fault, per owner decision D3 and the stubbed `Evaluator.cs`.
- **The DesugarsToRule synthesis (Slice 1.2) is compiler-pure only if a real consumer reads it.** Verified fact: today the synthesized artifact has **zero constraint-synthesis or enforcement consumers; 2 cosmetic/display consumers exist** — the grammar generator at `Program.cs:173` and the MCP formatter at `CatalogFormatters.cs:253+`. This count is Frank finding B2 — in the review's numbering, a blocking finding. The unconsumed-artifact guard is correctly flagged: if nothing in-scope reads the synthesized `TypedRule`, then synthesizing it produces dead model state, and the synthesis must be co-deferred with the runtime governance sweep. **The rating is MEDIUM because** one determination is still owed at slice start: whether the Phase-5 discharge work for 2c-ii — the half of the locked relational-rules-and-bounds design that makes field-referencing bound modifiers participate in proof — actually consumes the synthesized `TypedRule`, or instead discharges purely through relational narrowing. The claim "this is compiler-pure" is conditional on that answer.
- **Position-totality + the creation-exhaustiveness checker are fully compiler-side.** The catalog `ChildExpressionPositions` projection, the closures of the 10 fail-open positions, and the 3-layer build-time checker all gate `dotnet build` with the runtime stubbed. **The rating is MEDIUM rather than HIGH for two reasons.** First, the intra-guard short-circuit narrowing must land in the *same* slice, and its adequacy is not yet demonstrated. That narrowing is the piece that prevents a mass false-reject of the canonical optional-field idiom, the guard form `when X is set and X > 5`. In that form the newly-walked right conjunct raises a presence obligation, and the left conjunct's presence test must discharge it. The narrowing is therefore a minimum level of discharge power — and its sufficiency is asserted by argument, not yet demonstrated against the corpus. Second, BUG-031 carries an owner fork — walk guards, versus restricting the guard shape in the spec — that is still owed as a Tier-2/3 owner consultation.

### MEDIUM-LOW — still UNPROBED; evidence-gathering or owner conversation owed before a slice builds on it

- **Temporal-arithmetic overflow probe is evidence-gathering, not a build.** Phase 0 Slice 0.1(a) runs date and datetime values, plus or minus an out-of-range period, through NodaTime in order to **populate the known-limitation doc**. The obligation itself is PARKED alongside decimal overflow under owner decision D1, so this probe is an input to the CANON honesty note, not gated build work. **The rating is MEDIUM-LOW because** the factual NodaTime behavior has not yet been observed within this plan. The disposition — "park, document as known limitation" — is the recorded intent, and the verbatim probe output is still owed before the honesty doc can state what actually happens.
- **The `omit × global-rule` join disposition is unadjudicated.** Phase 0 Slice 0.1(b) must classify this join one of two ways. It is either floor-shaped or flag-shaped. Floor-shaped means the join sits in the presence lane of obligation creation — the presence lane being the family of obligations the obligation-creation walk generates wherever a possibly-absent field is read in value position — and therefore belongs in the Phase-2 position walk. Flag-shaped means the join is a hook-guard coverage concern for PRE0082 — `UnsatisfiableGuard`, the proof engine's satisfiability-scan warning for a guard that can never be true — and therefore belongs in the post-runtime flag layer, out of this plan's scope. **The rating is MEDIUM-LOW because** the coverage of the Phase-2 creation-exhaustiveness checker and the conditional fold in Slice 2.3 — the checker's own slice, which folds the omit-join into the position walk only if this probe classifies it floor-shaped — both depend on this answer, and any reclassification must be surfaced explicitly rather than moved silently. It is a recorded open probe, not a settled fact.
- **PRE0101 require-absence-vs-put semantics (GATE-D).** GATE-D is the Phase-0 owner gate on require-absence semantics for PRE0101, the duplicate-key diagnostic introduced in the Slice-1.4 item above. The fix for the false `CollectionEmptyOnMutation` collapse lands regardless: today the duplicate-key path mis-attaches the empty-collection fault code, so a duplicate-key site reports the message "Dequeue/pop called on empty collection" — that mis-attachment is the collapse, and its fix is classified COMPILER_CODE. But whether PRE0101 routes to `DuplicateKey`, staying in the fault lane, or routes to a uniqueness rule, exiting to governance with enforcement deferred, is owner-gated. **The rating is MEDIUM-LOW because** the routing in Slice 1.4 is conditional on an owner answer not yet recorded.
- **GATE-G min-bound vacuity disposition.** GATE-G is the Phase-0 owner gate on how min-bound vacuity resolves. The vacuity is this: today a declared minimum bound's discharge is recorded as vacuously `Proved`, because the proof engine cannot resolve a `DeclarationValue` satisfaction — a proof fact whose value comes from the field's own declaration — so the discharge succeeds without the fact ever being checked. It resolves one of two ways: (i) make `DeclarationValue` satisfactions resolvable in the proof engine, so a min-bound discharge stops being vacuous — classified COMPILER_CODE and landing in Phase 3; or (ii) narrow the spec instead — amend the discharge-tier sentence in `precept-language-spec.md` §0.7 — classified CANON_DESIGN and folding into Phase 1 Slice 1.5, the Phase-1 slice that applies the owner-approved spec/canon amendments and the overflow honesty doc. With overflow parked under D1, option (i)'s representability motivation evaporates, which makes option (ii) the more likely default — but the choice is **not yet made**. **The rating is MEDIUM-LOW because** even which phase contains the work is contingent on the owner call. This is a single register entry with a conditional COMPILER_CODE tail, deliberately not pre-resolved.
- **GATE-I fault-delivery contract reconciliation.** GATE-I is the owner gate on a verified contradiction *inside* the runtime canon. At `result-types.md:51/63/72` the canon says faults throw `FaultException`; at `:104/117/127`, together with `evaluator.md §7.6`, the canon defines `EventOutcome.Faulted(Fault)` as a structured variant; and `fault-system.md` lists this as open question Q1. **The rating is MEDIUM-LOW because** the contradiction is verified but the resolution is owner-facing — it sits adjacent to the guarantee surface — and it is not yet picked. The plan must not unilaterally select the shape. Until the owner picks, the downstream runtime-canon capture cannot be HIGH.
- **GATE-J / GATE-K canon contradictions (Phase 4 tail).** GATE-J is the owner gate on two internal-canon contradictions. The first is the sqrt contradiction between §3.2 and §3.7 of `precept-language-spec.md`: §3.7 says integer input to sqrt is a type error, while §3.2's integer-to-number widening implies acceptance — and the implementation accepts. The second is the choice field-vs-field contradiction: the canon contradicts itself over whether a choice field may be compared against another field, and today the comparison compiles clean — a false-clean the plan treats as a soundness-grade compiler bug to be fixed once the reading is set. GATE-K is the owner gate on the log-unit dB/Np/B cross-unit policy, which is Tier-2 language-surface work requiring an owner conversation before design. **The rating is MEDIUM-LOW because** the contradictions are identified but the canonical reading in each case is an owner pick. An interim safeguard holds in the meantime: the log units are catalogued with `ScaleIsRational=false`, the UCUM-catalog flag marking a unit whose scale is not an exact rational conversion factor, which keeps such units out of cross-unit conversion — so these items are unblocking but unsettled, carried as gates rather than as facts.

### Calibration summary

Ground-truthed against code read verbatim, the **HIGH** claims are: the boundary itself (the verified stub establishes the compiler-pure scope), the claim that the band reclassification is compiler-pure, the registry facts about OutOfRange and NumericOverflow, and the single hard ordering. Sound by argument and consistent with the verified facts, but each carrying an owner-gated choice or an in-diff discipline still owed, the **MEDIUM** claims are: the adjudications that *shape* the registry, the parked-overflow honesty carve-out, the admit-now / defer-enforcement captures, and the compiler-purity of the synthesis and the checker. The genuinely **MEDIUM-LOW** items are the ones the plan is honest about not having resolved: the temporal-overflow probe (evidence-gathering now, not a build), the omit-join classification, and the owner picks behind GATE-D, GATE-G, GATE-I, GATE-J, and GATE-K. Every one of those is recorded as a Phase-0 probe or gate that must clear before any downstream slice may treat it as settled. **No load-bearing claim is rated above its evidence**: where the code was read, the claim is HIGH; where an owner conversation or a probe is still owed, the claim is held at MEDIUM-LOW until that input lands.
